> **Executor note (2026-11-07):** the Codex workspace currently has **no credits**. The user decided that **Claude executes [Codex]-tagged tasks until credits return**; the tags are kept so the intended split is restored afterwards. Review rules do not change: a Claude-executed `[Codex]` task still gets an independent Claude review (a separate reviewer agent, not the implementer). The same lack of credits blocks every Task 0 row that sends a real Codex prompt (Q1-Codex, Q2, Q7, Q10–Q15); free Codex commands (`--help`, `--version`, `login status`, `debug models`, `features list`) still run. Tasks that depend on a blocked row proceed on synthetic fixtures; their acceptance step waits for the row.

**Goal:** Let a story be planned by Claude Code, coded by Codex, reviewed by Claude, tested by Codex and security-reviewed by Claude through the CLIs the user already has signed in, with SHA-bound gates whose lifecycle follows spec §5.7.0, a read-only story chat and visible subagents — without changing legacy OpenCode projects.

**Architecture:** An `AgentRuntime` interface (spec §5.1) with three adapters behind one new streaming spawner (`spawnStreaming()`, spec §5.2 — **not** the check runner) and an allowlisted environment. `WorkflowService` stays the only status authority and the only writer of `gate_runs` rows; QA and Security are ordered gates inside READY_FOR_REVIEW, frozen per candidate SHA (`freeze_seq`), claimed and resolved in fenced transactions. Chat is always a separate read-only Q&A session in its own `<key>.chat` worktree, in a capped light slot.

**Tech Stack:** TypeScript (Node ≥ 24.17, `--experimental-strip-types`), Fastify, Zod 4, Drizzle + better-sqlite3, Electron, React + TanStack Router, Vitest (+ fast-check, already a dev dependency of `packages/workflow`), Playwright. No new dependency is expected; if one becomes necessary it is `npm install -E` and `npm run check:pins` must pass.

## Execution rule

- Each task is independently reviewable, tests-first (RED → GREEN), and ends with its own commit.
- Per task: its targeted tests + `npm run typecheck` + `npm run lint`. At every sub-phase exit and before any completion claim, the full gate `npm run check:pins && npm run lint && npm run typecheck && npm test` (PowerShell 5.1 has no `&&`: run each command separately and stop at the first non-zero exit), the sub-phase's fake-CLI e2e specs, and one separately authorised opt-in live run.
- Tests never touch a real repository (`apps/orchestrator/test/helpers/temp.ts`: `makeTempDir`, `makeTempRepo`, `assertInTemp`), never spawn the real `claude`/`codex` (including detection probes), and never spend quota. Only `AIK_LIVE_AGENTS=1` + `vitest.live.config.ts` runs real CLIs. **Suite-wide guard (plan review r5 MAJOR-1):** from 6.1a-09 on, `vitest.config.ts` loads the setup file `apps/orchestrator/test/setup/no-real-agent-cli.ts`, which sets `AIK_AGENT_BIN_STRICT=1` and deletes every binary override variable from `process.env` before any test file runs — so a test that starts the app with the default environment (`app.test.ts`, `provider-wiring.test.ts`, …) gets a resolution error instead of a real binary; e2e gets the same guarantee from 6.1a-20.
- Story status changes only through `WorkflowService` (test arrange helpers like `forceStory` excepted). **`gate_runs` rows are written only by `WorkflowService` transactions** (spec §5.7.0). Every persisted byte of CLI/provider output (DB, run log, outbox, chat, SSE) passes the redactor first. Nothing is imported from `spikes/`.
- **Tick-driven tests drain every tick (plan review r4 m3, verified: `runner.ts:153-156` `tick()` starts handlers without awaiting them; `drain()` at `:159` awaits them).** Every test that drives the runner by ticking — the window guard (6.2-03), the gate pipeline test (6.2-24), gate discovery/startup (6.2-05), lane cap (6.2-06b), chat lifecycle (6.3-13) and any later one — runs each iteration as `await runner.tick(); await runner.drain();` and only then checks quiescence or the story status. A loop that checks after `tick()` alone races the handlers and is a review-blocking defect.
- **Task 0 tasks spend a little real quota** (spec OPEN QUESTIONS) unless a row says "free". They run only after the user consents, in an OS-temp scratch repo, and their raw captures stay outside the repository until `scripts/import-agent-fixture.ts` (6.1a-05) imports them through the redactor.
- **Task 0 credential rule:** no probe ever asks a model to read, print or copy a **real** credential or config file (`~/.codex/auth.json`, `~/.claude/.credentials.json`, `CODEX_HOME/*`, `.git-credentials`, …). A probe that tests credential exposure uses a **canary file** outside the workspace that contains a fake token, and deletes it afterwards. Detection-style probes (`auth status`, `login status`) are free and print status only; their captures still go through the import script (email/org scrub).
- **Task 0 cleanup rule:** every Claude prompt probe passes `--no-session-persistence` unless the row needs a resumable session (Q16), in which case it ends with `claude purge <cwd> -y` for each folder it used. Every `codex exec` probe carries the full `<HARDENING>` set (which includes `--disable daemon_auto_start`) and `--ephemeral` unless the row needs a resumable session (Q1 session-id capture, Q2), in which case it ends with `codex delete --force <id> --disable daemon_auto_start`. Every Task 0 task ends with `Get-Process codex, claude -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue` → must be empty (anything left is recorded in the execution record and killed with `taskkill /T /F`).
- **Task 0 shell rule:** any row that passes inline JSON (`--json-schema '{…}'`, `--agents '{…}'`) or captures output with `>` runs in **Git Bash**, not PowerShell 5.1 — PS 5.1 strips embedded double quotes from native-exe arguments (Claude would receive `{type:object,…}`) and its `>` writes UTF-16LE. PowerShell is fine for setup and for rows without JSON or redirection. The PowerShell snippets below show intent; translate them to Bash for those rows (`t=$(mktemp -d)`, `codex="$APPDATA/npm/node_modules/@openai/codex/node_modules/@openai/codex-win32-x64/vendor/x86_64-pc-windows-msvc/bin/codex.exe"`). `import-agent-fixture.ts` also normalises encoding (strips a BOM, converts UTF-16 to UTF-8) as a safety net.

**Executor tags** (user decision: Codex executes code, Claude reviews; see the executor note above): **[Codex]** = a coding agent implements it (Claude substitutes while Codex has no credits); **[Claude]** = Claude does it (verification, live evidence, review sign-off, docs). **Review: Claude-sec** = mandatory Claude security/state-machine review before the commit lands; otherwise normal Claude task review.

**Provenance:** (C) Claude draft · (X) Codex draft · (C+X) merged · (R2) new or changed by spec revision 2 · (R10) new or changed by spec revisions 3–10 (the exact change IDs are on each task's **Spec** line) · (V) changed after verifying the code at `9df7973`.

## Global Constraints

- Windows 11, 4C/8T, 15.7 GB RAM. One heavy role run per repository; `AIK_MAX_CONCURRENT_LANES` default 3; **if any active project uses the agent-team preset, the global effective cap is `min(AIK_MAX_CONCURRENT_LANES, 2)`** (§3.1 rule 1, R16). The chat slot is **not** counted in that cap (S12).
- Subagents: max **2** concurrent per run, `agents.maxSubagents` range 0–4. **Until 6.4 lands the adapters run with an effective `maxSubagents = 0`** (no `Agent` tool, Codex `--disable multi_agent`). UI QA (2b) runs after functional QA (2a), never concurrently.
- Memory guard `AIK_MIN_FREE_MEM_MB` default **2500**. Outside a claimed gate: `queue.defer(jobId, leaseToken, delayMs)` (refunds the attempt); after **30 min**: active story → BLOCKED `environment`; plan job → `planning_failed`; chat turn → `failed`. **Inside a claimed gate:** wait in place (re-check every 30 s, renew the lease), never `defer()`; after 30 min → row `error` not counted + `fail(environment)`; the wait ends at once, writing nothing, on stop / lost lease / failed fence (X1, Y3). Chat: light slot, **1** concurrent chat turn app-wide.
- Runtimes `opencode | claude-code | codex`. Roles `planner | implementer | reviewer | qa | security` (UI label "Coder" for `implementer`; never persist `coder`). `qa` and `security` may only use `claude-code` or `codex` (T12). **Every Claude/Codex × role combination is built and tested** (spec §5.5): Claude as planner/reviewer/security (6.1a-10), implementer (6.1b-11) and QA (6.2-20); Codex as implementer (6.1b-06), QA (6.2-10) and planner/reviewer/security (6.1b-10); chat on both for every role (6.3-04). OpenCode stays planner/implementer/reviewer only.
- Default Claude model `claude-opus-5-5`. Codex model chosen by the router (§5.6); `max`/`ultra` effort never chosen automatically (both allowed as explicit overrides).
- **Claude argv:** `claude -p --output-format stream-json --verbose --permission-prompts none`, prompt on stdin. Read-only roles (planner, code review, security, every chat turn) = `--tools Read,Grep,Glob[,Agent] --permission-mode dontAsk --permission-prompts none` (R14; `Agent` only when effective `maxSubagents > 0`, never for chat — S4). Write roles: implementer = `--tools Read,Grep,Glob,Edit,Write[,Agent] --permission-mode acceptEdits` in the story worktree, persisted like the Codex coder (6.1b-11, M34); QA = the 6.1a-T0 "Claude QA" probe's tool set in the QA worktree, non-persistent (6.2-20, M38). **Plan mode is never used.** Gate roles and candidate chat add `--restricted` + an empty scratch cwd + `--add-dir <worktree>` (R5), also when personal setup is on. Planner/review/security pass `--no-session-persistence`; chat uses `--session-id`/`--resume` with a **stable** cwd and `-n "aik:<KEY>:<role>"` (R4, S3). Clean setup (§5.4): `--strict-mcp-config --mcp-config <empty app config>` on every run, and `--setting-sources project` for the planner, which runs in `project.repoPath`. `--max-budget-usd` on every run and chat turn. Never `--fallback-model`, `--bare`, `--safe-mode`, `--input-format stream-json`, bypass permission modes.
- **Codex argv:** native `codex.exe` (never `codex.cmd`/`codex.ps1`); `codex exec - --json <HARDENING> …` with the prompt on stdin. **`<HARDENING>` is carried by every `codex exec` and `codex exec resume`** (S5, S6, T6): `--ignore-user-config --ignore-rules --disable daemon_auto_start --disable apps --disable plugins --disable remote_plugin --disable browser_use --disable browser_use_external --disable browser_use_full_cdp_access --disable computer_use --disable in_app_browser --disable image_generation --disable goals --disable tool_suggest -c web_search="disabled"`. Other subcommands (`login status`, `debug models`, `delete`) get only `--disable daemon_auto_start`. Gate-like and chat runs on a candidate add `--disable hooks -c project_doc_max_bytes=0` (R3). Only the coder and chat sessions persist; QA, and Codex as planner/reviewer/security (6.1b-10), use `--ephemeral` (R4). Never `-p <profile>`, `codex queue`, `--last`, `--dangerously-*`, `--skip-git-repo-check` (the Settings **Test** probe runs in a `git init`-ed temp dir instead — M36).
- No silent fallback between runtimes, models or paid tiers. `allowPaid: false` → Claude/Codex roles refuse and block with **`permission_denied`** + hint (R7). New and existing projects default to `opencode-legacy`; the agent-team preset needs an explicit choice with the confirmation "Claude and Codex use your paid subscriptions", which sets `allowPaid: true`, and shows the P6 residual-risk notice once (T4).
- Timeouts: planner 20 min, coder 60, code review 20, QA 30 (2a 15 + 2b 15), security 20, chat turn 5. Claude `--max-budget-usd` = `agents.claudeMaxUsdPerRun` default **2.00** on every run and chat turn (Q4 decides whether it bites). **Codex bounds (R6, S16):** the role timeout per run; `budgets.commandsPerRun` (default 300) command executions counted in the stream — the only mid-run kill; `budgets.cliTokensPerStory` (default 3 000 000) of `input − cached_input + output` tokens summed **between** runs — over it, the next Codex run is refused with `model_limit`. There is **no** mid-run token watchdog.
- Interrupt = after the stop request, wait up to **5 s** for a natural exit, then `taskkill /T /F` (stdin is already closed after the prompt). Interrupted sessions are non-resumable. Every spawned PID is recorded for the startup PID sweep (§5.13).
- CLIs, the QA dev server and the QA install get the §6.2 allowlisted environment (git hardening, `DISABLE_AUTOUPDATER=1`, Codex package-root variables included). Project-configured npm-style commands go through `cmd.exe /d /s /c` with shell metacharacters refused, exactly as checks do.
- Minimum CLI versions: Claude Code **2.1.319**, Codex **0.167.0** (the verified versions; settled MINOR M1).
- Exact dependency pins only; tests under OS temp only; status only via `WorkflowService`; redactor on all persisted output; nothing from `spikes/`.
- **Composition-root rule (plan review r4 MAJOR-B).** `app.ts` is the only composition root: role and gate agents are built only by `buildAgentTeam()` (`agents/team.ts`, 6.1a-12), runtimes only by `buildRuntimeRegistry()` (`agents/runtimes/registry.ts`, 6.1a-11), and the runner gets every handler as a constructor dep. The runner never completes a job it has no handler for: an unknown or unwired kind fails the job with `unwired_job_kind` and logs an error (6.1a-21). **`apps/orchestrator/test/app-wiring.test.ts` (6.1a-21) starts the real `startOrchestrator()` (fake CLIs, no network) and checks, on the objects the app actually built:** every `JOB_KINDS` value that any source path enqueues has a dispatch branch with a constructed handler; every runtime kind allowed by `ProjectSettings.agents` is registered; every role × allowed runtime resolves to a constructed agent and a registered runtime; every story-closure step a task has written is registered on the app's `StoryClosure` (`wiring.closure.stepNames()`, 6.1b-12 — round-5 m8); `POST /agent-runtimes/:kind/test` reaches `wiring.registry` (6.1a-11 — round-5 MAJOR-2). **Any task that adds a job kind (or gate kind), a runtime, a role/gate agent or a closure step extends this test — or its expectation lists in `apps/orchestrator/test/wiring/{job-kinds,runtimes,agents,closure-steps}.ts` — in the same commit.** A not-yet-wired item may sit in a list's `pending` section only with its owning task named, only while no service constructed by `startOrchestrator()` can produce it, and the test fails as soon as a pending item is wired (stale entry); each sub-phase acceptance (6.1a-17, 6.1b-08, 6.2-15, 6.3-12) asserts that no pending entry owned by its own sub-phase is left, and 6.4-06 asserts all pending sections are empty. Integration tests (`gate-pipeline.integration.test.ts` and later ones) build agents and runtimes through the same two factories, never a hand-built registry or agent.

## Review Focus

1. **Gate lifecycle invariants I-G1…I-G4** (spec §5.7.0) must hold under every interleaving of freeze, discovery, claim, result, late result, stop, fail, cancel, recover, `human_changes_requested`, new SHA, gate toggle, memory wait, `queue.defer()`, lease loss and restart → pinned in **6.2-17** (model-based property tests — the most important tests of the phase), plus 6.2-03 (machine), 6.2-18 (row effects), 6.2-19 (claim/fence), 6.2-05 (discovery/restart), 6.2-16 (upgrade migration), 6.2-24 (QA and Security agents constructed and dispatched in the real app — without it an agent-team story stalls after code review), 6.2-22 (removal of the temporary `gates === null` compatibility rule and the `gateEngine` flag — after it, no path reaches AWAITING_MERGE without gate rows).
2. A secret split across two stdout chunks or a UTF-8 boundary, typed into chat, or read from a CLI credential file must never be persisted or sent unredacted → 6.1a-04 (chunk-split + token-JSON rule), 6.3-03b (warn-then-send-redacted), 6.3-04 (context packet redaction).
3. Stop, crash, timeout or lease loss between CLI exit and the DB commit must not create a candidate, approve a gate, double-write usage, inherit a stale stop, or leave a grandchild/daemon process alive → 6.1a-03 (tree-kill), 6.1a-11 (usage-once), 6.1a-19 (PID sweep, `sweepExpired({allLeases:true})`), 6.2-19 (row-keyed stops, CAS), 6.1b-08 live (no `codex.exe` survives).
4. Coder-written `.claude/settings.json` hooks, `CLAUDE.md`, `.mcp.json`, `AGENTS.md`/`AGENTS.override.md`, `.codex/` in a candidate must never configure or execute inside a reviewer, QA, security or chat session → 6.1a-T0 (Q9 canary), 6.1a-10 (`--restricted` + scratch cwd + `--add-dir`), 6.1b-T0 (Q10, Q12), 6.1b-04 (`<HARDENING>`, `--disable hooks`, `project_doc_max_bytes=0`), 6.2-07 (`.codex/` removal if Q12), 6.2-11 (tampering finding), 6.3-04 (chat on candidate).
5. A chat turn that writes to its worktree, or a heavy job started while memory is low or another chat is running, must be reset/deferred/queued — never fail the story, never touch another job's worktree → 6.3-04 (`<key>.chat` reset; chat startup skip and `cancel_jobs` exclusion before any chat job is reachable — round 6 m3), 6.3-13 (chat lifecycle), 6.2-06a/b (defer-not-fail).
6. Nothing the plan builds may be left unconstructed or undispatched in the running app — a job kind with no handler, a runtime missing from the registry, or a role with no agent must fail a test, and at runtime fail visibly, never stall or complete silently → **6.1a-21** (`unwired_job_kind` + `app-wiring.test.ts`), extended by 6.1a-11 (runtimes; the Settings Test route reaches the app's registry), 6.1a-12 (role agents), 6.1b-04 (Codex), 6.1b-12 (`cleanup`; closure steps via `wiring.closure`, extended by 6.1b-09, 6.2-07, 6.2-11, 6.3-13), 6.2-02/6.2-05 (`gate` + gate kinds), 6.2-24 (QA/Security), 6.2-22 (`review` retired), 6.3-02/6.3-04 (`chat`). Files the app writes must also be cleaned up by something the app runs: run logs and QA evidence → 6.2-25 (retention + project purge).

| 2 | `runner.ts:344 discoverReview` skips a story once **any** review row exists at the SHA; key `review_<story>_<sha>` | Replaced by gate discovery keyed by `gate_runs` `pending` rows and attempt number (6.2-05, T1) | C, R10 |
| 3 | `machine.ts:125 candidate_committed` ignores `ev.protectedPaths` (computed in `implementer.ts:235`) | Not enforced in Phase 6 unless the user answers P2; defaults gain `.claude/**`, `.codex/**`, `.mcp.json` for parity (6.1a-01) | C, V |
| 4 | `checks/run.ts:87 checkEnvironment` is a deny-list; the check runner sets stdin `ignore`, buffers output and routes `.cmd` through `cmd.exe`; `verify/cleanroom.ts` runs installs with the full parent environment and `shell: true` on Windows | New `spawnStreaming()` + allowlist (6.1a-03, R10, R11); clean-room install gets the allowlist and `cmd.exe /d /s /c` without `shell:true` (6.2-07) | C+X, V, R10 |

| 7 | `planner.ts:187` runs in `project.repoPath`; `planner.ts:316-337` fabricates a plan with `complexity:'M'` on parse failure | Spec §5.4: the Claude planner **stays** in `project.repoPath` with `--setting-sources project` (old M6 plan worktree withdrawn); invalid output is `invalid_output` (6.1a-12) | C+X, V, R10 |

---

## File-ownership map

New module boundaries (one owner each): `process/*` (spawn, env, PID records/sweep), `agents/runtimes/*` (CLI protocol), `agents/role-run.ts` (run bookkeeping), `agents/{qa,security,gate-handler}.ts`, `agents/team.ts` (created by 6.1a-12 with the role agents, extended by 6.2-24 with the gate agents: the one place the app constructs its agents), `buildRuntimeRegistry()` in `agents/runtimes/registry.ts` (6.1a-11; the one place runtimes are constructed — app and integration tests), `apps/orchestrator/test/app-wiring.test.ts` + `apps/orchestrator/test/wiring/*` (6.1a-21: the composition-root guard), `apps/orchestrator/test/setup/no-real-agent-cli.ts` (6.1a-09: suite-wide strict mode), `maintenance/artifacts.ts` (6.2-25: age pruning and project purge of run logs and QA evidence), `chat/messages.ts` (6.3-03b: the one chat-message/turn-status writer that also emits `chat.message`), `workflow/gate-rows.ts` (all `gate_runs` writes), `resources/memory-guard.ts`, `qa/*` (dev server, evidence), `chat/*`, `stories/closure.ts` (the one story-closure hook) + `agents/session-purge.ts`, `agents/credential-risk.ts`, `components/story/*`, `components/kanban/merge-ack-dialog.tsx`, `apps/orchestrator/test/helpers/gates.ts`. Contracts are split per feature (`agent-team.ts`, `codex-tiers.ts`, `gates.ts`, `chat.ts`, `subagents.ts`) so parallel tasks do not share a contracts file.

**Hot shared files — strictly serial, in this order; one task holds the file at a time:**

| File | Owner sequence |
|---|---|
| `packages/contracts/src/index.ts` (one export line per task) | 6.1a-01 → 6.1b-02 → 6.2-01 → 6.3-01 → 6.4-01 |
| `packages/contracts/src/{enums,projects}.ts` | 6.1a-01 only. (Round 6 m5, verified: 6.1a-01 already adds every `agents.qa` default incl. `allowUiNetwork`, and 6.2-01's gate defaults live in `agent-team.ts` (preset) and its new `gates.ts`/`json-schema.ts`; `ReviewV1` is in `agents.ts`. 6.2-01 therefore does not touch these two files; the earlier "→ 6.2-01" entry contradicted its Files list.) |
| `packages/contracts/src/agent-team.ts` | 6.1a-01 → 6.1a-09 (`RuntimeStatusDTO` incl. `warnings`, `binaryPath`, `binaryPrefixArgs`) → 6.2-01 (preset gates on) → 6.1b-01 (`sandboxReady`, Codex catalog fields — the detector that fills them) → 6.1b-06 (preset coder → Codex) → 6.1b-07 (tier-mapping fields for the card) |
| `packages/contracts/src/stories.ts` | 6.1a-14 → 6.2-13 → 6.2-14 → 6.3-01 |
| `packages/contracts/src/events.ts` + `packages/contracts/test/sse.test.ts` (`EVENT_TOPICS`) | 6.3-01 (`chat.message`) → 6.4-01 (`subagent.updated`) |
| `packages/db/src/schema.ts`, `packages/db/migrations/**` incl. `meta/_journal.json`, `packages/db/test/open.test.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/src/projects/purge-repo.ts` | 6.1a-02 (0003) → 6.2-02 (0004) → 6.2-16 (0005 SQL + snapshot, **not** journaled) → 6.2-22 (0005 journal entry — the migration takes effect with the engine-on commit; also takes over `packages/db/test/migrate-0005.test.ts` and deletes **both** copies of 6.2-16's test-only helper, `packages/db/test/helpers/apply-pending-migration.ts` and `apps/orchestrator/test/helpers/apply-pending-migration.ts`) → 6.3-02 (0006) → 6.4-01 (0007). **A parallel agent generating a migration corrupts `_journal.json`.** Every migration SQL + snapshot is hand-written (no interactive `npm run db:generate`). **Journal `when` rule (plan review r3 minor 4, verified in `drizzle-orm@0.45.4` `migrator`: an entry is applied only if `lastApplied.created_at < entry.when`):** every hand-written entry's `when` must be **strictly greater** than the previous entry's — use `max(previous when + 1, Date.now())` — and `idx` continues by one; an entry with a smaller or equal `when` is silently skipped on every DB that already applied the previous migration. 6.1a-02 adds `packages/db/test/journal.test.ts` (reads `meta/_journal.json`: `when` strictly increasing, `idx` = 0…n−1, every `tag` has its `.sql` file and every journaled `.sql` has an entry — the un-journaled `0005_gate_backfill.sql` is the one allowed exception until 6.2-22, which removes the exception); each later migration owner keeps it green. |

| `apps/orchestrator/src/workflow/snapshot.ts` | 6.2-03 (`gates: null` literal only, so the new required `Evidence.gates` typechecks) → 6.2-04 → 6.2-22 |

## Parallel lanes (target: up to 20 concurrent agents)

Honest sizing: the dependency graph never offers 20 independent, non-redundant coding tasks at once. The waves below were **computed** from every task's **Depends on** plus the hot-file order above (a task waits for the previous owner of every hot file it edits); the graph has no cycles. Pure, unwired pre-work from later sub-phases (modules with their own tests and no runtime wiring — they keep the gate green and ship no behaviour) is pulled forward; tasks that used to span waves (6.2-06, 6.3-03) are split into `a`/`b` so each half sits in exactly one wave. The peak is **11** in W0, of which **6 are coding tasks** (the other 5 are Task P and Task 0 verification); W1 and W2 offer 6 coding tasks each. After W2, hot-file serialisation keeps most waves 1–4 wide — that is the honest result for 85 tasks with this graph (round 4 put 6.1a-12 between 6.1a-11 and 6.1a-19 in the `app.ts` order, which shifts everything from W6 on by one wave; round 5 moved 6.1a-15 from W3 to W5 and added 6.2-25 to W18 without shifting any other task; round 6 put 6.3-04 before 6.3-05 in the `workflow/service.ts` order, which splits old W24 and shifts W25 on by one, ending at W30); extra agents would duplicate work, which AGENTS.md forbids. Practical recommendation: ≤ 6 concurrent implementers while Claude also substitutes for Codex, because every Review: Claude-sec task funnels through Claude review. The gate engine (6.2-03 → 6.2-04 → 6.2-18 → 6.2-19 → 6.2-05 → 6.2-24 → 6.2-22 → 6.2-17, with 6.2-21 test helpers alongside) is deliberately serial: it is the critical path of the phase. Task 0 rows that need Codex credits run whenever credits return; tasks depending on them proceed on synthetic fixtures meanwhile.

|---|---|---|

## Sub-phase 6.1a — Claude runtime (TEAM-1, TEAM-2, TEAM-4 schema + presets, TEAM-12 Claude half, env allowlist, bridge fix)

### 6.1a-T0 — Verify open questions Q1 (Claude), Q4, Q9; record Q3/Q8 (answered) and Q5 (dropped) (R2, R10) [Claude]

**Spec:** OPEN QUESTIONS Q1, Q3, Q4, Q5, Q8, Q9; R5, R14, §10 rev 2→3 item 17.

**Goal:** Replace guesses with observed CLI behaviour before any parser or argv builder is written. Needs user consent (Q1, Q4, Q9 spend a little quota; Claude has quota — only Codex lacks credits).

Setup (PowerShell): `$t = Join-Path $env:TEMP "aik-q-$(Get-Random)"; New-Item -ItemType Directory $t | Out-Null; git -C $t init; Set-Content "$t\README.md" '# probe'; Set-Content "$t\a.txt" 'alpha'; git -C $t add .; git -C $t commit -m init`. Empty scratch folder: `$e = Join-Path $env:TEMP "aik-e-$(Get-Random)"; New-Item -ItemType Directory $e | Out-Null`. Raw output goes to a separate `$env:TEMP\aik-captures-<rand>\` folder (never into the repo).

| Q | Exact command (from spec) | Answer → what the plan does |
|---|---|---|
| Q1 (Claude) | In `$t`: `claude -p "say hi" --output-format stream-json --verbose --no-session-persistence > claude.jsonl` — plus the variants the adapter needs: the same with `--json-schema '{"type":"object","properties":{"ok":{"type":"boolean"}},"required":["ok"]}' --tools Read,Grep,Glob --permission-mode dontAsk --permission-prompts none` (also `--no-session-persistence`); and one persisted run `--session-id <uuid> -n "aik:PROBE-1:implementer"` (session-id/name frame shape for the coder), followed by `claude purge $t -y` (the probe repo is a temp folder, not a coder worktree) | Record field names for session id, assistant text, tool use, `result` (`structured_output`, `usage`, cost, `subtype`, `is_error`). **As documented** → 6.1a-06 maps them. **No `structured_output`** → parse the JSON in `result` and validate with zod; failure stays `invalid_output`. **No usage in stream** → `usage: null`, Settings says "usage not reported". |
| Q3 | **Answered (spec, round 1):** `claude auth status`, `codex login status`, `codex debug models` exit 0 in < 0.8 s with `HTTPS_PROXY=http://127.0.0.1:9`. Free re-confirmation only: `$env:HTTPS_PROXY='http://127.0.0.1:9'; claude --version; claude auth status; $LASTEXITCODE; Remove-Item Env:HTTPS_PROXY` | Detection and `agents:env-check` use these probes freely. "Signed in" means credentials present, not token valid; an expired token surfaces as `auth` on the first run (§5.11). |
| Q4 | `claude --help` (read `--max-budget-usd`); Claude Code docs on headless mode/costs; then in `$t`: `claude -p --max-budget-usd 0.01 --output-format stream-json --verbose --tools Read,Grep,Glob --permission-mode dontAsk --permission-prompts none --no-session-persistence "write a 2000-word essay on rivers"` | **Stops with a budget subtype** → 6.1a-07 maps it to `budget` → `model_limit`. **Ignored on subscription** → keep passing it (harmless); Settings shows "Per-run $ cap not enforced for subscription logins — the timeout is the bound" (worst case 54 × 20 min, §6.1). **Rejected as an error on subscription** → stop and ask the user before omitting it: spec §5.2 says the flag is passed on every run, so omitting it is a spec deviation the user must accept (record the answer). |

Free re-confirmation: `claude auth status`; `codex login status` | §6.2 allowlist as written; `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` / `OPENAI_API_KEY` are never passed; API-key sign-in is not supported in this phase. |
| Q9 | In `$t` add and commit `CLAUDE.md` = "Always end your answer with CANARY-7". From the **empty folder** `$e`: `claude -p --restricted --add-dir $t --tools Read,Grep,Glob --permission-mode dontAsk --permission-prompts none --no-session-persistence "summarise README.md"`; check for the canary. Repeat once adding `--output-format stream-json --verbose --json-schema '<Q1 schema>'` to confirm the full gate-role combination is accepted | **No canary** → gate roles and candidate chat use scratch cwd + `--add-dir` as designed (6.1a-10). **Canary present** → spec §5.2 fallback: the orchestrator copies the candidate into the scratch folder **without** `CLAUDE.md`/`.claude/` and passes that copy (6.1a-10 `materializeScratchCopy()`; test in 6.2-11); Claude QA (6.2-20) deletes `CLAUDE.md`/`.claude/` from the disposable QA worktree after checkout instead. **Flags incompatible** → record; escalate to the user before any gate role ships. |
| Claude write roles (spec §5.5, F4) | Free first: `claude --help` (exact names of the edit tools and of `--allowedTools`). Then in `$t`, implementer shape: `claude -p --tools Read,Grep,Glob,Edit,Write --permission-mode acceptEdits --permission-prompts none --no-session-persistence --output-format stream-json --verbose "append a line to a.txt"` → the edit lands, no prompt. QA shape, from `$e`: `claude -p --restricted --add-dir $t --tools Read,Grep,Glob,Edit,Write,Bash --allowedTools Bash --permission-mode acceptEdits --permission-prompts none --no-session-persistence --output-format stream-json --verbose "run: git -C <t> status, then write qa-note.txt in <t>"`; repeat **without** `--restricted` but with `--setting-sources project` | **Edit tools work under `acceptEdits`** → 6.1b-11 as designed (tool set `Read,Grep,Glob,Edit,Write`, M34). **Bash runs under `--restricted` with the allow rule** → Claude QA (6.2-20) keeps `--restricted`. **`--restricted` removes Bash** → Claude QA runs without `--restricted`, with scratch cwd + `--add-dir <qa worktree>` + `--setting-sources project` (the empty scratch cwd has no project settings, so nothing loads) + empty `--strict-mcp-config` (M38). **Bash denied in both** → Claude QA cannot run commands: 6.2-20 ships with every command-dependent AC `unclear` "Claude QA cannot run commands" and Settings warns; record and tell the user. Bypass permission modes are never tried. |

### 6.1a-21 — Composition-root guard: unwired job kinds fail visibly; `app-wiring.test.ts` (plan review r4 MAJOR-B, systemic) [Codex] · Review: Claude-sec

(Numbered last, runs first: W0, first owner of `app.ts` and `runner.ts`.)

**Spec:** §3 ("tests never call real CLIs"), §5.1 (runtime abstraction), §5.7 "Discovery" (gate jobs dispatched), §5.8.1 (chat jobs), §5.13 (no silent stalls); plan review r4 MAJOR-B.

**Why (verified at `9df7973`, Reality check #23):** three round-4 findings had one cause — components built by one task and never constructed or dispatched by the running app (routed role agents, the Codex runtime, the chat turn executor; round 3 found the same for QA/Security). Nothing catches this class: `runner.ts:420-421` completes any kind it has no case for, so an unwired job just disappears, and the integration tests build their own agents and registries. This task makes the failure visible at runtime and adds one test that inspects the **real** composition, which every later task that adds a job kind, runtime or agent must extend (Global Constraints).

**Files:** Modify `apps/orchestrator/src/agents/runner.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/src/app.ts`. Create `apps/orchestrator/test/app-wiring.test.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/test/wiring/job-kinds.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/test/wiring/scan-enqueues.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/test/runner-dispatch.test.ts` (own file, so `runner.test.ts`'s owner order is untouched).

**Runner (`runner.ts`):**
- `handleJob`'s `switch` becomes a dispatch table `private readonly handlers: Partial<Record<JobKind, (job: LeasedJob) => Promise<void>>>`, filled in the constructor **only for kinds whose handler deps were passed** (`plan`, `implement`, `verify`, `review`, `merge` — all five have required deps or, for `merge`, today's explicit no-merger branch at `runner.ts:425-428`, kept unchanged and now logged as a warning so existing runner tests built without a merger behave as before). Later tasks add entries the same way (`cleanup` 6.1b-12, `gate` 6.2-05, `chat` 6.3-04).
- **No silent complete:** a claimed job whose kind has no entry → `queue.fail(job.id, job.leaseToken, 'unwired_job_kind: <kind>', { retry: false })` (terminal `failed`, never `completed`), `logger.error({ jobId, kind }, 'unwired job kind')`, and, when the job carries a story id (or `payload.storyId`), one redacted story activity line "internal error: no handler for job kind <kind>". `AgentRunnerDeps` gains `logger?: Pick<Logger, 'error' | 'warn'>` (optional; default writes to stderr; `app.ts` passes its pino logger) — optional so the existing `new AgentRunner(` calls in tests stay untouched.
- `wiredJobKinds(): ReadonlySet<JobKind>` — the keys of the dispatch table (read-only introspection for the wiring test; 6.2-05 adds `wiredGateKinds()`).

**App (`app.ts`):** `OrchestratorOptions` gains `env?: NodeJS.ProcessEnv` (default `process.env`; later tasks resolve agent binaries and detection from it — 6.1a-09, 6.1a-11, 6.1b-01 — so tests never stub the global environment; under `npm test` the default is made safe by 6.1a-09's setup file). `RunningOrchestrator` gains a read-only test seam `wiring: { runner: AgentRunner }` (later owners add `registry` 6.1a-11, `team` 6.1a-12, `closure` 6.1b-12, `chat` 6.3-04) — the objects `startOrchestrator()` actually built, nothing re-created. No other behaviour change.

**`app-wiring.test.ts`** (temp `dataDir` via `makeTempDir`, real `startOrchestrator()`, personal mode — no gateway token, no network; from 6.1a-09 on the suite-wide strict-mode setup file guarantees that no real CLI is ever resolved, and from 6.1a-11 on `opts.env` = `fakeCliEnv(…)` (6.1a-09's helper: fake binaries + `AIK_AGENT_BIN_STRICT=1`) so detection reports the fake CLIs as installed; `close()` in `afterEach`):
- **Job kinds:** `scanEnqueuedKinds()` (`wiring/scan-enqueues.ts`) reads every `apps/orchestrator/src/**/*.ts` and finds every call of the form `<receiver>.enqueue(` / `<receiver>.enqueueIn(` (also with optional chaining, `<receiver>?.enqueue(` / `?.enqueueIn(` — round 6 m2: 6.1b-12 gives `WorkflowService` an optional `queue`). **Only `JobQueue` receivers are job enqueues (round-5 m1, verified: `office/bridge.ts:253,260` call `this.enqueue(event)` on its own `HookEvent` buffer, with no `kind`):** a receiver that is `queue`, `this.queue`, `deps.queue` or `this.deps.queue` must carry a literal `kind: '<x>'`, which is collected — a `JobQueue` call **without** a literal `kind` fails the scan (so the check cannot be bypassed by a variable); any **other** receiver must be listed in `wiring/scan-enqueues.ts` `NON_JOB_ENQUEUE_SITES` (file + receiver + reason, today exactly `office/bridge.ts` `this.enqueue` "Office hook-event buffer, not the JobQueue"), and an unlisted one fails the scan (so a `JobQueue` held under another name cannot slip through either). `wiring/job-kinds.ts` exports `NEVER_ENQUEUED: Record<JobKind, reason>` (today `publish`, `postmerge`, `pr_poll`, `fetch`, `clone`, `cleanup`, `retention` — "declared in `JOB_KINDS`, no enqueue site") and `PENDING: Record<JobKind, owningTask>` (empty today). Assertions: every `JOB_KINDS` value is in exactly one of {scanned, `NEVER_ENQUEUED`, `PENDING`}; every scanned kind not in `PENDING` ∈ `wiring.runner.wiredJobKinds()`; every `PENDING` kind ∉ `wiredJobKinds()` (a stale entry fails); a `NEVER_ENQUEUED` kind that appears in the scan fails.
- (6.1a-11 adds the runtime block, 6.1a-12 the role × runtime → agent block, 6.2-05 the gate-kind block.)

- [ ] **RED** `runner-dispatch.test.ts` (temp DB, real `JobQueue`, `AgentRunner` with fakes): a queued job of a kind with no handler (e.g. `retention` and `cleanup` jobs inserted directly, one with a `storyId`) → after `await runner.tick(); await runner.drain();` each job is `failed` with `lastError` starting `unwired_job_kind`, never `completed`; the logger spy got one error per job with its kind; the story activity line is written and redacted; `plan`/`implement`/`verify`/`review`/`merge` jobs dispatch exactly as before (spies; a `merge` job on a runner without a merger still completes, now with a logged warning); `wiredJobKinds()` = `{plan, implement, verify, review, merge}`.
- [ ] **RED** `app-wiring.test.ts` as above; plus negative self-tests of the scan over fixture source strings: `this.queue.enqueue({ kind: someVar, … })` → scan error; with a literal → collected; `this.queue?.enqueueIn(tx, { kind: 'x' })` and `this.queue.enqueueIn(tx, { kind: 'x' })` → both collected as `x`; `this.enqueue(event)` in a file listed in `NON_JOB_ENQUEUE_SITES` → ignored; the same call in an unlisted file → scan error; the real source tree → no error (the two `office/bridge.ts` calls are the only allowlisted ones).
- [ ] Run `npx vitest run apps/orchestrator/test/runner-dispatch.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/app-wiring.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/runner.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/run-interruption.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/plan-feedback.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/app.test.ts` → FAIL → PASS (existing files unchanged).
- [ ] Commit: `feat(orchestrator): unwired job kinds fail visibly; composition-root wiring test`

**Depends on:** none. **Parallel with:** W0. **Next owners:** `app.ts` 6.1a-09; `runner.ts` 6.1a-12; `app-wiring.test.ts` 6.1a-11; `wiring/job-kinds.ts` 6.2-02.

### 6.1a-01 — Contracts: roles, runtimes, agent settings, presets, CLI budgets (C+X, R2, R10) [Codex] · Review: Claude-sec (defaults)

### 6.1a-03 — `spawnStreaming()`, PID records and the allowlisted agent environment (C+X, R2, R10) [Codex] · Review: Claude-sec

**Spec:** §5.2 "Process spawning", §5.13 "Orphaned processes" (PID records), §6.2; R10, R11, R16 (no-shell), S-minor `CLAUDE_CODE_GIT_BASH_PATH`.

**Files:** Create `apps/orchestrator/src/process/{spawn-streaming,agent-env,tree-kill,pid-records,project-command}.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/test/{spawn-streaming,agent-env,project-command}.test.ts`. Modify `apps/orchestrator/src/checks/run.ts` (import the extracted tree-kill and `cmd.exe /d /s /c` rule; **no behaviour change** — the check runner itself is not reused for CLIs, R10), `apps/orchestrator/test/checks-run.test.ts` (unchanged assertions stay green).

**Interfaces — Produces:**
```ts
interface StreamingSpawnOptions { runId: string; file: string; prefixArgs?: readonly string[]; args: readonly string[]; cwd: string;
  env: NodeJS.ProcessEnv; stdinText: string | null; deadlineMs: number; pidDir: string;
  onStdoutChunk(chunk: Buffer): void; onStderr?(chunk: Buffer): void; }
interface StreamingProcess { pid: number|undefined;
  interrupt(): Promise<void>;   // stop request → wait ≤ 5 s for a natural exit → taskkill /T /F
  killTree(): Promise<void>;    // idempotent shared promise
  done: Promise<{ exitCode: number|null; timedOut: boolean; interrupted: boolean; stderrTail: string }>; }
function spawnStreaming(o: StreamingSpawnOptions): StreamingProcess;          // throws UnsupportedCommandError
function agentEnvironment(base: NodeJS.ProcessEnv, o: { runtime: 'claude-code'|'codex'|'qa-ui'|'install'|'dev-server'; codexPackageRoot?: string }): NodeJS.ProcessEnv;
function projectCommandArgv(command: string, env: NodeJS.ProcessEnv): { file: string; args: string[] };   // npm/npx/yarn/pnpm → cmd.exe /d /s /c, metacharacters refused; file always absolute
function resolveExecutable(name: string, env: NodeJS.ProcessEnv): string | null;   // PATH × PATHEXT lookup in the given (allowlisted) env; absolute .exe/.com only
function writePidRecord(dir: string, runId: string, r: { pid: number; image: string; startTime: string }): void;
function readPidRecords(dir: string): PidRecord[];
```
Rules: argv only, `shell:false`, `windowsHide:true`; throws `UnsupportedCommandError` for a relative `file` or one ending `.cmd|.bat|.ps1`; stdin is a pipe: `stdinText` is written, then stdin is **closed** (both CLIs read the prompt until EOF); stdout chunks are handed over as they arrive (line decoding in 6.1a-04); stderr captured (tail kept). Every spawned PID is written with its image name and start time to `<pidDir>/<runId>.json` and the file is removed on exit. Allowlist exactly spec §6.2: Windows system (`SystemRoot`, `windir`, `ComSpec`, `PATHEXT`, `PATH`, `TEMP`, `TMP`, `OS`, `NUMBER_OF_PROCESSORS`, `PROCESSOR_ARCHITECTURE`, `ProgramData`, `ProgramFiles`, `ProgramFiles(x86)`, `ProgramW6432`, `CommonProgramFiles`, `CommonProgramW6432`, `ALLUSERSPROFILE`, `PUBLIC`, `PSModulePath`, `SystemDrive`), user profile (`USERPROFILE`, `HOME`, `HOMEDRIVE`, `HOMEPATH`, `APPDATA`, `LOCALAPPDATA`, `USERNAME`, `USERDOMAIN`, `COMPUTERNAME`), network (`HTTP_PROXY`, `HTTPS_PROXY`, `NO_PROXY` and lowercase forms, `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS`, `SSL_CERT_FILE`), CLI-specific if set (`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `CLAUDE_CODE_GIT_BASH_PATH`, `CODEX_HOME`; `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH` for `qa-ui` only). **Always set:** `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, `GCM_INTERACTIVE=never`, `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT=1`, `GIT_CONFIG_KEY_0=credential.helper`, `GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_0=` (empty), `DISABLE_AUTOUPDATER=1`; for codex also `CODEX_MANAGED_PACKAGE_ROOT=<codexPackageRoot>` and `CODEX_MANAGED_BY_NPM=1`. **Never:** `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`, any `GITHUB_*`/`GH_*`, the app's API/gateway/office tokens, `AIK_*` (Q8 answered — there is no passthrough option).

- [ ] **RED** `spawn-streaming.test.ts` (child = `process.execPath -e <script>`, cwd from `makeTempDir`): argv elements `a&b`, `"q"`, `%PATH%`, `$(x)`, `^`, `|` arrive byte-identical; `.cmd`/`.bat`/`.ps1`/relative → throws before spawning; the child reads the full `stdinText` then sees EOF; output arrives before the child exits (child prints, sleeps 1 s, prints); deadline on a sleeping child → `timedOut:true` within deadline+2 s and its **grandchild** pid is gone (`process.kill(pid,0)` throws); `interrupt()` on a child that exits by itself within 2 s → no `taskkill`; on a child that ignores the stop → killed after 5 s ± 1 s; `killTree()` twice → one `taskkill`; a PID record with image + start time exists while running and is gone after exit.
- [ ] **RED** `agent-env.test.ts`: base env with `GITHUB_TOKEN, GH_TOKEN, AIK_API_TOKEN, OPENAI_API_KEY, ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD, NODE_OPTIONS, SOME_RANDOM_VAR, PATH, USERPROFILE, CODEX_HOME, CLAUDE_CODE_GIT_BASH_PATH, HTTPS_PROXY, https_proxy` → the codex env has exactly the allowlisted keys present + the fixed keys (five git keys, `DISABLE_AUTOUPDATER=1`, `CODEX_MANAGED_PACKAGE_ROOT`, `CODEX_MANAGED_BY_NPM=1`); the claude env keeps `CLAUDE_CODE_GIT_BASH_PATH` and has no Codex package-root keys; `qa-ui` adds `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH` only when present; no option makes `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` appear.
- [ ] **RED** `project-command.test.ts`: `npm run dev -- --port 4000` → `{file: <env.ComSpec>, args: ['/d','/s','/c','npm run dev -- --port 4000']}` with `file` absolute (`ComSpec` taken from the env passed in; falls back to `<SystemRoot>\System32\cmd.exe`; neither present → `UnsupportedCommandError`); commands containing `&`, `|`, `>`, `<`, `^`, `%X%` → refused; plain `node server.js` → `{file: <absolute node.exe found by resolveExecutable>, args: ['server.js']}`; a first token that resolves only to a `.cmd`/`.bat`/`.ps1` and is not npm/npx/yarn/pnpm → refused; unresolvable first token → `UnsupportedCommandError` "not found on PATH"; every returned `file` passes `spawnStreaming`'s absolute-path check (table test feeds each result into a stubbed `spawnStreaming` validator). `resolveExecutable` uses a fake PATH of temp dirs (`makeTempDir`) holding dummy files, never the real PATH.
- [ ] Run `npx vitest run apps/orchestrator/test/spawn-streaming.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/agent-env.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/project-command.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/checks-run.test.ts` → FAIL, implement, → PASS.
- [ ] Commit: `feat(orchestrator): streaming argv spawner with PID records and allowlisted agent environment (TEAM-2/3, §5.2, §6.2)`

### 6.1a-04 — JSONL decoder, redacted run-log writer, credential-token redaction rule (X, C, R10) [Codex] · Review: Claude-sec

**Spec:** §5.3, §6.2 "Credential exposure through reads"; R12.

**Files:** Create `apps/orchestrator/src/agents/runtimes/{jsonl,transcript}.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/test/{cli-jsonl,cli-transcript}.test.ts`. Modify `apps/orchestrator/src/secrets/redactor.ts` and its existing test file.

**Interfaces — Produces:** `createJsonlDecoder(onFrame: (frame: unknown) => void, onMalformed: (line: string) => void): { push(chunk: Buffer): void; end(): void }` (StringDecoder UTF-8, `\r\n` tolerant, 1 MiB per-line cap → `onMalformed` with a fixed marker, never OOM); `createRunLog({ path, redact, maxBytes = 8 MiB }): { appendFrame(f: unknown): void; appendDiagnostic(text: string): void; close(): Promise<void> }` — redacts **complete** frames (recursive string leaves) before writing and before truncation; overflow writes one fixed diagnostic line and drops the rest. Log path `<dataDir>/runs/<runId>/run.ndjson` (never inside a worktree). New redactor rule (R12): `"(access_token|refresh_token|id_token)"\s*:\s*"[^"]+"` → value replaced, also when the JSON appears escaped inside a tool-output string.

- [ ] **RED**: a frame split inside a multi-byte character decodes once and correctly; missing final newline flushed on `end()`; 2 MiB line → one malformed marker, decoder continues; a registered fake secret `sk-test-SECRET` split across two chunks never appears in the log file; log stops at 8 MiB with one overflow diagnostic; stderr bytes are never parsed as frames; a tool-output frame containing `{"tokens":{"access_token":"eyJ…","refresh_token":"rt-…","id_token":"x"}}` (the shape of `~/.codex/auth.json` / `~/.claude/.credentials.json`), raw or JSON-escaped, is written with all three values redacted.

### 6.1a-05 — Fake CLI shim, fixture provenance, live-suite separation (C+X, V) [Codex] · Review: normal

**Spec:** §3 (tests never call real CLIs; opt-in live suite).

**Files:** Create `apps/orchestrator/test/helpers/{fake-cli.mjs,fake-cli.ts,fake-runtime.ts}`, `apps/orchestrator/test/fake-cli.test.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/test/fixtures/agent-cli/manifest.json`, `apps/orchestrator/test/fixtures/agent-cli/{claude-2.1.319,codex-0.167.0}/` (scenario files), `scripts/import-agent-fixture.ts`, `vitest.live.config.ts`. Modify `vitest.config.ts` (exclude `apps/orchestrator/test/live/**`).

**Why argv routing (plan review r6 MAJOR-1, verified against this plan):** one app start, one story and one acceptance spec make many different calls to the same binary — detection probes (`--version`, `auth status`/`login status`, `debug models`, `features list`), prompt runs for several roles (Claude planner → `PlanV1`, reviewer → `ReviewV1`, security → `SecurityV1` in one story), sequences (QA fails → rework → all approve, 6.2-15), and housekeeping (`claude purge`, `codex delete`). A shim that replays one fixed scenario for every call fails all of these: detection probes get prompt-run output, one Claude scenario cannot satisfy three schemas, sequences are impossible, and — because Codex structured output is read from the `-o <file>` that the shim never wrote — every Codex structured run would end `invalid_output`. The shim therefore chooses its response **from the argv of each call**.

**Interfaces — Produces:**
- **`FakeCliConfig`** (`test/helpers/fake-cli.ts`, also what `fakeCliEnv()` (6.1a-09), `withFakeCli()` and the e2e default (both 6.1a-20) take):
  ```ts
  type ScenarioRef = string;                                  // a scenario file name in the CLI's fixture dir
  type PromptKey = 'planner' | 'reviewer' | 'qa' | 'security' | 'implementer' | 'ask' | '*';
  type ProbeKey = 'version' | 'auth' | 'models' | 'features' | 'purge' | 'delete' | 'sandboxHelp';
  type FakeCliConfig =
    | ScenarioRef                                             // shorthand = { prompts: { '*': ref } }
    | { probes?: Partial<Record<ProbeKey, ScenarioRef>>;      // overrides of the built-in probe defaults
        prompts?: Partial<Record<PromptKey, ScenarioRef | ScenarioRef[]>>; // array = per-key call sequence
        missing?: true };                                     // e2e/integration only: the override points at a
                                                              // non-existent file under the test's temp dir
  ```
- `makeFakeCli(cli: 'claude'|'codex', config: FakeCliConfig, opts?: { recordPath?: string }): { binary: { file: string; prefixArgs: string[] }; recordPath: string; configPath: string }` — `file = process.execPath`, `prefixArgs = [fake-cli.mjs, '--aik-fake-config=<tempfile>.json', '--aik-fake-record=<recordPath>']`; **shared record (round 7 m4):** `opts.recordPath` (under OS temp, else rejected) lets both CLIs append to **one** record file — `fakeCliEnv`, `withFakeCli` and the e2e default always pass one shared path for Claude and Codex, so record order is the cross-CLI call order (6.2-24 asserts Claude review → Codex QA → Claude security from it); without it a fresh temp file is created (argv, not env, because the env is allowlisted; the config is a JSON file `{cli, fixtureDir, probes, prompts}` written under OS temp, so argv stays short and quote-free). `--aik-fake-scenario=<name>` stays accepted as the string shorthand.
- **Call classification — deterministic, first match wins** (the shim strips its own `--aik-fake-*` args first):
  1. **Probes** (built-in defaults, each overridable by `probes.<key>`; matching looks at the subcommand words only and ignores `--disable <feature>` pairs, so `codex login status --disable daemon_auto_start` still matches): Claude `--version` → `version` (default `version-ok`), `auth status` → `auth` (`auth-signed-in`), `purge <dir> -y` → `purge` (`purge-ok`); Codex `--version` → `version` (`version-ok`), `login status` → `auth` (`auth-signed-in`), `debug models` → `models` (`models`), `features list` → `features` (`features`), `delete --force <id>` → `delete` (`delete-ok`), `sandbox --help` → `sandboxHelp` (`sandbox-help-ok`). Probes never consult `prompts`, so a test that only sets prompt scenarios still gets a healthy, signed-in detection.
  2. **Prompt runs** (Claude argv contains `-p`; Codex argv starts with `exec`): the **prompt key** is derived as follows — (a) a schema is present (Claude `--json-schema <json>`, parsed; Codex `--output-schema <path>`, read): key from the schema's top-level `title` — `PlanV1` → `planner`, `ReviewV1` → `reviewer`, `QaV1` → `qa`, `SecurityV1` → `security`; any other title (e.g. the tiny schema an adapter unit test passes) → no role key; (b) no schema and a write-capable run (Claude `--permission-mode acceptEdits`; Codex `-s workspace-write` or `-c sandbox_mode="workspace-write"`) → `implementer`; (c) otherwise (read-only, no schema: chat turns and the Settings **Test** probe) → `ask`. The `-n "aik:<KEY>:<role>"` tag is **recorded but not used** for selection: it is absent on resumed chat turns and on every non-persisted run, so it cannot key all calls. The scenario is `prompts[key]`, else `prompts['*']`; neither → the shim writes `fake-cli: no scenario for prompt key <key>` to stderr and exits 2 (a visible `crashed` failure — never a silent success, never a real CLI).
  3. **Anything else** → stderr `fake-cli: unrecognised argv` and exit 2.
- **Sequences:** when `prompts[key]` is an array, call *n* of that key (1-based, counted per `cli:seqKey` across processes) uses element *n*; past the end the last element repeats. **Counter key (round 7 m4):** `seqKey` is the `prompts` entry that answered — the derived key when `prompts[key]` exists, else the **literal `'*'`** when the fallback answered (so a `'*'` array advances once per fallback call, whatever role key the call derived); probe lines use their probe key and never count toward a prompt sequence. The counter is derived from the **record file**: under an exclusive lock the shim counts earlier `kind:'prompt'` record lines with the same `cli` and `seqKey`, appends its own line `{seq, cli, kind:'probe'|'prompt', key, seqKey, scenario, callNo, argv, cwd, envKeys, stdin, nameTag}` and releases the lock before replaying — so concurrent CLI processes (a chat turn beside a heavy run) never get the same call number. **`seq` is global to the record file** (earlier line count + 1, across both CLIs when the record is shared). **Lock (round 7 m4):** `<record>.lock` is created with `wx` and holds `{pid, createdAt}`; a waiter retries every 10 ms and **breaks** the lock (deletes it, then retries `wx`) when its `pid` is no longer alive (`process.kill(pid, 0)` → `ESRCH`) or it is older than **5 s** (a live holder keeps it only for a count + append); the lock is removed in `finally`. A waiter that still has no lock after **10 s** writes `fake-cli: record lock <path> not acquired within 10 s (holder pid <pid>)` to stderr and exits 2 — a visible `crashed`, never an unrecorded replay.
- **Replay:** `<scenario>.jsonl` with optional per-line delays (stdout), optional `<scenario>.stderr.txt` and exit code from the manifest entry. **Codex `-o <file>`:** if the argv has `-o <path>` (alias `--output-last-message`), the shim writes `<scenario>.out.json` (the scenario's structured payload — byte-exact, so `structured-invalid` writes the invalid payload) to that path **before** exiting, only for scenarios whose manifest entry has `writesOutput: true` (success and invalid-output scenarios; auth/quota/crash/hang write nothing). Claude structured output stays in the stream's `result` frame. `writes-file` / `code-writes-file` write `fake-<callNo>.txt` (content includes the call number) into the cwd (Codex `-C <dir>` when given), so successive coder attempts produce distinct candidate SHAs.
- **Scenario set seeded here (both CLIs, synthetic):** probe scenarios `version-ok`, `version-old`, `auth-signed-in` (incl. email/org fields), `auth-signed-out`, `auth-expiring` (`loginExpiresAt` at a fixed date; tests set the clock), `models` (incl. one `visibility:"hide"` model), `features`, `features-no-multi-agent`, `delete-ok`, `purge-ok`, `sandbox-help-ok`; generic prompt scenarios `success`, `structured-ok` (payload `{ok:true}`), `structured-invalid`, `auth`, `quota`, `budget`, `crash`, `hang` (spawns a `setInterval` grandchild), `exit-on-stop`, `malformed`, `writes-file`, `subagents`, `many-commands` (N command executions), `usage-at-end`; **role scenarios** `plan-ok` (valid `PlanV1`), `plan-invalid`, `review-approve`, `review-changes` (valid `ReviewV1`), `code-writes-file`, `ask-ok` (plain answer, no schema). QA and security role scenarios (`qa-pass`, `qa-fail`, `qa-unclear`, `security-pass`, `security-high`, `security-medium`) are added by **6.2-01**, which owns `QaV1`/`SecurityV1`.
- `fake-runtime.ts`: in-memory `AgentRuntime` double (scripted events/results, records inputs). Manifest entry: `{ file, cli, cliVersion, provenance: 'recorded'|'synthetic', capturedAt?, redactorVersion?, sha256, exitCode, writesOutput?, contract?: 'PlanV1'|'ReviewV1'|'QaV1'|'SecurityV1' }`. `import-agent-fixture.ts <raw.jsonl> <dest>` redacts with `createRedactor()`, scrubs the user's email, org ids and home paths (`<HOME>`), and writes the manifest entry.
- **Schema title rule (consumed by 6.1a-10, 6.1b-04, 6.2-01):** every JSON Schema an adapter hands to a CLI (`--json-schema`, `--output-schema`) carries a top-level `title` equal to its contract name (`PlanV1`, `ReviewV1`, `QaV1`, `SecurityV1`). It is harmless to the real CLIs and is what makes rule 2(a) deterministic; the owning tasks' golden-argv tests assert it.

Codex `--version`, `login status`, `debug models`, `features list`, `delete --force <id> --disable daemon_auto_start`, `sandbox --help` each get their default; none consumes a prompt sequence slot; (2) **probe override** — `probes: { version: 'version-old', auth: 'auth-signed-out' }` changes exactly those two; **Suite-wide strict mode (round-5 MAJOR-1, verified — Reality check #24):** once this task wires detection into `app.ts`, every app start probes the CLIs, and `app.test.ts` (five `startOrchestrator(opts(dataDir))` calls) and `provider-wiring.test.ts` (:38, :75) start the app with **no** `env`, i.e. `process.env`. `vitest.config.ts` gains `setupFiles: ['apps/orchestrator/test/setup/no-real-agent-cli.ts']` (the include globs only match `*.test.ts`, so the setup file is never collected as a test). The setup file runs before every test file and sets `process.env.AIK_AGENT_BIN_STRICT = '1'` and **deletes** `AIK_ALLOW_BIN_OVERRIDE`, `AIK_CLAUDE_BIN`, `AIK_CLAUDE_BIN_PREFIX_ARGS`, `AIK_CODEX_BIN`, `AIK_CODEX_BIN_PREFIX_ARGS` (a developer's shell cannot re-enable a real binary). `vitest.live.config.ts` (6.1a-05) is a separate config and does **not** load it. **Interfaces — Produces:** `resolveClaudeBinary(env, platform): {file, prefixArgs: string[]} | null` (throws `AgentBinaryForbiddenError` in strict mode, below). **Override:** `AIK_CLAUDE_BIN` (absolute path) is honoured only when `AIK_ALLOW_BIN_OVERRIDE=1`; `AIK_CLAUDE_BIN_PREFIX_ARGS` is a **JSON array of strings** (round-5 m3), e.g. `["C:\\…\\fake-cli.mjs","--aik-fake-config=C:\\…\\cfg.json","--aik-fake-record=C:\\…\\rec.ndjson"]`, parsed with zod `z.array(z.string().max(4096)).max(16)` and returned as `prefixArgs` (absent → `[]`; invalid JSON or shape → resolution error `installed:false`, `lastError: "invalid AIK_CLAUDE_BIN_PREFIX_ARGS"`, nothing spawned). Without an override: PATH search for the native `claude.exe`, then `%USERPROFILE%\.local\bin\claude.exe`; absolute, exists, not `.cmd`. **Strict mode:** with `AIK_AGENT_BIN_STRICT=1` (every e2e launch — 6.1a-20 — and every Vitest file — the setup file above) the PATH/profile search is skipped entirely: only the override is used, and no override → `AgentBinaryForbiddenError` ("real agent binaries are disabled (AIK_AGENT_BIN_STRICT)"), which detection reports as `installed:false` with that `lastError` and every later caller (6.1a-11 `runtimeFor`, 6.1b-09 purge, 6.1a-11 Test probe) turns into a visible failure — never a spawn. `fakeCliEnv({ claude?: FakeCliConfig, codex?: FakeCliConfig, record })` (`test/helpers/fake-cli.ts`; `FakeCliConfig` is 6.1a-05's argv-routed map form — probe overrides, role-keyed prompt scenarios, sequences — or the string shorthand) builds exactly these variables (`AIK_ALLOW_BIN_OVERRIDE=1`, `AIK_AGENT_BIN_STRICT=1`, `AIK_CLAUDE_BIN=process.execPath`, `AIK_CLAUDE_BIN_PREFIX_ARGS=JSON.stringify([fake-cli.mjs, '--aik-fake-config=…', '--aik-fake-record=…'])`, same for `AIK_CODEX_BIN*`) on top of a minimal base env; `record` is **one** shared record path passed to both `makeFakeCli` calls (round 7 m4: its global `seq` is the cross-CLI call order); an omitted CLI gets `{}` (probe defaults: installed, supported, signed in; any prompt run fails visibly with exit 2); `{ missing: true }` sets `AIK_<CLI>_BIN` to a non-existent file under the test's temp dir (strict mode stays on, nothing is spawned, detection reports `installed:false`); integration tests (6.2-24 and later) and `app-wiring.test.ts` use it, and 6.1a-20's e2e fixture uses the same encoding. `detectRuntime(kind): Promise<RuntimeStatusDTO>` = `{kind, installed, version, supported (≥ min), signedIn: boolean|null, authMethod: string|null, subscriptionType: string|null, loginExpiresAt: string|null, binaryPath: string|null, binaryPrefixArgs: string[], lastError, checkedAt, warnings: string[]}` (`binaryPath`/`binaryPrefixArgs` = the resolved file and prefix args — what 6.1a-15's `openCliLogin` spawns; round-5 m3); probes `claude --version`, `claude auth status` via the injected spawn (`opts.agentSpawn ?? spawnStreaming`) + `agentEnvironment`, 15 s timeout, cwd `os.tmpdir()`, 5-min cache, `refresh()` forces. **Only** `loggedIn`, `authMethod`, `subscriptionType`, `loginExpiresAt` are kept; email and organisation fields are discarded before anything is stored, logged or returned. Routes: `GET /agent-runtimes`, `POST /agent-runtimes/:kind/refresh`. `agentRuntimesRoutes(app, deps)` receives `{ detect, db, now }` (6.1a-16 adds the credential-risk endpoints with `db`; 6.1a-11 adds `registry` and the Test route).

### 6.1a-10 — Claude Code adapter (C+X, R2, R10) [Codex] · Review: Claude-sec

**Spec:** §5.2 "Claude adapter", §5.4, §6.4; R4, R5, R14, R16 (`-o`/schema files outside worktrees, session naming/purge), S3, S4, OQ Q4, Q9.

**Files:** Create `apps/orchestrator/src/agents/runtimes/{claude-code,scratch}.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/test/{claude-args,claude-adapter,claude-scratch}.test.ts`.

**Interfaces — Produces:** `buildClaudeArgs(input: ClaudeArgsInput): string[]` (pure), `class ClaudeCodeRuntime implements AgentRuntime` (`start` here; `ask` in 6.3-04), `prepareScratchCwd(runDir): string` (`<dataDir>/runs/<runId>/cwd`, empty), `materializeScratchCopy(worktree, dest)` (Q9 fallback only: copy without `CLAUDE.md`/`.claude/`). Argv always: `-p --output-format stream-json --verbose --permission-prompts none --model <id> [--effort <lvl>] --strict-mcp-config --mcp-config <runDir>/mcp.json` (content `{"mcpServers":{}}`) and `--max-budget-usd <claudeMaxUsdPerRun>` (per Q4 decision), then:
- **read-only roles** (planner, reviewer, security): `--tools Read,Grep,Glob --permission-mode dontAsk` (`,Agent` appended only when effective `maxSubagents > 0` — 6.4-03). Never `--permission-mode plan`. Write roles are added later in this file: implementer (6.1b-11) and QA (6.2-20); until then `buildClaudeArgs` throws for `implementer`/`qa` so no half-built route can run.
- **planner (clean setup):** cwd = `project.repoPath` (the user's checkout, whatever branch — not a candidate), `--setting-sources project`.
- **gate roles (reviewer, security):** `--restricted` (always, also with personal setup), cwd = empty scratch dir, `--add-dir <worktree under review>` (or the Q9 scratch copy).
- **persistence (R4):** planner, reviewer, security pass `--no-session-persistence`; no role run is ever resumed. Persisted sessions (chat, 6.3-04) pass `--session-id <uuid>` and `-n "aik:<KEY>:<role>"`.
- personal setup: drop `--setting-sources`, `--strict-mcp-config`, `--mcp-config`; keep `--restricted` + scratch cwd on gate roles.
- `outputSchema` (`{ name, schema }`, 6.1a-08) → `--json-schema <json>` = `schema` with its top-level `title` set to `outputSchema.name` (`PlanV1`, `ReviewV1`, … — 6.1a-05's schema title rule, which the fake shim keys on; an existing different `title` is overwritten) (argv > 24 000 chars → fail fast); `systemAppend` → `--append-system-prompt`. Schema/MCP files and the scratch cwd live under `<dataDir>/runs/<runId>/`, never inside a worktree.
- Driver: `spawnStreaming(stdinText: prompt)` → `createJsonlDecoder` → `decodeClaudeFrame` → `onEvent` + `createRunLog`; completion = terminal `result` + process exit, bounded by `timeoutMs`; `interrupt()` = `StreamingProcess.interrupt()` (session marked non-resumable); `structured` validated by the caller's zod schema → `invalid_output`; failure via `classifyRun`. When a non-persisted run ends, its scratch cwd is purged with `claude purge <cwd> -y`, then the folder is deleted (§5.4).

- [ ] **RED** `claude-args.test.ts`: golden argv for planner (clean: `--setting-sources project`, `--no-session-persistence`, read-only tool set, cwd = repoPath), reviewer (`--restricted` + scratch cwd + `--add-dir` + schema + `--no-session-persistence`), reviewer with personal setup (still `--restricted` + scratch cwd), security; `--tools` is exactly `Read,Grep,Glob` while effective `maxSubagents = 0`; budget flag present/absent per Q4 decision; the `--json-schema` value parses as JSON whose `title` is the contract name (`PlanV1` for the planner, `ReviewV1` for the reviewer); effort absent when unset; builder throws on a model starting with `-`; argv never contains `--fallback-model`, `--bare`, `--safe-mode`, `--input-format`, `plan`, `bypassPermissions`, `--resume`.
- [ ] **RED** `claude-adapter.test.ts` (fake shim; adapter-level tests use the string shorthand `makeFakeCli('claude', '<scenario>')`, i.e. `prompts['*']`, with a tiny test schema — round 6 MAJOR-1): `structured-ok` → `ok` + `structured`; `structured-invalid` → `invalid_output`; `auth`/`quota`/`budget`/`crash` → failures; `hang` + `interrupt()` → `interrupted:true`, grandchild gone within 5 s + 2 s; `exit-on-stop` → no `taskkill`; recorded stdin equals the prompt; recorded env keys ⊆ allowlist; recorded cwd for a gate role is the empty scratch dir; run log contains no registered fake secret; after a non-persisted run the fake `claude purge <cwd> -y` was invoked — answered by the shim's built-in `purge` probe default, not by the prompt scenario (record line `kind:'probe'`, `key:'purge'`) — and the folder is gone.

### 6.1a-11 — Runtime registry, `allowPaid` enforcement, role-run bookkeeping, Settings Test probe (C+X, R2, R10) [Codex] · Review: Claude-sec

**Spec:** §5.1, §5.5 "Paid opt-in", §5.11 (usage rows; Settings **Test**), §5.12 (`purpose`), §6.2 "Test"; R7, S15, T12, X7 (route check shared with the gate claim), B7.

**Files:** Create `apps/orchestrator/src/agents/runtimes/registry.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/src/agents/role-run.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/src/agents/runtimes/probe-dir.ts` (`makeProbeDir()`), `apps/orchestrator/test/{runtime-registry,role-run,runtime-test-probe}.test.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/test/wiring/runtimes.ts`. Modify `apps/orchestrator/src/app.ts` (`const registry = buildRuntimeRegistry({…, opencode: runtime })` — no adapter is constructed in `app.ts` itself; `registry` added to the `agentRuntimesRoutes` deps; `wiring.registry` added to the 6.1a-21 seam), **`apps/orchestrator/src/api/routes/agent-runtimes.ts`** + `apps/orchestrator/test/agent-runtimes-routes.test.ts` (round-5 MAJOR-2: `POST /agent-runtimes/:kind/test` and the `registry` dep move here from 6.1a-09), `apps/orchestrator/src/agents/interrupts.ts` (compare-and-unregister by run id; row-keyed gate stops come in 6.2-19), `apps/orchestrator/test/app-wiring.test.ts` (runtime block + Test-route case).

**`buildRuntimeRegistry(deps: { env, dataDir, detect, redact, opencode: OpenCodeRuntime, now?, spawn? }): RuntimeRegistry`** — the **only** place runtime adapters are constructed (round-4 MAJOR-B b); `opencode` is the app's one `OpenCodeRuntime` (round-5 m2, verified: `app.ts:154-156` builds it with the managed-mode default model from `providerService`, so the factory must not build a second one without that default; tests inject a mock/fake-HTTP `OpenCodeRuntime`); `app.ts` calls it once, and every integration test (`gate-pipeline.integration.test.ts`, later chat/subagent integration tests) calls it with a fake-binary `env` instead of building a registry by hand. This task registers `opencode` (6.1a-08 adapter over the existing `OpenCodeRuntime`) and `claude-code` (6.1a-10); **6.1b-04 registers `codex` here** in the same commit as the adapter. `registry.kinds(): ReadonlySet<RuntimeKind>`; `registry.get(kind)` for a kind that is not registered never throws past the caller and never substitutes another runtime: `runtimeFor` returns `{ blocked: 'provider_down', detail: 'the <kind> runtime is not available in this build' }` — visible, never a fallback.

**Interfaces — Produces:** `checkRoute(project, role): {ok:true} | {ok:false, reason:'permission_denied', hint}` (the role's route is allowed — T12 — and, for any Claude/Codex route, `allowPaid === true`; reused by the gate claim in 6.2-19); `runtimeFor(project, role): { runtime: AgentRuntime; choice: ModelChoice } | { blocked: BlockedReason; detail: string }` — reads `settings.agents.roles[role]`; refuses with **`permission_denied`** + hint "Enable the agent-team preset (allows paid Claude/Codex) or switch this role to OpenCode" when `allowPaid` is false (R7); refuses when cached detect says not installed / too old / signed out (`provider_down`/`auth`; arranged with `{ missing: true }`, `{ probes: { version: 'version-old' } }`, `{ probes: { auth: 'auth-signed-out' } }`); **never returns another runtime**. `RoleRunService.run({storyId, role, purpose:'role_run'|'chat', inputSha, assertLease, input, hooks?}): Promise<RoleRunResult & {runId; runIds: number[]}>` — creates the `agent_runs` row (runtime, runtime_version, `model_slug`, effort, model_choice_reason, session_ref, purpose, `log_path` = `<dataDir>/runs/<runId>/run.ndjson`) before spawn; **timeout (round-5 m7):** `timeoutMs = (settings.agents.roles[role].timeoutMin ?? ROLE_TIMEOUTS_MS[role] / 60_000) × 60_000` for role runs (for `qa` the override is the total, split evenly between 2a and 2b by 6.2-10/6.2-20), while a `purpose:'chat'` turn always uses the fixed 5-min chat timeout (§6.1); before this round `timeoutMin` (spec §5.5, editable in 6.3-10) was read by nothing; redacts the assembled prompt before sending (§6.3), maps events → `appendRunActivity` (redacted) + `run.output`, registers the interrupt, writes exactly one `model_usage` row **per spawned run** (runtime, model_slug, effort, tokens in/out, `cached_input_tokens`, `cost_usd` or NULL, `duration_ms`), and unregisters in `finally`; on `crashed` it retries once with a fresh session (new run row) within the remaining deadline.

**Hooks (F5 — one run-creation path shared with the gate claim):**
```ts
interface RoleRunHooks {
  preCreatedRunId?: number;          // the first attempt uses this existing agent_runs row (created by the gate claim
                                     // transaction, 6.2-19) instead of inserting one; it is updated, never duplicated
  beforeRetry?(): boolean;           // called before the crash retry spawns; false → no retry, the crashed result is returned
                                     // (the gate handler re-checks its fence + row-keyed stop here; default: () => true)
  onRunCreated?(runId: number): void;// called synchronously right after ANY agent_runs row this service inserts (a first
                                     // attempt without preCreatedRunId, or an approved retry) and before spawn; never for
                                     // a preCreatedRunId row (the gate handler calls gate-rows `recordRun` in a
                                     // WorkflowService tx, W7 — so a second run() inside one gate is recorded too)
}
```
The service never creates a run row except (a) the first attempt when no `preCreatedRunId` is given and (b) an approved retry. Callers outside gates (planner, implementer, chat) pass no hooks and behave as before.

**Settings Test probe (moved here from 6.1a-09 — round-5 MAJOR-2).** `POST /agent-runtimes/:kind/test` in `routes/agent-runtimes.ts`, whose deps become `{ detect, registry, db, now }`: user-initiated only; `registry.get(kind)` (an unregistered kind → 200 `{ok:false, failure:'not_installed', detail:'the <kind> runtime is not available in this build'}`, nothing spawned); a tiny read-only prompt (no output schema — the fake shim keys it as `ask`) through that adapter's `start()` in a fresh OS-temp dir prepared by `makeProbeDir()` = `mkdtemp` + `git init` + one empty commit, deleted afterwards; 30 s; **run id (round 6 m7):** the probe has no `agent_runs` row, so the adapter is given the run id **`probe-<uuid>`** (`crypto.randomUUID()`) — its run dir is `<dataDir>/runs/probe-<uuid>/` (run log, MCP config, Codex `-o`/schema files), which can never collide with a numeric `agent_runs` id; 6.2-25 prunes `probe-*` folders by age; failures through `classifyRun` (6.1a-07); writes one `model_usage` row with `agent_run_id NULL` (M24 — the nullable columns come from 6.1a-02). The `git init` is there for Codex (6.1b-04): `codex exec` refuses a non-git cwd and `--skip-git-repo-check` stays forbidden everywhere (M36); Claude uses the same helper so both probes run in identical conditions. It is not a role run: no `agent_runs` row, no `allowPaid` check (the user pressed the button that says it uses quota).

- [ ] **RED**: legacy settings → OpenCode adapter; `allowPaid:false` + claude role → blocked `permission_denied` with the hint, the OpenCode double never called; `qa` routed to opencode → `permission_denied`; detect signed-out → `auth` with the CLI named; role-run writes every new column (incl. `model_slug`) and `purpose`; usage row exactly once per spawned run on success, failure and timeout; interrupt unregistered when the runtime throws; a late registration after Stop cannot clear the stop; crash then success → two run rows, distinct `session_ref`; quota → zero retries; lost lease before finalisation → no usage/transition written. **Hooks:** with `preCreatedRunId` the first attempt inserts **no** `agent_runs` row (row count unchanged, the given row gets runtime/version/session_ref) — no double run creation; crash + `beforeRetry` → `false` (simulating Stop) → **no second spawn** (fake shim record has one entry), result `crashed`, one run row; crash + `beforeRetry` → `true` → `onRunCreated` called exactly once with the new run id **before** the second spawn (record order asserted), `runIds` = both ids; `beforeRetry` is never called for quota/auth/budget/invalid_output; a `run()` **without** `preCreatedRunId` but with `onRunCreated` → called once with the first attempt's new id before its spawn (round-3 minor 1: a gate's second `run()` call, e.g. the reviewer's invalid-output retry or QA 2b); with `preCreatedRunId` → not called for the first attempt. **Factory:** `buildRuntimeRegistry` with a fake-binary `env` (`fakeCliEnv({ claude: {}, codex: {} })` — probe defaults = installed and signed in, no prompt run expected) and an injected `OpenCodeRuntime` mock → `kinds()` = `{opencode, claude-code}` and the `opencode` adapter wraps **that** instance (a planner run on it reaches the mock; the factory constructs no `OpenCodeRuntime` of its own); a project routing a role to `codex` → `runtimeFor` blocked `provider_down` "not available in this build", no other runtime called; under the suite-wide strict mode with no override → `runtimeFor` for a Claude route is blocked `provider_down` naming strict mode, nothing spawned. **Timeout (m7):** `roles.reviewer.timeoutMin: 7` → the spawned run's deadline is 7 min (fake clock); unset → 20 min; a chat-purpose run ignores `timeoutMin` (5 min). `log_path` is written on the run row and points at the run log the adapter wrote.
