### 6.1a-12 — Planner and reviewer on the routed runtime (C+X, V, R10) [Codex] · Review: Claude-sec

**Spec:** §5.2, §5.4 (planner cwd, skills scope), §8 P5 (auto-retry exclusions); R16 (skills scope), S15, S-minor "planner cwd wording".

**Files:** Modify `apps/orchestrator/src/agents/{planner,reviewer,implementer,runner}.ts` (implementer: constructor wiring + the S15 attempt-history filter only, stays OpenCode; runner: `failedPlanCount` filter + auto-retry exclusions), **`apps/orchestrator/src/app.ts`** (round-4 MAJOR-B a: replace the three `new PlannerAgent/ImplementerAgent/ReviewerAgent(…)` lines at `app.ts:234-236` with `const team = buildAgentTeam({ … })` and pass `team.planner`, `team.implementer`, `team.reviewer` to `new AgentRunner`; `wiring.team` added to the seam), `apps/orchestrator/test/app-wiring.test.ts` (role × runtime → agent block); create **`apps/orchestrator/src/agents/team.ts`**, `apps/orchestrator/test/wiring/agents.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/src/git/worktree-guard.ts` (`assertWorktreeUntouched(path, expectedHead): Promise<{ok:true}|{ok:false, changes:string[]}>`), `apps/orchestrator/test/{role-routing,worktree-guard}.test.ts`; existing `planner/reviewer/implementer/provider-failure` tests get constructor wiring only — **acceptance: zero edits to existing assertions**.

**Why `app.ts` is in this task (round-4 MAJOR-B a, verified):** the routed agents' deps change from `runtime: OpenCodeRuntime` (`planner.ts:17`, same shape for implementer and reviewer) to `{ registry, roleRuns, … }`; `app.ts:234-236` constructs them with `runtime`, so this commit cannot typecheck — let alone route anything — without editing `app.ts`. Leaving `app.ts` to 6.2-24 (W17) would mean the 6.1a exit (Claude plans and reviews a real story) never runs in the app.

**`buildAgentTeam(deps): AgentTeam`** (`agents/team.ts`) — the **only** place role agents are constructed; `app.ts` and every integration test call it. Deps = what `app.ts` already has (`db`, `workflow`, `bus`, `registry` + `roleRuns` from 6.1a-11, `worktreeManager`, `interrupts`, `skills`, `redact`). Returns `{ planner, implementer, reviewer }` with a non-optional return type (dropping an agent is a type error); routes are resolved per run through `registry.runtimeFor`, never at construction, so a settings change needs no restart. 6.2-24 extends it with `qa` and `security` (it does not create the file).

- Planner on claude-code: read-only tool set, `outputSchema: PlanV1`, cwd = `project.repoPath`, `--setting-sources project`, `--no-session-persistence`; no `.opencode` skills (and no skill-vault skills at all) for Claude/Codex runtimes (§5.4); invalid structured result → planning failure with `invalid_output` recorded on the run (no fabricated plan); legacy fallback parse kept for OpenCode only. `autoAcceptPlans` passed explicitly; never auto-accept a plan with open clarifications.
- Planning failures stay in NEW (`planning_status = planning_failed`) with the reason shown (P5 default). Runner: `failedPlanCount` counts only `purpose = 'role_run'` planner runs (S15); `auth`/`quota`/`budget` failures are **excluded from the automatic retry**, `invalid_output` keeps it. (`retry_planning`: 6.1a-18.)
- **Implementer attempt history (S15, `implementer.ts:349-353`):** the query that lists the last three `role = 'implementer'` runs for the repair prompt adds `eq(agentRuns.purpose, 'role_run')`, so coder **chat** turns (6.3-04 writes `purpose='chat'` runs with the real role name) never appear as "attempt N" in a rework prompt.
- Reviewer on claude-code: read-only + `--restricted` + scratch cwd + `--add-dir <review worktree>`, `outputSchema: ReviewV1`, non-persistent, existing HEAD/porcelain post-check via `assertWorktreeUntouched`. (Gate fencing and the stale-denial fix land in 6.2-19/6.2-09.)
- [ ] **RED** `role-routing.test.ts` (fake runtime): agent-team → planner claude read-only with cwd = repoPath and `setting-sources project`; reviewer claude with `restricted:true`, `addDirs:[reviewWorktree]`, `scratchCwd` set; implementer still OpenCode; invalid planner output → no plan row, run failure `invalid_output`, auto-retry job enqueued; quota/auth/budget planning failure → **no** auto-retry job; a `purpose='chat'` planner run does not count as a failed plan; a `purpose='chat'` implementer run is absent from the implementer's repair-prompt attempt history (S15) while the `role_run` ones are listed; reviewer fake writing a file → review discarded (existing behaviour) and story unchanged; **skills scope (§5.4, §9 call 17):** a table over all five roles plus chat on `claude-code` and `codex` → `skills/bridge.ts` installs nothing and no skill text reaches the prompt (OpenCode roles unchanged).
- [ ] **RED** `app-wiring.test.ts` role × runtime → agent block (extends 6.1a-21, Global Constraints rule): for every role in `ROLES` and every runtime the `ProjectSettings.agents` schema allows for that role (T12: `qa`/`security` only `claude-code`/`codex`), on a temp project with that route and `allowPaid: true` and fake detection signed in (`opts.env = fakeCliEnv({ claude: {}, codex: {} })`: probe defaults only; the block resolves routes and never starts a run, so an accidental prompt run fails with the shim's exit 2) — (a) `wiring.team` holds a constructed agent for the role (`planner`, `implementer`, `reviewer`; `qa`/`security` from 6.2-24) and (b) `wiring.registry.runtimeFor(project, role)` resolves to the registered runtime of exactly that kind (not blocked, never another kind). Cells whose role is in `wiring/agents.ts` `PENDING = { qa: '6.2-24', security: '6.2-24' }` or whose runtime is in `wiring/runtimes.ts` `PENDING` are skipped and must still be unwired (stale entry fails). **Subset kept from the retired 6.2-24 source guard:** `src/app.ts` contains `buildAgentTeam(` and `buildRuntimeRegistry(` exactly once each and no `new PlannerAgent(`/`new ImplementerAgent(`/`new ReviewerAgent(`/`new QaAgent(`/`new SecurityAgent(`/`new ClaudeCodeRuntime(`/`new CodexRuntime(` (so the app cannot drift from what the integration tests build). Argv per cell is not this test's job — that is `runtime-role-matrix.test.ts` (6.2-20, chat columns 6.3-04); a cell whose argv builder is not built yet (e.g. Claude implementer before 6.1b-11) fails at run time with a thrown builder error, never silently.
- [ ] Run `npx vitest run apps/orchestrator/test/role-routing.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/worktree-guard.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/planner.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/reviewer.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/implementer.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/runner.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/app-wiring.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/app.test.ts` → FAIL → PASS.

### 6.1a-13 — Remove the `aik:open-terminal-cli` shell injection (C+X, R2, V, R10) [Codex] · Review: Claude-sec

**Spec:** §6.3, §5.11 (no-shell console launch via the orchestrator API); R16.

**Files:** Create `apps/desktop/src/main/open-cli.ts`, `apps/desktop/test/open-cli.test.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/test/open-cli-target.test.ts`. Modify `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge.ts`, `apps/desktop/src/preload/index.ts`, `packages/contracts/src/bridge.ts` (+ `OpenCliTargetDTO`), `apps/ui/src/components/kanban/terminal-modal.tsx` (caller; also delete the non-existent `/api/desktop/open-terminal` call), **`apps/orchestrator/src/api/routes/stories.ts`** (new `GET /stories/:id/open-cli-target`, already-wired route file — no `app.ts` change), `apps/orchestrator/src/stories/service.ts` (`getOpenCliTarget(storyId)`).

**Interfaces — Produces:** IPC payload `{ storyId: number }` (zod `.strict()`; legacy `{sessionId, cwd}` rejected). Main has no database: it asks the orchestrator (authenticated bridge request) for `GET /stories/:id/open-cli-target` → `OpenCliTargetDTO = {runtime:'opencode', sessionRef: string, cwd: string} | {available:false, reason}` — the story's latest OpenCode run's `opencode_session_id` and the story worktree path from its `workspaces` row (`getAgentRun` exposes the session id but no cwd, so a dedicated lookup is added) — never trusts the renderer. `buildOpenCliCommand({runtime:'opencode', sessionRef, cwd}): {file, args}` validates `sessionRef` `^ses_[A-Za-z0-9]{1,64}$`, `cwd` absolute, existing, `realpath` inside the worktrees root or a registered `repoPath`. Launch `spawn(<resolved exe>, args, {cwd, detached:true, stdio:'ignore', shell:false, windowsHide:false})` — no `cmd.exe`, no `start`, no `sh -c` (M7).

### 6.1a-20 — e2e never spawns a real CLI: fake binaries by default (plan review minor 9) [Codex] · Review: Claude-sec

**Spec:** §3 ("tests never call real CLIs"); B7 (detection is free but still a real process — e2e must not run it).

**Files:** Modify `e2e/fixtures.ts`, `apps/desktop/src/main/supervisor.ts`. Create `e2e/tests/no-real-cli.spec.ts`, `apps/desktop/test/supervisor-agent-env.test.ts`.

From the moment 6.1a-09 wires detection into `app.ts`, every app start probes `claude --version` / `claude auth status` — so an e2e run would spawn the user's real CLI. This task makes the safe setting the **default for every e2e launch**, not something each spec opts into:
- `e2e/fixtures.ts` `launchApp(dataDir, env)` (today it spreads the caller's `env` last, at line 20) applies the fake-binary variables **after** the caller's `env`, so no spec can drop them, and always sets `AIK_ALLOW_BIN_OVERRIDE=1`, `AIK_AGENT_BIN_STRICT=1`, `AIK_CLAUDE_BIN=<process.execPath>` + `AIK_CLAUDE_BIN_PREFIX_ARGS=JSON.stringify([<fake-cli.mjs path>, '--aik-fake-config=<per-test temp file>.json', '--aik-fake-record=<per-test temp file>.ndjson'])` — the JSON-array encoding 6.1a-09 defines (round-5 m3), built by the same `makeFakeCli` as `fakeCliEnv` — and the same for `AIK_CODEX_BIN*` (inert until 6.1b-01 reads them). **Default config (round 6 MAJOR-1, map form):** `{ probes: { auth: 'auth-signed-out' }, prompts: {} }` for both CLIs — every detection probe gets a valid answer (version ok, signed out, so the agent-team preset stays unavailable in specs that do not opt in), and any prompt run fails visibly with the shim's exit 2 "no scenario for prompt key" instead of replaying a probe answer or spending anything. A spec changes the **config** through `withFakeCli()`, never removes the fakes; `missing: true` points the override at a non-existent file inside the test's temp dir, which keeps strict mode and spawns nothing. The fixture's teardown reads every record file and fails the test if any recorded `argv[0]`/file is not the fake shim, and asserts (via `tasklist` filtered on the test's process tree start time) that no `claude.exe`/`codex.exe` was started during the test.
- **`withFakeCli({ claude?: FakeCliConfig, codex?: FakeCliConfig })` (`e2e/fixtures.ts`, created here — round 7 m2; it was in 6.1a-17 (W7), too late for 6.1a-15 (W5)):** per-test selection on top of the default fakes, in 6.1a-05's map form (probe overrides, role-keyed prompt scenarios, sequences, `missing`); the fixture still applies the fakes after the spec's env, so a spec can change the config but never remove them. **Semantics (round 7 m1):** each CLI named in the argument **replaces** that CLI's fixture default as a whole (no merge with `{ probes: { auth: 'auth-signed-out' } }`); a CLI **omitted** from the argument gets `{}`, exactly as in `fakeCliEnv` — probe defaults, i.e. installed and **signed in**, and any prompt run exits 2 — not the signed-out fixture default. A spec that calls `withFakeCli` therefore has both CLIs signed in unless it says otherwise (needed from 6.1b on, when the preset needs both); a spec that never calls it keeps the signed-out default. Both CLIs share one per-test record file (6.1a-05 `recordPath`).
- `supervisor.ts` (which today has no env filtering for these) builds the orchestrator child's env so that `AIK_ALLOW_BIN_OVERRIDE`, `AIK_AGENT_BIN_STRICT` and the `AIK_*_BIN*` variables reach it **only when `!app.isPackaged`**; a packaged build strips them even if set in the parent env.

- [ ] **RED** `supervisor-agent-env.test.ts` (env builder extracted as a pure function): unpackaged → the variables passed through; packaged → all stripped.
- [ ] **RED** `no-real-cli.spec.ts`: launch the app with decoy files `claude.exe`/`codex.exe` first on a temp `PATH` (round-5 self-check: the earlier "node scripts that write a marker file" could never run on Windows — a `.exe` must be a PE image, so a resolved decoy would fail to start and the marker test would pass even if the decoy **had** been chosen; empty decoy files plus the resolved path are checked instead) → after Settings mounts and the board loads, `GET /agent-runtimes` (through the app's bridge) reports `binaryPath` = the fake shim's `process.execPath` for Claude — never a path in the decoy dir — and the fake record shows the detection probes as `kind:'probe'` lines (`version`, `auth`) answered by their probe scenarios, with no `kind:'prompt'` line; the teardown assertion is itself tested by a negative case run in a unit test of the fixture helper. **`withFakeCli` (round 7 m1/m2):** `withFakeCli({ claude: { prompts: { planner: 'plan-ok' } } })` → Claude's config file holds exactly that map (the default `auth-signed-out` override is gone, so `GET /agent-runtimes` says Claude signed in) and Codex's config file is `{}` (signed in too); `withFakeCli({ codex: { missing: true } })` → `AIK_CODEX_BIN` points at a non-existent file in the test's temp dir (checked on the launch env — Codex detection reads it only from 6.1b-01) and Claude is signed in; without `withFakeCli` both stay signed out; the record file path is the same for both CLIs.
- [ ] `npm run build`; `npx playwright test -c e2e/playwright.config.ts e2e/tests/no-real-cli.spec.ts` → PASS; then the existing e2e suite once (must stay green with the fakes on).
- [ ] Commit: `test(e2e): every e2e launch uses fake agent CLIs, selectable per test with withFakeCli; real binaries are never spawned`

### 6.1a-19 — Startup recovery for CLI runs, PID sweep, `sweepExpired({allLeases:true})` (R10) [Codex] · Review: Claude-sec

**Spec:** §5.13 (startup sweep for `agent_runs`, orphaned processes, fast restarts); R9, S-minor "`cmd.exe` in the PID sweep", U2, X4.

**Files:** Create `apps/orchestrator/src/process/pid-sweep.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/test/{pid-sweep,startup-recovery}.test.ts`. Modify `apps/orchestrator/src/jobs/queue.ts` (`sweepExpired(opts?: { allLeases?: boolean })`), `apps/orchestrator/src/app.ts` (startup order: PID sweep → `sweepExpired({allLeases:true})` → CLI run recovery → existing `fail(interrupted)` loop), `apps/orchestrator/test/job-queue.test.ts`.

- `sweepExpired({ allLeases: true })` ignores `leaseExpiresAt` (only this process can hold a lease) and uses the real clock for every timestamp it writes; the periodic in-process sweep keeps the default (expired leases only).
- PID sweep: for each `<dataDir>/pids/*.json`, `taskkill /T /F` only if the PID is alive **with the same image name and start time** and the image is `claude.exe`, `codex.exe`, `node.exe` or `cmd.exe`; delete the file either way.
- `agent_runs` left `running` by a CLI runtime (`runtime != 'opencode'`) → `interrupted`, `session_ref` marked non-resumable (never used by "continue previous coder session").
- Gate rows and chat turns join this startup sequence in 6.2-05 and 6.3-13.

- [ ] **RED** `job-queue.test.ts`: a leased job with an unexpired lease is untouched by `sweepExpired()` and swept by `sweepExpired({allLeases:true})`; written timestamps equal the injected real clock, never a far-future value.
- [ ] **RED** `pid-sweep.test.ts` (process listing injected): matching PID + image + start time → `taskkill /T /F` once; same PID with a different start time (PID reuse) → not killed; image `explorer.exe` → not killed; dead PID → file deleted, nothing killed.
- [ ] **RED** `startup-recovery.test.ts`: a fast restart (lease 10 s from expiry) leaves no job `leased`; a `running` Claude run → `interrupted` + non-resumable; an OpenCode run is left to the existing path.
- [ ] Run `npx vitest run apps/orchestrator/test/pid-sweep.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/startup-recovery.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/job-queue.test.ts` → FAIL → PASS.
- [ ] Commit: `feat(orchestrator): startup PID sweep, all-lease job sweep and CLI run recovery (§5.13)`

**Depends on:** 6.1a-03 (PID records), 6.1a-12 (`app.ts` order: 6.1a-11 → 6.1a-12 → 6.1a-19, round-4 MAJOR-B). **Parallel with:** 6.1a-18.

### 6.1b-02 — Versioned tier file and deterministic model router (R2, C+X, R10) [Codex] · Review: Claude-sec

**Spec:** §5.6; B1, R16 (hidden models, `ultra` override).

**Files:** Create `packages/contracts/resources/codex-model-tiers.json`, `packages/contracts/src/codex-tiers.ts`, `packages/contracts/test/codex-tiers.test.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/src/agents/runtimes/model-router.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/test/model-router.test.ts`. Modify `packages/contracts/src/index.ts`, `apps/desktop/scripts/build.mjs` + `apps/desktop/test/packaging-config.test.ts` (the JSON must ship in the packaged app).

**Tier file shape:** `{ "schemaVersion": 1, "codexCliVersion": "0.167.0", "tiers": { "fast": {"model": "<slug>", "efforts": [...]}, "default": {...}, "strongest": {...} } }` — slugs filled from 6.1b-T0's `codex debug models` output (free) and confirmed by the user (USER question N1); no slug hard-coded in TypeScript.

**Interfaces — Produces:** `routeCodexModel({ role: 'implementer'|'qa', complexity: 'S'|'M'|'L'|'XL'|null, reworkCount: number, tiers: CodexTiers, catalog: CodexCatalog, override?: {model?: string; effort?: Effort} }): { model, effort, reason, warnings: string[] } | { error: 'provider_down', detail }`.
Rules (§5.6): base table (coder S fast/low, M default/medium, L default/high, XL strongest/xhigh; QA S fast/low, M fast/medium, L default/medium, XL default/high); escalation one step per rework, effort first up to `xhigh`, then next tier keeping `xhigh`, cap strongest + `xhigh`; never `max`/`ultra` automatically (allowed only as an explicit override); effort clamped to the model's supported levels; slug missing from the (visible) catalog → nearest available model of the **next tier down** + warning; none → `provider_down`; never a stronger tier than mapped; override model and/or effort → used as-is, no escalation (M10), reason "user override"; `complexity:null` → `M` with reason "plan complexity unknown". Reason format: `"L story, rework #1 → effort high→xhigh"`.

- [ ] **RED**: all 8 cells; rework 0..5 sequences from each cell; cap; override incl. `ultra`; strongest missing → default tier + warning; all missing → `provider_down`; a mapped slug present only as `visibility:"hide"` counts as missing; unsupported effort clamp; tier-file schema rejects unknown `schemaVersion`; the JSON is present in the packaged resources list.
- [ ] Run `npx vitest run packages/contracts/test/codex-tiers.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/model-router.test.ts apps/desktop/test/packaging-config.test.ts` → FAIL → PASS.

Modify **`apps/orchestrator/src/agents/runtimes/registry.ts`** (`buildRuntimeRegistry` registers `codex` — round-4 MAJOR-B b), `apps/orchestrator/test/runtime-registry.test.ts`, **`apps/orchestrator/test/wiring/runtimes.ts`** (remove the `codex` pending entry). `app.ts` is **not** touched: the factory's deps (`env`, `dataDir`, `detect`, `redact`) already carry everything the adapter needs (`resolveCodexBinary(env)` and the package root from 6.1b-01).

**Registration (round-4 MAJOR-B b, verified):** before this round, 6.1a-11 built the registry in `app.ts` before this adapter existed and this task edited neither file, so the running app had no Codex runtime and the 6.2-24 pipeline test — which built its own registry — could not notice. Now the adapter is constructed only inside `buildRuntimeRegistry`, in the same commit that creates it, and `app-wiring.test.ts` fails if `codex` is allowed by the settings schema but not in the app's registry.

`CODEX_HARDENING` (constant, one source) = the `<HARDENING>` list in Global Constraints (with any Q10/T0 corrections). `buildCodexExecArgs(input)`: `exec - --json ...CODEX_HARDENING -C <dir> -s <read-only|workspace-write> -m <model> -c model_reasoning_effort=<lvl> [-c agents.max_threads=<n> | --disable multi_agent] [--output-schema <runDir>/schema.json -o <runDir>/last.json]` (`schema.json` carries a top-level `title` = the contract name — 6.1a-05's schema title rule; `strict-schema.ts` sets it from `RoleRunInput.outputSchema.name` — round 7 m7 — and `toStrictJsonSchema` (6.2-01) preserves it), then per run kind:
- **coder:** `-s workspace-write -C <story worktree>`; persisted (no `--ephemeral`); `-c sandbox_workspace_write.network_access=true` **only** when `input.network` (set by 6.1b-06 from the approved plan's non-empty `dependencyChanges`).
- **QA (6.2-10) and Codex chat about a candidate (6.3-04):** `--ephemeral` for QA (chat persists); `--disable hooks -c project_doc_max_bytes=0`; the orchestrator prepends the **base branch's** `AGENTS.md` text to the prompt (`input.baseAgentsMd`).
- Effective `maxSubagents = 0` (until 6.4) → `--disable multi_agent`, never `-c agents.max_threads`.
- **personal setup** (M11, pending N2): **no effect on Codex argv** until the user answers N2 — spec §5.2 (the full `<HARDENING>` set on every `codex exec` and `codex exec resume`) is the more specific security rule, so it wins over §5.4's general toggle wording. The toggle still affects Claude (6.1a-10). If N2 is answered "drop restrictions for the coder", a follow-up commit adds that path and a matching carve-out in the test below.
`buildCodexResumeArgs(ref, input)`: `exec resume <ref> - --json ...CODEX_HARDENING [--disable hooks -c project_doc_max_bytes=0] [--disable multi_agent] -c sandbox_mode="<read-only|workspace-write>" [-c sandbox_workspace_write.network_access=true]` — **no `-s`, no `-C`**; cwd is set by the spawner (§5.2, T6). Never `-p`, `--skip-git-repo-check`, `--last`, `--dangerously-*`, `codex queue`. `-o`/schema files live in the app-owned run dir, are read, redacted, validated, then deleted (M12). Session ref from the first event; interrupted sessions marked non-resumable. `-c` values are separate argv elements. Strict schemas: all properties required, optional fields nullable (§5.2, Q11 in 6.2-T0).

### 6.1b-05 — Codex budgets: command cap and per-story uncached-token admission (R2, X, R10) [Codex] · Review: Claude-sec

**Spec:** §6.1 "Codex budget"; R6, S16, X5 (worst case recounted). Replaces the rev-2 token watchdog (A4) — there is **no** mid-run token kill.

**Files:** Create `apps/orchestrator/src/agents/runtimes/cli-budget.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/test/cli-budget.test.ts`. Modify `apps/orchestrator/src/agents/runtimes/codex.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/src/agents/role-run.ts`.

- `createCommandCounter({ cap: budgets.commandsPerRun, onTrip })` consumes `command` events; above the cap → `interrupt()` + failure `budget` (→ `model_limit`), no retry.
- `storyCliTokens(storyId)` = Σ over the story's Codex runs of `input − cached_input + output` (from `model_usage`). `assertCodexAdmission(storyId)` runs **before every Codex run** (coder, QA 2a, QA 2b, rework, Codex chat counts too — M32) and refuses when the total is over `budgets.cliTokensPerStory` → story blocks `model_limit` (gate runs: through the fenced runtime-failure path, row `error` not counted — 6.2-19). Usage is read once, at the end of each run.
- The existing `budgets.*` OpenCode fields stay unenforced (S16).

- [ ] **RED**: `many-commands` scenario with N = cap + 1 → exactly one trip, run interrupted, failure `budget`, no retry; N = cap → no trip; admission: total just under cap → allowed, at cap + 1 → refused before spawn (no process started); raw input tokens with a large cached share do **not** trip the cap (uncached formula); a run reporting no usage adds 0 and sets the "usage not reported" flag.

### 6.1b-12 — Story-closure hook: one DONE/CANCELLED cleanup path for local merge, GitHub merge and cancel (plan review F1) [Codex] · Review: Claude-sec (state machine)

**Spec:** §5.4 "Accepted residue" (purge when DONE or CANCELLED), §5.7.1 step 4 (QA worktree removed with the story's other worktrees on DONE/CANCELLED), §5.8.1 (`<key>.chat` removed on DONE/CANCELLED; DONE cleanup fails queued chat turns first), §9 call 18.

**Why:** the plan previously hooked purge and worktree removal into "the existing DONE/CANCELLED cleanup path". It does not exist (Reality check #18): only `git/merger.ts:322` removes the story worktree after a **local** merge; GitHub merge (`github/merge-service.ts` :283, :394) and cancel (`machine.ts:252-257`, `stories/service.ts:435`) remove nothing, and the `cleanup` job kind is never enqueued.

**Files:** Create `apps/orchestrator/src/stories/closure.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/test/story-closure.test.ts`. Modify `apps/orchestrator/src/workflow/service.ts` (new optional dep `WorkflowServiceDeps.queue?: JobQueue`, stored as `this.queue`; enqueue in `transitionIn`), `apps/orchestrator/src/jobs/queue.ts` (`enqueueIn(tx, job)` — the existing insert logic, callable inside a caller-owned transaction), `apps/orchestrator/src/agents/runner.ts` (dispatch kind `cleanup` — a dispatch-table entry added only when the required `closure` dep is passed; `AgentRunnerDeps.closure?` stays optional so existing test constructions are untouched, and a runner built without it fails a `cleanup` job as `unwired_job_kind`, never completes it), `apps/orchestrator/src/app.ts` (**reorder:** `const queue = new JobQueue(db)` moves above `new WorkflowService({…})` — today `app.ts:113` builds the workflow before `app.ts:119` builds the queue — and `queue` is passed as the workflow's new dep; construct `StoryClosure` with **every** dep its later steps need, pass it to the runner and the merger, `wiring.closure` added to the seam), `apps/orchestrator/src/git/merger.ts` (line 322's removal replaced by `await closure.run(storyId)`), `apps/orchestrator/test/wiring/job-kinds.ts` (`cleanup` moves from `NEVER_ENQUEUED` to enqueued — the scan now finds `this.queue.enqueueIn(tx, { kind: 'cleanup', … })` in `workflow/service.ts`, a `this.queue` receiver that 6.1a-21's `JobQueue` rule already recognises — and `app-wiring.test.ts` therefore requires it in `wiredJobKinds()`), `apps/orchestrator/test/app-wiring.test.ts` (closure-step block), create `apps/orchestrator/test/wiring/closure-steps.ts`.

**Queue access (round 6 m2, verified: `WorkflowService` holds only `db`, `bus`, `now`, `redact`, `beforeCommit`, `onRunInterrupt` — `service.ts:59-66` — and `app.ts:113` constructs it before `JobQueue` at `app.ts:119`).** `WorkflowServiceDeps` gains `queue?: JobQueue`; `transitionIn` enqueues only `if (this.queue)`, written as `this.queue.enqueueIn(tx, { kind: 'cleanup', … })` behind that guard (the scan also accepts `?.`). Optional, so the many existing `new WorkflowService({ db, bus })` test constructions stay untouched and enqueue nothing (their job-count assertions, e.g. `run-interruption.test.ts:239`, `workflow-service.test.ts:165`, are unaffected); `app.ts` — reordered so the queue exists first (`JobQueue` needs only `db`) — always passes it, and `app-wiring.test.ts` proves that (below). Chosen over a post-commit callback (lost on crash) and over passing the queue per call (every transition caller would change).

**Closure deps and wiring (round-5 m8).** `new StoryClosure(deps)` in `app.ts` receives, in this task, everything the later steps use, so 6.1b-09, 6.2-07, 6.2-11 and 6.3-13 only edit `stories/closure.ts` and never `app.ts`: `{ db, worktreeManager, interrupts, redact, logger, now, env: opts.env ?? process.env, dataDir, resolveBinaries: { claude: () => resolveClaudeBinary(env), codex: () => resolveCodexBinary(env) } (6.1a-09/6.1b-01), spawn (spawnStreaming), agentEnv (agentEnvironment) }`. Steps are registered **inside** `StoryClosure`'s constructor from `closure.ts`'s own step table, never by callers, and `stepNames(): readonly string[]` exposes the registered names in order. `wiring/closure-steps.ts` exports `EXPECTED_STEPS` (the full final order: `story-worktree`, `review-worktree`, `session-purge`, `qa-worktree`, `security-worktree`, `chat-fail-turns`, `chat-worktree`, `chat-sessions`) and `PENDING` (`session-purge` → 6.1b-09, `qa-worktree` → 6.2-07, `security-worktree` → 6.2-11, the three chat steps → 6.3-13); each of those tasks removes its own entry in the same commit, under the same stale-entry rule as job kinds.

**Design — one hook, two triggers, idempotent:**
- **Durable trigger:** `WorkflowService.transitionIn` — the single commit path for system and human transitions — inserts, **in the same transaction and after `applyEffects(...)` (`service.ts:116`)**, a `cleanup` job (slot `heavy`, repo lane, `sideEffecting: false`, idempotency `cleanup_<storyId>_<DONE|CANCELLED>`, **`storyId: NULL`**, payload `{storyId, reason}`) whenever a transition enters `DONE` or `CANCELLED` (T10, T17; keyed on `result.to`, so any future path into those states is covered). No machine change; no post-commit callback that a crash could lose. **Why after `applyEffects` and why `storyId: NULL` (plan review m2, verified):** T17 carries `cancel_jobs`, whose executor (`service.ts:224-230`) cancels every queued/leased job **with that `story_id`** — inserted before it, the cleanup job would cancel itself; inserted after it with the story's id, it would appear in the story's job list and break `run-interruption.test.ts:239` (`rows` has length 2) and `workflow-service.test.ts:165` (`['cancelled','cancelled']`). With `storyId: NULL` it is invisible to every story-scoped job query (`hasActiveJob`, `cancel_jobs`, story job lists), so both assertions stay unchanged and no later `cancel_jobs` can cancel it. The handler reads `payload.storyId`; a missing story (project purged meanwhile) → job completes as a no-op.
- **Executor:** `StoryClosure.run(storyId): Promise<{ warnings: string[] }>` runs an ordered list of **closure steps** (`registerStep(name, fn)` at construction; each step is idempotent, catches and logs its own error — redacted — and never throws past the hook). Step 1 (this task): remove the story worktree with `removeStoryWorktree` (never forced; dirty → kept, warning "story worktree kept: <reason>" as a story activity line) — **first waiting until no interrupt handle is registered for the story** (`interrupts.active(storyId) === undefined`, polled every 500 ms for up to 60 s): T17's interrupt fires post-commit, and a CLI still exiting holds files in the worktree (Windows locks). Still registered after 60 s → the step removes nothing and reports `retry`; the runner then `queue.fail(job, …, { retry: true })` so the job runs again after back-off (idempotent; `maxAttempts` 3, after which the worktree is kept with the warning "story worktree kept: a run was still stopping"). Step 2: remove a leftover `<key>.review` worktree (same wait). The story's status is never changed by a step.
- **Callers:** the runner's `cleanup` job handler calls `closure.run()`. The **local merger** additionally `await`s `closure.run()` right after its T10 transition (where line 322 used to remove the worktree) and returns the step-1 warning as today — so `merger.test.ts:404` (`result.warning` "story worktree kept: dirty") keeps its assertion unchanged; the queued job then finds nothing to do. GitHub merge and cancel need **no** code change: their T10/T17 go through `transitionIn` and get the job.
- **Extension points (later tasks add steps, in this order):** 6.1b-09 session purge · 6.2-07 QA worktree · 6.2-11 `<key>.security` worktree · 6.3-13 fail queued chat turns ("story closed") → remove `<key>.chat` → purge chat sessions. A step may not delete the story branch.
- CANCELLED now removes a clean story worktree too (spec §5.7.1 "the story's other worktrees on DONE/CANCELLED"); the branch and any dirty worktree are kept (M35).

- [ ] **RED** `story-closure.test.ts` (temp repos via `makeTempRepo`, real `WorkflowService` built with the real `JobQueue` as its `queue` dep):
  - **local merge path:** `approveAndMergeStory` → DONE → story worktree gone, `cleanup` job enqueued in the T10 transaction and, when run, a no-op (second `run()` changes nothing);
  - **GitHub merge path:** `GitHubMergeService` T10 (both the app-driven merge at :283 and the external-merge detection at :394, fake GitHub client) → exactly one `cleanup` job; running it removes the story and review worktrees;
  - **cancel path:** `StoryService.transition({event:'cancel'})` (human path, `transitionIn`) → one `cleanup` job; running it removes a clean story worktree and keeps the branch; a dirty worktree is kept with the warning activity line;
  - the job is inserted atomically: a `beforeCommit` hook that throws → no job, no transition; idempotency key → a duplicate DONE event enqueues nothing new;
  - **cancel with queued + leased story jobs (m2):** after `cancel`, the story's own jobs are all `cancelled` and the `cleanup` job is `queued` with `story_id NULL` and `payload.storyId` = the story (it was inserted after `cancel_jobs` ran, so it is not cancelled); a story-scoped job query returns only the story's own jobs;
  - **worktree removal waits for the stop (m2):** cancel while a fake run's interrupt handle is registered → step 1 does not remove the worktree until `unregister()`; a handle that stays registered past 60 s (fake clock) → the job is failed with `retry: true`, the worktree still exists, and the re-run after unregister removes it;
  - a step that throws is logged (redacted) and later steps still run; `run()` never changes `status`/`statusVersion`;
  - restart with a queued/leased `cleanup` job → swept and re-run safely (`sideEffecting: false`).
- [ ] **RED** `app-wiring.test.ts` closure-step block (round-5 m8): `wiring.closure.stepNames()` equals `EXPECTED_STEPS` minus `PENDING`, in order; every `PENDING` name is absent (stale entry fails); `wiring.closure` is the same instance the runner's `cleanup` handler and the local merger hold (identity check through the seam); **the app's `WorkflowService` has the queue (round 6 m2):** create a story through the running app's HTTP API and cancel it → exactly one `cleanup` job (`story_id` NULL, `payload.storyId` = the story) exists in the app's DB — a build that forgot to pass `queue` fails here, not silently in production.
- [ ] **RED** `story-closure.test.ts` (addition): a `WorkflowService` constructed **without** `queue` reaches DONE/CANCELLED and enqueues nothing (the optional-dep path existing tests rely on).
- [ ] Re-run `npx vitest run apps/orchestrator/test/merger.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/github-merge.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/stories.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/run-interruption.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/workflow-service.test.ts` → existing assertions unchanged and green (in particular `run-interruption.test.ts:239` and `workflow-service.test.ts:165`, which count the story's jobs). **Window guard (round-3 minor 2):** 6.2-03 (W7) creates `apps/orchestrator/test/review-window.integration.test.ts` before this task (W8) lands, so this commit must also run it → PASS, unchanged, and record it in the window guard log (this task edits `runner.ts`, `workflow/service.ts` and `app.ts`, all on the window path).

### 6.1b-09 — Session purge on DONE/CANCELLED (R10) [Codex] · Review: Claude-sec

**Spec:** §5.4 "Accepted residue" (purge rules); R4, R16 (session purge), T5 (`codex delete --force`), T11 (no `claude purge` on the coder worktree), S3 (purge targets scratch folders).

**Files:** Create `apps/orchestrator/src/agents/session-purge.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/test/session-purge.test.ts`. Modify `apps/orchestrator/src/stories/closure.ts` (register `purgeStorySessions` as the `session-purge` closure step — 6.1b-12's hook is the only DONE/CANCELLED path; no second cleanup path is added; the step uses only the deps `StoryClosure` already holds since 6.1b-12 — `resolveBinaries`, `spawn`, `agentEnv`, `env`, `dataDir`, `db`, `redact` — so `app.ts` is not touched), `apps/orchestrator/test/wiring/closure-steps.ts` (remove `session-purge` from `PENDING`).

`purgeStorySessions(storyId)`: for every scratch cwd the story used that still exists (each role run's `<dataDir>/runs/<runId>/cwd`; each chat session's `<dataDir>/chat/<id>/cwd` from 6.3), run `claude purge <cwd> -y` then delete the folder; for every stored Codex session id (`agent_runs.session_ref` where `runtime='codex'`, plus Codex chat sessions), run `codex delete --force <uuid>` (with `--disable daemon_auto_start`; without `--force` it would prompt and stdin is closed). **Never** `claude purge` the coder's story worktree or `project.repoPath` (the user may have their own interactive session there). Failures are logged (redacted) and do not block the cleanup. Idempotent.

- [ ] **RED** (fake shims record calls; `claude purge` and `codex delete` are answered by the shim's `purge`/`delete` probe defaults, and the failing case overrides `codex: { probes: { delete: 'crash' } }`): DONE story with two role-run scratch cwds and two Codex sessions → two `purge <cwd> -y` and two `delete --force <uuid>` calls, folders gone; no call ever targets the story worktree or repoPath; a failing `codex delete` → logged, the rest continues, cleanup completes; running it twice → no extra calls; CANCELLED behaves the same; **through the hook:** local merge, GitHub merge and cancel each end with the purge calls recorded (the three 6.1b-12 paths re-run with the purge step registered); Claude **implementer** sessions (6.1b-11, persisted in the story worktree's `~/.claude/projects` slug) are never purged — accepted residue per T11.

## Sub-phase 6.2 — Gates (TEAM-6, TEAM-7, gate lifecycle §5.7.0, QA worktree, memory guard, acknowledgement before merge)

Exit (spec §7): QA and Security gates block/approve in order and drive rework; UI QA works on a scratch repo with Playwright; the §5.7.0 invariants hold under the 6.2-17 property suite; legacy projects (only `code_review` frozen) behave exactly as today.

**Gate engine order (critical path):** 6.2-01 contracts → 6.2-02 schema → 6.2-03 machine → 6.2-04 snapshot → 6.2-18 row effects → 6.2-19 claim/fence/handler → 6.2-05 discovery + restart → 6.2-24 QA + Security agents wired into the app (after 6.2-10, 6.2-11, 6.2-20) → **6.2-22 engine on** → 6.2-17 invariants. 6.2-16 (upgrade migration) slots in after 6.2-18; 6.2-21 (test helpers + arrange steps) after 6.2-04. Everything in §5.7.0's table is owned by exactly one of these tasks (see traceability).

**Keeping review → merge working across the ~10 engine commits (plan review F2, round-2 MAJOR-1).** From 6.2-03 until **6.2-22** the machine, the snapshot, the row effects, discovery and the gate-path reviewer land one commit at a time, while the **running app** keeps reviewing the old way: legacy `discoverReview` enqueues `review` jobs, and the reviewer writes `reviews` and fires `review_approved` / `review_changes_requested`. Four temporary rules keep every intermediate commit green with **no assertion edits** and keep the running app moving stories out of READY_FOR_REVIEW; all four are removed (or activated) by one named task, **6.2-22**:
1. **Compatibility rule (machine, 6.2-03):** when `evidence.gates === null`, `gate_completed{code_review}` / `review_approved` / `review_changes_requested` and T16 → AWAITING_MERGE are judged from `evidence.review` exactly as today (`candidateApproved`, the old T7/T8 guard semantics, today's — empty — effects). With `gates !== null` the frozen-set rules apply.
2. **`gateEngine` switch (orchestrator, default `false`):** a constructor option of `WorkflowService` (exposed read-only as `workflow.gateEngine`), passed by it to `loadSnapshot` (6.2-04). **The runner has no switch of its own** (round-3 minor 5): it reads `this.workflow.gateEngine` at each discovery and dispatch, so the snapshot, the row effects, the reviewer and the runner can never disagree. While it is off: `loadSnapshot` returns `gates: null` whatever rows exist; the `freeze_gates`/`reopen_gate`/`close_gate_rows`/`next_gate_attempt` executors are no-ops; discovery runs the legacy `discoverReview`. All engine tests (6.2-04, 18, 19, 05, 09, 10, 11, 16, 17, 20) construct their harness with `gateEngine: true`. Without the switch, 6.2-18's `freeze_gates` on T5 would make `gates` non-null before the engine is complete, and every story would stall in READY_FOR_REVIEW.
3. **Legacy reviewer path (6.2-09):** `ReviewerAgent.reviewStory({ storyId })` keeps today's path (insert `reviews`, fire `review_approved`/`review_changes_requested`, today's failure handling) whenever `workflow.gateEngine` is off, and uses the fenced `runGate` path only when it is on. **Why (MAJOR-1, verified):** with the reviewer on `runGate` only, a `review` job from `discoverReview` (`runner.ts:344-367`) would find no `pending` gate row (freeze is a no-op while off), complete as a no-op, and the story would sit in READY_FOR_REVIEW forever — `discoverReview` skips it only while a `review` job is active, but the spent key `review_<id>_<sha>` (`queue.ts:64` `onConflictDoNothing`) means it is never re-enqueued.
4. **Migration 0005 is activated by 6.2-22, not by 6.2-16 (MAJOR-1, decision).** 6.2-16 writes and tests `0005_gate_backfill.sql` + its snapshot but does **not** add the `_journal.json` entry (drizzle's migrator only runs journaled migrations); 6.2-22 adds the entry in the engine-on commit. Chosen over "0005 skips the `review`-job cancellation while the engine is off" because (a) a SQL migration cannot see a runtime switch, so "skip while off" really means "cancel later", and a later cancel still needs its own once-only mechanism at engine-on; (b) 0005's cancellation spends the `review_<id>_<sha>` key, so run while the engine is off it stalls every in-review story (the legacy discovery can never re-enqueue); (c) 0005's **backfilled rows** also go stale while the engine is off — the legacy reviewer approves or rejects stories whose 0005 `code_review` row stays `pending`, and stories entering review in the window get no rows — whereas nothing else writes `gate_runs` while the switch is off, so running the whole migration at engine-on sees the real final state of every story and cancels every `review` job, including those the legacy runner enqueued after 6.2-16 landed. Numbering is unchanged (0005 is still the next migration after 0004; 0006/0007 follow 6.2-22).

**Window guard test (MAJOR-1).** 6.2-03 creates `apps/orchestrator/test/review-window.integration.test.ts`: temp repo (`makeTempRepo`), real DB, real `WorkflowService`, real `JobQueue`, real `AgentRunner` and a real `ReviewerAgent` with a fake OpenCode runtime (no CLI is spawned), every constructor called with its **defaults** (no `gateEngine` option — off until 6.2-22, then the only behaviour). Cases: a READY_FOR_REVIEW story with passing verification and an approved plan → `await runner.tick(); await runner.drain();` repeated until quiescent (≤ 10 iterations; quiescence and status are checked only after `drain()` — round-4 m3) → runner discovery enqueues the review work → the real reviewer runs → story **AWAITING_MERGE**, exactly one `reviews` row (APPROVED, `gate = 'code_review'` once 6.2-02's column exists — asserted only as "one APPROVED row") and no job left `queued`/`leased`; same with a CHANGES_REQUESTED review → **REWORK** — **the tick loop stops as soon as the story leaves READY_FOR_REVIEW** (round-3 minor 6: exit condition "quiescent, or status ≠ READY_FOR_REVIEW"), so the REWORK case asserts only the outcome of the review job: status REWORK, exactly one CHANGES_REQUESTED row, one implement/rework job `queued` (never claimed or run by the test) and no `review`/`gate` job left `queued`/`leased`. The "no job left queued" assertion belongs to the AWAITING_MERGE case only. The runner is constructed with defaults; its memory check is injected as `plentyOfMemory` from 6.2-06b on (the one sanctioned edit after 6.2-03, see the ownership map), so the guard never reads the real `os.freemem()`. Across 6.2-22 the same assertions then prove the engine path (lazy freeze → `gate` job → reviewer via `runGate` → AWAITING_MERGE). **It must be run and green, unchanged apart from 6.2-06b's and 6.3-04's constructor lines, in the commit of each task on the window path:** 6.2-03, 6.1b-12 (W8, after 6.2-03 in W7), 6.1b-10, 6.2-04, 6.2-21, 6.2-18, 6.2-16, 6.2-19, 6.2-05, 6.2-09, 6.2-06b, 6.2-12, 6.2-24, 6.2-22 (each task's Run step lists it). It fails if the reviewer ever goes `runGate`-only, or discovery stops enqueuing review work, while the switch is off. (Early journaling of 0005 is guarded separately: 6.2-16's `migrate-0005.test.ts` asserts that `openDatabase` does not apply 0005 yet.)

6.2-22 flips the default on, deletes the switch, the compatibility rule, `discoverReview`/`handleReviewJob` and the legacy reviewer path, and journals 0005, after 6.2-05, 6.2-09, 6.2-16, 6.2-21 and **6.2-24** have landed.

**Gate agents in the running app (round-3 MAJOR-A, verified; adjusted in round 4).** At `9df7973` the agents exist only where `app.ts` builds them (`app.ts:234-236`: planner, implementer, reviewer) and passes them to `AgentRunner` (`app.ts:246-258`; `AgentRunnerDeps` at `runner.ts:31` has `reviewer` and no QA/security slot). From **6.1a-12** on, `app.ts` builds the role agents through `buildAgentTeam()` (`agents/team.ts`). 6.2-10 and 6.2-11 create the QA and Security agents but touch neither `app.ts`, `team.ts` nor `runner.ts`, and 6.2-22 does not touch `app.ts` — so without a wiring task an agent-team story would freeze `[code_review, qa, security]`, pass code review, and then sit in READY_FOR_REVIEW forever with a `pending` QA row and no agent to run it. **6.2-24** owns that wiring (extends `buildAgentTeam` with `qa`/`security`, passes them from `app.ts` to the runner and dispatches them in `runner.ts`, after 6.2-10/11/20, before 6.2-22; `wiring/agents.ts` lists both as pending with owner 6.2-24 from 6.1a-12 on, 6.2-24 empties that entry, and 6.2-15's acceptance check fails if it is still there) and adds `gate-pipeline.integration.test.ts`: an agent-team READY_FOR_REVIEW story driven only by `runner.tick()` + `runner.drain()`, with the agents built by `buildAgentTeam()` and the runtimes by `buildRuntimeRegistry()` with fake CLI binaries (6.1a-05's role-keyed map, so one fake Claude answers both the reviewer and security calls) — the same two factories `app.ts` uses — reaches AWAITING_MERGE via code_review → qa → security. It must be green at 6.2-24, at 6.2-22 and in every later commit listed in 6.2-24. The alternative (one integration commit for 6.2-03…6.2-09) was rejected: it would put ~7 Claude-sec reviews into one diff.

### 6.2-03 — Workflow machine: `gate_completed`, gate effects, recovery rules (C, R2, R10) [Codex] · Review: Claude-sec (state machine)

**Spec:** §5.7 "Workflow machine", §5.7.0 (story-effect column), §8 (§16 parent amendment); R1, S1 (closing on stop), S2 (T7 non-empty set), S7, S8, S19, T1, U1, V1, V4, V6, Y5.

Types: `Evidence.gates: { frozen: GateKind[]; results: Partial<Record<GateKind, { sha: string; status: GateStatus; errorCount: number }>> } | null` (loaded in 6.2-04); event `gate_completed { sha, gate }`; `review_approved` / `review_changes_requested` remain as **pure aliases** evaluated exactly as `gate_completed { sha, gate:'code_review' }` — the old T7/T8 guard bodies are **deleted** (S7). **Temporary compatibility rule (F2, removed in 6.2-22):** `gate_completed{code_review}` (and its aliases) with `evidence.gates === null` is evaluated by `legacyReviewOutcome(story, evidence)` — a single function holding today's T7/T8 semantics from `evidence.review` — and T16 into AWAITING_MERGE with `gates === null` keeps `candidateApproved`; `gate_completed` for `qa`/`security` with `gates === null` is denied (`gates_frozen`). The function carries a `// TEMPORARY — removed by 6.2-22` comment and has its own test block so 6.2-22 deletes both; it returns **today's effects for T7/T8, i.e. none** (`machine.ts:145-169` emit no effects) — no `close_gate_rows`, no `cancel_jobs`. **Gate effects only when there are gate rows (plan review m1, verified):** `close_gate_rows` and `cancel_jobs{kinds}` are emitted **only when `evidence.gates !== null`**; with `gates === null` every transition emits exactly today's effect list (`stop_run`/`fail`/`cancel` → `[interrupt_active_run?, { kind: 'cancel_jobs' }]` with **no** `kinds` key). This keeps `guards.test.ts:157` (`fail` effects `toEqual([interrupt_active_run, cancel_jobs])` on the `evidence()` default) and `run-interruption.test.ts:200` (the BLOCKED event's persisted `effects`) unchanged. This is not a compatibility branch — with `gates === null` there is no row to close — so 6.2-22 keeps it. `freeze_gates` on T5 is unconditional (it is what creates the rows; no existing test asserts T5's effect list). New effects: `freeze_gates { sha }`, `reopen_gate`, `close_gate_rows`, `next_gate_attempt { gate }`, and `cancel_jobs` gains `{ kinds?: JobKind[] }` (default: all story kinds **except `chat`** — the exclusion is added and asserted in **6.3-04** once the kind exists; round 6 m3 moved it there from 6.3-13, round 7 m5 corrected this pointer). Outcomes of `gate_completed` (READY_FOR_REVIEW only, `sha === candidateSha`):
- frozen set non-empty and every frozen gate `approved` at the SHA and verification passed → **T7** (→ AWAITING_MERGE);
- this gate `changes_requested` and `attemptsRemain` → **T8** (→ REWORK); no attempts → BLOCKED `retry_limit` (existing retry-limit path);
- this gate `blocked` → **T13** (→ BLOCKED `reviewer_blocked`, detail prefixed "QA gate:"/"Security gate:"/"Code review:" — M14);
- **each** of T7, T8, BLOCKED `retry_limit` and T13 reached through `gate_completed` carries `close_gate_rows` + `cancel_jobs{kinds:['gate']}` (§5.7.0: "each of these closes the other rows and cancels gate jobs");
- this gate `error` with `errorCount ≤ GATE_ERROR_RETRIES` → no status change, effect `next_gate_attempt`; above → BLOCKED `environment`;
- this gate `approved` but others pending → no status change (accepted as transition id `GATE`, status unchanged — M27).
Other transitions: **T5** gains effect `freeze_gates`; `stop_run`, `fail`, `cancel` (T17) gain `close_gate_rows`; **T16** into READY_FOR_REVIEW gains `reopen_gate` (the all-approved → T7 and the Y5 retry-limit → T8 decisions are taken by `WorkflowService` inside the same transaction in 6.2-18, because they need row data); **T16** into AWAITING_MERGE requires every frozen gate `approved` at the candidate SHA (replaces `candidateApproved`, S19). `provenanceFor(gate_completed)` = `'review_decision'`.

- [ ] **RED** `guards.test.ts`: **compatibility block** (deleted by 6.2-22) — with `gates: null` (the `evidence()` fixture default stays `null`, so every existing guard/matrix/property test runs unchanged) `review_approved` / `review_changes_requested` / T16 → AWAITING_MERGE behave exactly as before, and `gate_completed{qa}` is denied; S7 — `review_approved` with `qa` frozen and `pending` → **no T7** (denied/`GATE`); legacy (frozen `[code_review]`) `review_approved` → T7 exactly as before; `gate_completed` with an empty frozen set → never T7 (S2); stale `sha` → `gate_sha` guard; qa `changes_requested` with attempts → T8; exhausted → BLOCKED `retry_limit`; `blocked` → T13 `reviewer_blocked`; all approved → T7 — **each of these four** carries `close_gate_rows` + `cancel_jobs{kinds:['gate']}`; `error` errorCount 1 → `next_gate_attempt`, errorCount 2 → BLOCKED `environment`; T16 → AWAITING_MERGE with QA not approved → denied; with `gates !== null`, `stop_run`/`fail`/`cancel` carry `close_gate_rows` and a `cancel_jobs` that includes kind `gate`; with `gates === null` they carry exactly today's effects (the existing `guards.test.ts:157` assertion, untouched, is the proof) and `legacyReviewOutcome`'s T7/T8 carry no effects.

### 6.2-18 — `WorkflowService` gate-row effects and the interrupted-gate operation (R10) [Codex] · Review: Claude-sec (state machine)

**Spec:** §5.7.0 rows "T5 freeze_gates", "Result `error` (counted)", "stop_run, fail, cancel, human_changes_requested", "T16 into READY_FOR_REVIEW", "T11 / new candidate SHA", "Interrupted-gate op", "Frozen set", "Runs per row"; §5.7 bullets "Who creates pending rows", "Rows closed by story transitions", "freeze_gates on a SHA that already has rows"; T1, U1, U3, V1, V4, V5, V6, V7, W1, W2, W7, X2, X3, Y5.

**Files:** Create `apps/orchestrator/src/workflow/gate-rows.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/test/gate-rows.test.ts`. Modify `apps/orchestrator/src/workflow/service.ts` (effect executors; nothing else may write `gate_runs`).

`gate-rows.ts` (every function takes the open `WorkflowService` transaction):
- `freezeGates(tx, storyId, sha, enabledGates)` — new `freeze_seq` = max+1 at (story, sha); per enabled gate insert `pending` at attempt = highest+1 (1 if none), `reopened_at_attempt` = previous highest (0 for attempt 1) — fresh value (X3, V7); called by T5 and by lazy freeze.
- `closeGateRows(tx, storyId)` — every `pending`/`running` row of the story → `error`, `counted = false` (U3, V6).
- `nextAttempt(tx, row)` — insert attempt+1 `pending`, copying `reopened_at_attempt` **and** `freeze_seq` from the previous highest row (V5).
- `reopenGate(tx, story)` — close live rows; then for **every** frozen gate whose highest row is not `approved`, insert attempt+1 `pending` with a fresh `reopened_at_attempt` = that gate's previous highest attempt (V1, V4); returns `'reopened' | 'all_approved' | 'retry_limit_rework'`: if every frozen gate is approved → no rows, the caller applies **T7** in the same transaction (U1); if the story was BLOCKED `retry_limit` and the **first** non-approved frozen gate's highest row is `changes_requested` → no rows, the caller applies **T8** (→ REWORK) instead (Y5).
- `interruptedGateOp(tx, { storyId, sha, gate, attempt, counted })` — acts **only if** the row at that attempt is still the highest attempt **and** `running` or `pending` (W1, X2); then row → `error` with `counted` from the caller; if the story is READY_FOR_REVIEW at that SHA and the gate's `errorCount` is within `GATE_ERROR_RETRIES` → `nextAttempt`; if counted and over the limit → `fail(environment)` (which closes other rows). Whether it acted or not, it does the `activeRunId` compare-and-set: clear only if `activeRunId` equals **any** run recorded on that row in `gate_run_agent_runs` (W7).
- `recordRun(tx, gateRowId, agentRunId)` — every agent run a row starts (QA 2a and 2b).
- Effect wiring in `service.ts`: `freeze_gates` (T5), `close_gate_rows` (stop_run, fail, cancel, T8, T13, and `human_changes_requested` from 6.3-05), `next_gate_attempt`, `reopen_gate` (T16 into READY_FOR_REVIEW, with the two follow-on outcomes above). All four executors are no-ops while `gateEngine` is off (temporary, removed in 6.2-22).
- **`cancel_jobs` `kinds` filter (plan review m3, verified):** today's executor (`service.ts:224-230`) cancels **every** queued/leased job of the story and ignores any payload. It gains `AND kind IN (:kinds)` when the effect carries `kinds`; without `kinds` it behaves exactly as today (the "except `chat`" default is added by **6.3-04** once the kind exists — round 6 m3; pointer corrected in round 7 m5). Not gated by the switch (the machine only emits `kinds` when `gates !== null`, 6.2-03). Without this filter T8's `cancel_jobs{kinds:['gate']}` would also cancel the story's other queued work.
- **Y5 with no attempts left (spec §5.7.0 T16 row):** when `reopenGate` would return `'retry_limit_rework'` but `attemptsRemain` fails (attemptCount + the recover's `extraAttempts` ≥ maxAttempts), the service **denies the recover** — `GUARD_FAILED` with guard id `attemptsRemain` — before writing anything: the story row, `statusVersion` and every gate row are unchanged, the story stays BLOCKED `retry_limit`, no job is enqueued.

- [ ] **RED** `gate-rows.test.ts` (temp DB, real `WorkflowService` with `gateEngine: true`; one case with the default `false` asserts T5 writes no row): T5 freezes one `pending` row per enabled gate with a new `freeze_seq`; freeze on a SHA with existing rows → attempts continue at highest+1, fresh `reopened_at_attempt`, never reuses an old approval (V7); `stop_run` closes `pending` and `running` rows uncounted; **"recover with all frozen gates approved reaches AWAITING_MERGE"** (spec machine test, U1); recover after code review approved and QA closed → only QA (and security) reopened, code review stays approved (V1); recover of a `retry_limit` story whose first non-approved gate is `changes_requested` → REWORK, no new rows (Y5); **the same recover with no attempts left** (no `extraAttempts`) → `GUARD_FAILED` `attemptsRemain`, story still BLOCKED `retry_limit`, `statusVersion` and all `gate_runs` rows unchanged, no job enqueued; with `extraAttempts: 1` → REWORK (Y5, spec §5.7.0); counted `error` under the limit → `next` copies `reopened_at_attempt` and `freeze_seq`; interrupted-gate op on a row that is no longer the highest (a fast recover inserted attempt+1) → changes no row, still CAS-clears `activeRunId` (W1); on an `approved` row (cleanup failed after approval) → row stays `approved` (W1); on a `pending` row with `counted=true` → `error` counted + Next (X2); counted and over the limit → BLOCKED `environment`; story no longer in review → no Next (V6); `activeRunId` set to a different run → not cleared; **closing on gate-driven transitions (V1, V6):** after a recover leaves QA and security both `pending` with queued jobs, a QA `changes_requested` → T8 → security's `pending` row is `error` uncounted and its queued job `cancelled` in the real DB (same for T13 and BLOCKED `retry_limit`); `stop_run`/`fail`/`cancel` → every `pending`/`running` row closed and every queued gate job cancelled; **error-count reset (S8, T1, X3):** one counted error, then stop + recover, then one more counted error → the gate retries (Next), not BLOCKED `environment`; **Y5 negative:** a story BLOCKED for a reason other than `retry_limit` whose first non-approved gate is `changes_requested` → reopens (new `pending` row), not REWORK; **`kinds` filter (m3):** a story in READY_FOR_REVIEW with a queued `gate` job **and** a queued non-gate job (e.g. a `merge` or `verify` job inserted directly) → QA `changes_requested` → T8 → the `gate` job is `cancelled`, the non-gate job is still `queued` with its lease fields untouched; a `cancel_jobs` without `kinds` (stop_run) still cancels both.

### 6.2-19 — Gate handler: claim transaction, fenced results, `activeRunId` CAS, row-keyed stops (R10) [Codex] · Review: Claude-sec (state machine)

**Spec:** §5.7.0 rows "Gate claim", "Memory wait inside a claimed gate", "Result approved/changes_requested/blocked", "Runtime quota/auth/budget", "Late result, fence fails", "Handler exits without result"; §5.7 "Stale results and fencing", "Gate claim"; §3.1 rule 4 (in-place wait); S1, S8, T3, U1, V2, W4, W6, X1, X2, X7, Y1, Y2, Y3, Y4.

**Files:** Create `apps/orchestrator/src/agents/gate-handler.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/test/gate-handler.test.ts`. Modify `apps/orchestrator/src/agents/interrupts.ts` (keyed stops), `apps/orchestrator/src/workflow/service.ts` (fenced transaction helpers `claimGate`, `writeGateResult`, `writeGateRuntimeFailure`), `apps/orchestrator/test/interrupts.test.ts`.

`runGate(job, { gate, execute })` — the one path every gate kind (code review 6.2-09, QA 6.2-10, security 6.2-11) runs through. **Job context (for m4):** `gate-handler.ts` also exports `withGateJob(job, fn)` / `currentGateJob()` (an `AsyncLocalStorage<LeasedJob>`): 6.2-05's `gate` dispatch wraps the gate agent's entry call in `withGateJob`, so the code reviewer keeps its `reviewStory({ storyId })` signature and calls `runGate(currentGateJob(), …)` itself. With **no** job context (a direct call — tests, never the runner) `runGate` runs with a null lease: no heartbeat to renew or lose, and the pre-claim memory check is skipped (it has nothing to `defer()`); every fence still applies. **Also exported (round-3 MAJOR-A):** `interface GateAgent { runGateJob(input: { storyId: number }): Promise<GateJobResult> }` — the entry point of the QA (6.2-10) and Security (6.2-11) agents, which, like the reviewer, locate their own highest-attempt `pending` row and call `runGate(currentGateJob(), …)`; `GateJobResult = { ok: true; status: GateStatus } | { ok: true; skipped: string } | { ok: false; reason: string }`. **Return contract (round-3 minor 1):** `runGate` resolves `{ ok: true, status }` only when its fenced result transaction committed; runtime failure (`writeGateRuntimeFailure`, incl. `invalid_agent_output`), a failed claim or result fence, a `defer()` exit and an exception exit all resolve `{ ok: false, reason }` — the gate path never reports `ok: true` for a run that produced no committed result (`reviewer.test.ts:390` asserts `result.ok === false` for the invalid-output case). **Run hooks for every run in a gate (round-3 minor 1, spec W7):** `gateRunHooks(ctx)` returns `{ beforeRetry, onRunCreated: (id) => recordRun(tx, gateRowId, id) }`; **every** `RoleRunService.run` call made inside `execute` passes it — the claim's run (which also passes `preCreatedRunId`) and each additional call: the reviewer's invalid-output retry (the `for (attempt = 1; attempt <= 2 …)` loop at `reviewer.ts:199`), QA 2b, and any future one. Only the first passes `preCreatedRunId`; the others get their run row from the service and are recorded on the gate row by `onRunCreated` before they spawn (6.1a-11's extended hook). An `execute` that calls `RoleRunService.run` without the hooks is a review-blocking defect.
1. **Memory guard first** (6.2-06a): below the floor **before** the claim → `queue.defer()`; a defer exit changes **no row** (Y1).
2. **Claim** — one `WorkflowService` transaction fenced on: story READY_FOR_REVIEW, `candidateSha` = row SHA, row is the **highest** attempt and still `pending` (W4). Inside it: route check via `checkRoute()` (6.1a-11) — gate's current route allowed (QA/security: `claude-code`/`codex` only) and `allowPaid` for Claude/Codex (X7, W6); failure → `fail(permission_denied)` with hint "gate frozen for this candidate: re-enable an allowed runtime for this role or recover to READY"; else row → `running`, agent run created and recorded (`recordRun`), `activeRunId` CAS `NULL → run`. This claim-created run is the **only** first-attempt run: `execute` hands its id to `RoleRunService.run({…, hooks: { preCreatedRunId, beforeRetry, onRunCreated }})` (6.1a-11), so the role-run service never inserts a second row for it (F5). **Fence fails → job completes, nothing else** (no row change, Y1). **Only the CAS fails** → `queue.defer(30 s)` (X2) with its **own** `claimDeferredSince` payload timestamp (separate from the memory guard's); after 10 min of claim deferral: if the holding run is not `running`, clear the stale `activeRunId`, run the interrupted-gate op (not counted), log a warning (Y4).
3. **Execute** with a stop flag keyed by **this gate row id / run id** (Y2): `RunInterruptRegistry` gains `requestStop(storyId)` → marks the key of the currently registered gate row; a handler polls `isStopRequested(key)`; the flag stays until **that** handler exits; a new handler (other row) never inherits a flag set for an earlier row or while nothing was registered. Internal waits (memory before 2b / dev server / internal retry) use `waitForMemoryInPlace` — never `defer()` — renewing the lease; they end **at once, writing nothing**, on stop, lost lease or failed fence (Y3); after 30 min → `writeGateRuntimeFailure(environment)` (row `error` not counted + `fail(environment, "not enough free memory")`, X1). Before each internal retry (e.g. invalid-output) the fence is re-checked; failed → stop without writing. **Crash retry inside `RoleRunService` (F5):** the handler's `beforeRetry()` returns `false` when the row-keyed stop is set or the fence (READY_FOR_REVIEW at SHA, row highest and `running`, lease held) fails — the service then returns the crashed result without spawning; `onRunCreated(runId)` records the retry run on the gate row with `recordRun` in a `WorkflowService` transaction. `beforeRetry`, the run-row insert and `onRunCreated` are synchronous and run back-to-back with no `await` between them (better-sqlite3 is synchronous), so the fence checked in `beforeRetry` still holds when the run is recorded and spawned. `activeRunId` stays the claim's run; its CAS-clear matches any run recorded on the row (W7).
4. **Result** — `writeGateResult` in one transaction fenced on: READY_FOR_REVIEW at SHA, row highest and still `running` → row status = result, result FK, then `gate_completed` evaluated in the same transaction, `activeRunId` CAS `run → NULL`. **Fence fails** → result payload (result FK, findings, agent run) stored on its own row, **status unchanged** (stays `error`, `counted=false`), nothing else — never `fail`, never `reviewer_blocked` (U1, T3).
5. Runtime `quota`/`auth`/`budget` → `writeGateRuntimeFailure(reason)`: row `error` not counted + `fail(reason)` (fenced; stale → ignored). The helper takes any `BlockedReason`; 6.2-09 also routes "invalid output after the retry" through it as `invalid_agent_output` (m4).
6. **Exit without a result because of an exception or a lost lease** → `interruptedGateOp(counted: true)` (V2, X2). A `defer()` exit or a failed claim fence is **not** such an exit (Y1). How steps 3 and 6 combine (both spec rules hold): an in-place memory wait that ends early **writes no memory-timeout result** (Y3); the handler then exits through the normal path — after a Stop or a failed fence the row is already closed or no longer the highest live row, so `interruptedGateOp` changes nothing but the CAS; after a **lost lease** the row is still `running`, so it becomes a counted `error` with a Next (table row "Handler exits without result").

- [ ] **RED** `gate-handler.test.ts` (fake runtime, temp DB, injected clock/memory): claim on a non-highest or non-`pending` row → job completes, no change; route check on **every** gate (X7): QA routed to opencode, security routed to opencode, and code review / QA / security on a Claude or Codex route with `allowPaid:false` → each BLOCKED `permission_denied` with the hint (W6); a legacy code-review gate on OpenCode with `allowPaid:false` → claims normally; after a fenced result `activeRunId` is NULL, and a result whose run is not the recorded `activeRunId` leaves it set (CAS); exception **before** the claim commits → the still-`pending` row becomes a counted `error` with a new attempt (X2); lost lease during execution → counted `error` + Next; lost lease during an in-place memory wait → no memory-timeout result written, then counted `error` + Next; CAS conflict → deferred 30 s, attempts unchanged, row still `pending` (X2); CAS conflict for 10 min with a dead holder → stale `activeRunId` cleared, row errored uncounted, Next inserted, warning logged (Y4); low memory before claim → deferred, **no row change** (Y1); low memory inside the gate → row stays `running`, lease renewed, Stop → returns within one poll writing nothing (Y3), 30 min → row `error` uncounted + BLOCKED `environment` (X1); **late `approved` after Stop → payload stored, row stays `error`, story unchanged, no `reviewer_blocked`** (U1, S1, T3); result after the story moved to REWORK → no `fail`; stop requested for row A, handler for row B starts → B runs normally (Y2); stop requested between internal retries → second attempt never starts (Y2); exception after claim → counted error + Next; exception after writing `approved` (cleanup failure) → row stays `approved` (W1); quota → row `error` uncounted + BLOCKED `quota`. **Run bookkeeping (F5):** a claimed gate whose run succeeds has exactly **one** `agent_runs` row (the claim's) and one `gate_run_agent_runs` link — no double run creation; **crash then Stop** (fake `crash` scenario, Stop requested before the retry) → `beforeRetry` false → **no second spawn** (shim record has one entry), row closed by the stop, no second run row; crash with no stop → **the retry run is recorded on the gate row** (two `gate_run_agent_runs` links, the second inserted before the second spawn) and a fenced result after it clears `activeRunId` (CAS matches the first run, which is still on the row); crash after the story moved to REWORK → `beforeRetry` false, nothing written; `withGateJob(job, …)` → `currentGateJob()` returns that job inside and `undefined` outside; `runGate` without a job context claims, executes and writes the fenced result with no lease calls; `writeGateRuntimeFailure('invalid_agent_output')` → row `error` uncounted + BLOCKED `invalid_agent_output`. **Hooks + return (round-3 minor 1):** an `execute` that makes two `RoleRunService.run` calls (the second without `preCreatedRunId`) with `gateRunHooks` → two `gate_run_agent_runs` links, the second inserted before the second spawn (shim record order), and a fenced result after it CAS-clears `activeRunId`; `runGate` resolves `ok: false` for runtime failure, invalid output, failed claim fence, failed result fence, `defer()` exit and exception, and `ok: true` only after a committed result.

- [ ] Commit: `feat(orchestrator): fenced gate claim and result transactions with row-keyed stops (TEAM-6/7, §5.7.0)`

### 6.2-05 — Gate discovery, job kind `gate`, startup recovery for gate jobs (C+X, R10) [Codex] · Review: Claude-sec

**Spec:** §5.7 "Discovery", §5.7.0 rows "Discovery enqueue", "Restart"; §5.13 "Startup sweep", "Job sweep interaction", "Fast restarts"; R1, R9, S2 (lazy freeze), S14, T1, T2, U2, U3, V1, V2.

**Files:** Create `apps/orchestrator/src/agents/gate-discovery.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/test/{gate-discovery,gate-startup}.test.ts`. Modify `apps/orchestrator/src/agents/runner.ts` (`discoverGates` when `this.workflow.gateEngine` is on — the runner gets **no** `gateEngine` dep of its own (round-3 minor 5) — the legacy `discoverReview` while it is off — 6.2-22 deletes `discoverReview`; `hasActiveJob` kinds; `gate` job dispatch by `payload.gate`, each wrapped in 6.2-19's `withGateJob(job, …)` — `code_review` → `reviewer.reviewStory({ storyId })` with exactly that argument (m4; `runner.test.ts:594` asserts the call shape), `qa`/`security` → the **no-agent path** until **6.2-24** adds their `AgentRunnerDeps` slots and dispatch (round-3 MAJOR-A; the engine is off in the app until 6.2-22, which depends on 6.2-24, so no `qa`/`security` job exists in the running app before then). A gate job whose kind has no handler is **never completed silently**: the runner logs an error "no <gate> agent configured", and the handler exits without a result → `interruptedGateOp(counted: true)` → retried once, then BLOCKED `environment` — a visible failure, not a stall; the legacy `review` job keeps `handleReviewJob` while the switch is off), `apps/orchestrator/src/app.ts` (startup), `apps/orchestrator/test/runner.test.ts` (new cases only; existing ones run with the default `gateEngine: false` and keep their assertions), `apps/orchestrator/test/wiring/job-kinds.ts` + `apps/orchestrator/test/app-wiring.test.ts` (Global Constraints rule: `gate` leaves `PENDING` — it is now enqueued and has a dispatch entry; new **gate-kind block**: `runner.wiredGateKinds(): ReadonlySet<GateKind>` — the gate kinds whose agent dep is present — must contain every `GATE_KINDS` value except those in `job-kinds.ts` `PENDING_GATE_KINDS = { qa: '6.2-24', security: '6.2-24' }`, which must be absent (stale entry fails)). Every tick loop in this task's tests is `await runner.tick(); await runner.drain();` (round-4 m3).

- **Discovery:** enqueue a `gate` job (slot heavy, repo lane, `sideEffecting: true`, idempotency `gate_<story>_<sha>_<gate>_<attempt>`, payload `{sha, gate, attempt, gateRunId}`) only for a **highest-attempt** row in `pending` that has no queued gate job and no `leased` gate job with an **unexpired** lease for (story, sha, gate) (a lease with `leaseExpiresAt ≤ now` counts as absent, as in `stories/service.ts`'s merge path), **and only when** the story is READY_FOR_REVIEW with `candidateSha` = the row SHA and every earlier frozen gate (`code_review → qa → security`) has its highest row `approved` (C5, V1). A READY_FOR_REVIEW story with **no** rows at its candidate SHA → lazy `freeze_gates` in a `WorkflowService` transaction (S2). Legacy projects freeze `[code_review]` and run the existing reviewer through the same gate job.
- **Startup** (after 6.1a-19's steps): the existing `fail(interrupted)` loop **skips kind `gate`** (T2); for each swept `gate` job whose row at its attempt is still `pending` → interrupted-gate op, not counted (V2); then for **every** row still `running` → interrupted-gate op, not counted, with the `activeRunId` CAS (U2). Restart errors never block the story.

### 6.2-06a — Memory guard module and attempt-neutral `queue.defer()` (R2, V, R10) [Codex] · Review: Claude-sec

**Spec:** §3.1 rule 4; R13, S9, X1, Y3. (Split from the old 6.2-06 so each half sits in one wave; 6.2-19 needs this half.)

**Files:** Create `apps/orchestrator/src/resources/memory-guard.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/test/memory-guard.test.ts`. Modify `apps/orchestrator/src/jobs/queue.ts` (`defer(jobId, leaseToken, delayMs)`), `apps/orchestrator/test/job-queue.test.ts`.

- `checkMemory({minFreeMb = Number(env.AIK_MIN_FREE_MEM_MB ?? 2500), freemem = os.freemem}): {ok, freeMb}`.
- `queue.defer(jobId, leaseToken, delayMs)` re-queues the job and sets `attempts = attempts − 1` (the claim already incremented it), so deferral never consumes attempts and never reaches `dead`.
- `memoryDeferralOutcome(kind, deferredSince, now): 'keep_waiting' | 'fail_story' | 'planning_failed' | 'chat_failed'` — pure decision (30 min); the wiring that acts on it is 6.2-06b.
- `waitForMemoryInPlace({ renewLease, shouldAbort, everyMs = 30_000, limitMs = 30 min }): Promise<'ok'|'aborted'|'timeout'>` — for code already inside a claimed gate (used by 6.2-19/6.2-08/6.2-10); `shouldAbort` covers stop, failed lease heartbeat and failed fence.

- [ ] **RED**: `queue.defer` → re-queued, `attempts` unchanged across 5 claim/defer cycles, never `dead`, wrong lease token → refused; `memoryDeferralOutcome` table (29 min → keep waiting; 30 min → per-kind outcome); `waitForMemoryInPlace` → `'aborted'` within one poll after `shouldAbort` flips, `'timeout'` at 30 min (fake clock), lease renewed each poll, `'ok'` as soon as memory recovers.
- [ ] Run `npx vitest run apps/orchestrator/test/memory-guard.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/job-queue.test.ts` → FAIL → PASS.
- [ ] Commit: `feat(orchestrator): memory guard primitives and attempt-neutral queue deferral (§3.1)`

**Depends on:** 6.1b-12 (`queue.ts` order after 6.1a-19). **Parallel with:** 6.1b-05, 09.

### 6.2-06b — Memory-guard wiring and the agent-team lane cap (R2, V, R10) [Codex] · Review: Claude-sec

**Spec:** §3.1 rules 1, 4, 5; R16 (lane cap wording), S9, S12.

**Files:** Modify `apps/orchestrator/src/agents/runner.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/src/jobs/lane.ts` (cap), `apps/orchestrator/src/resources/memory-guard.ts` (`deferForMemory`), `apps/orchestrator/test/runner.test.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/test/{run-interruption,plan-feedback}.test.ts` and `apps/orchestrator/test/review-window.integration.test.ts` (each **only** adds `memoryCheck: plentyOfMemory` to its `new AgentRunner({…})` call — arrange-only, zero `expect(` lines changed), create `apps/orchestrator/test/lane-cap.test.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/test/helpers/memory.ts`.

**Injectable memory check (round-3 minor 3).** The guard must never read the real `os.freemem()` in a test: with the 2500 MB floor a busy CI or dev machine would defer jobs at random and flake every runner-driven test. `AgentRunnerDeps` gains `memoryCheck?: () => { ok: boolean; freeMb: number }` (default `() => checkMemory()` — the real reading, used only by `app.ts`); the runner passes the same function to every guard point, to `deferForMemory`, and into the gate job context so `runGate`'s pre-claim check and `waitForMemoryInPlace` (6.2-19, 6.2-08, 6.2-10, 6.2-20) use it too. `test/helpers/memory.ts` exports `plentyOfMemory` (always `{ ok: true, freeMb: 1e6 }`), `lowMemory`, and `scriptedMemory([...])` for the low-memory cases. Every test that constructs an `AgentRunner` passes `plentyOfMemory` unless it is testing the guard (8 constructions at `9df7973`: `runner.test.ts` ×6, `run-interruption.test.ts`, `plan-feedback.test.ts`, plus the window test). Chosen over tests setting `AIK_MIN_FREE_MEM_MB=0`: an env override leaks across files in one Vitest worker and still calls the real `os.freemem()`.

- `deferForMemory(job, kind)`: stores the first deferral time (`memoryDeferredSince`) in the job payload, activity line "waiting for memory", story unchanged; after 30 min acts on `memoryDeferralOutcome`: active story (implement/rework/verify/gate-before-claim) → `fail(environment, "not enough free memory")`; plan job → `planning_failed` (P5; no failed run written — `retry_planning` recovers it); chat turn → turn `failed`, story untouched (wired in 6.3-13).
- Guard points: before a role run, a chat turn, the dev server, and QA step 2b.
- Lanes (rule 1): if **any active project** uses the agent-team preset, the **global** effective cap is `min(AIK_MAX_CONCURRENT_LANES, 2)`; otherwise unchanged. The `chat` slot is not counted (S12).

- [ ] **RED**: low memory → implement job deferred, story unchanged; 30 min (fake clock) → implement job's story BLOCKED `environment`; plan job → `planning_failed` with no failed run row; one agent-team project active → only 2 heavy jobs claimed across three repos (including a legacy repo); no agent-team project → 3; a `light`-slot job is claimable while both heavy lanes are busy (the `chat` kind itself is asserted in 6.3-13, after 6.3-02 adds it); low/ok memory in these cases comes only from `scriptedMemory`/`lowMemory` (no test reads `os.freemem()`); a runner built without `memoryCheck` uses `checkMemory` (spy on the default, asserted once in `memory-guard.test.ts` with an injected `freemem`).
- [ ] Run `npx vitest run apps/orchestrator/test/lane-cap.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/runner.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/run-interruption.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/plan-feedback.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/memory-guard.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/review-window.integration.test.ts` → FAIL → PASS (the window test's only change is the `memoryCheck: plentyOfMemory` line; its assertions are untouched). `git diff` on the four existing test files: only the constructor line changes.
- [ ] Commit: `feat(orchestrator): memory-guard deferral wiring and agent-team lane cap (§3.1)`

**Depends on:** 6.2-05 (`runner.ts` order), 6.2-06a. **Parallel with:** 6.2-12, 20.

Removal with the story's other worktrees on DONE/CANCELLED (§5.7.1 step 4): `removeQaWorktree` (+ its `workspaces` row) is registered as a **closure step** in `stories/closure.ts` (6.1b-12's hook, after the session-purge step); nothing is ever committed.
