### 6.2-09 — Code-review gate: plan alignment, grounded "no errors", fenced writes (C+X, R10) [Codex] · Review: Claude (prompt semantics) + Claude-sec (fencing)

**Spec:** §5.7 "Code review" bullet, "Stale results and fencing"; §5.2 (gate role argv); T3, S8.

**Files:** Create `apps/orchestrator/src/agents/prompts/reviewer.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/test/reviewer-alignment.test.ts`. Modify `apps/orchestrator/src/agents/reviewer.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/test/reviewer.test.ts`.

`buildReviewerPrompt({plan, acs, diff, verification, priorFindings}): string` — full approved PlanV1 (summary, subtasks with files, ACs with verification, requiredTests), every verification check with command/status/SHA and redacted output tail. Normalisation: any `planAlignment` entry not `done` → CHANGES_REQUESTED; APPROVED while verification is not `mandatoryAllPassed` → CHANGES_REQUESTED "checks not green"; missing diff/check evidence → `error`, never an approval. (The prompt and normalisation apply on both paths below.)

**Two paths until 6.2-22 (MAJOR-1).** The entry point stays **`reviewStory({ storyId })`** (m4; `runner.test.ts:594` asserts `toHaveBeenCalledWith({ storyId })`), and it branches on `workflow.gateEngine` (6.2-04):
- **Engine off (default until 6.2-22) — legacy path, kept as is:** exactly today's behaviour — insert `reviews` (the column default tags it `code_review`), fire `review_approved`/`review_changes_requested`, today's failure handling (`reviewer_blocked`, `invalid_agent_output`, …). This is what the running app executes for every `review` job from `discoverReview` during the window; the `review-window.integration.test.ts` guard proves it reaches AWAITING_MERGE / REWORK. Marked `// TEMPORARY — removed by 6.2-22`.
- **Engine on — gate path:** the reviewer **locates its own row**: the highest-attempt `code_review` row at the story's candidate SHA that is `pending`; none → returns `{ ok: true, skipped: 'no pending code_review row' }` and changes nothing (a stale or duplicate job). Otherwise `runGate(currentGateJob(), { gate: 'code_review', execute })` (6.2-19): the `reviews(gate='code_review')` insert + gate-row result + `gate_completed` happen in the fenced result transaction; the old paths that turned a denied transition into `reviewer_blocked`, and that called `fail` on error/quota regardless of the story's state, are **not used** on this path (T3). The invalid-output retry re-checks the fence first, and **both** `RoleRunService.run` calls of the `reviewer.ts:199` loop pass 6.2-19's `gateRunHooks()` (only the first also passes the claim's `preCreatedRunId`), so the retry's run is recorded on the gate row through `onRunCreated → recordRun` before it spawns (round-3 minor 1, spec W7) and the result's `activeRunId` CAS still matches. **Return value:** the gate path returns `runGate`'s result — `{ ok: false, reason }` on every runtime failure, invalid output, fence failure or exception, `{ ok: true, … }` only after a committed result (the legacy path's return values are unchanged). **Invalid output after the retry** = `writeGateRuntimeFailure('invalid_agent_output')` (row `error` uncounted + fenced `fail(invalid_agent_output)`), so the story ends BLOCKED `invalid_agent_output` exactly as `reviewer.test.ts:369` ("blocks with invalid_agent_output after two invalid reviewer outputs") asserts today — kept, not changed; fence failed → nothing written.

Absent `planAlignment` → not enforced (OpenCode reviewers unaffected). Claude argv per 6.1a-10 (gate role).

- [ ] **RED** (new cases with `gateEngine: true`; the existing `reviewer.test.ts` cases keep their assertions and, with the default switch, still run the legacy path — their arrange steps were moved by 6.2-21): prompt contains every subtask id and AC (both paths); missing subtask → CHANGES_REQUESTED with note (both paths); failing insert → neither review nor gate row; the same gate run on the **Codex** reviewer route (6.1b-10) and on the Claude route produces identical gate-row outcomes; legacy scenario still reaches AWAITING_MERGE; team scenario stays READY_FOR_REVIEW with QA `pending`; a review finishing after the story moved to REWORK → no `fail`, no `reviewer_blocked`, row unchanged; invalid output while the fence fails → no retry run; **two invalid outputs on the gate path → `result.ok === false`, BLOCKED `invalid_agent_output`, row `error` uncounted, no `reviews` row, and two `gate_run_agent_runs` links (the claim's run and the retry's, recorded through `onRunCreated`)** (the engine-on twin of `reviewer.test.ts:369`); invalid output then a valid approval → two links and a fenced result that clears `activeRunId`; quota on the gate path → `result.ok === false`, row `error` uncounted, BLOCKED `quota`; `reviewStory({ storyId })` with no `pending` row → `skipped`, nothing written; **engine off:** a `review` job → `reviews` row + `review_approved` → AWAITING_MERGE with **no** `gate_runs` row and no `runGate` call (spy).

### 6.2-10 — QA gate agent: two Codex runs (functional, then UI) merged into one result (C+X, R2, R10) [Codex] · Review: Claude-sec

**Spec:** §5.7.2, §5.7.1, §5.2 (QA argv: `--ephemeral`, `--disable hooks`, `project_doc_max_bytes=0`, base `AGENTS.md`), §6.1 (2a 15 + 2b 15 min); B6, C4, R3, R4, R16 (QA as two runs, screenshots before clean, JS-only installs), W7, X1.

**Files:** Create `apps/orchestrator/src/agents/{qa.ts,prompts/qa.ts}`, `apps/orchestrator/src/qa/evidence.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/test/{qa,qa-evidence}.test.ts`. Modify `apps/orchestrator/src/skills/bridge.ts` (role map).

Flow inside `runGate` (6.2-19): `refreshQaWorktree` (evidence of the previous run moved out in `beforeClean`) → **2a** Codex `workspace-write` run (cwd QA worktree, `outputSchema: QaV1` strict, 15 min — or half of `roles.qa.timeoutMin` when set, through 6.1a-11's timeout rule; 2b likewise —, `--ephemeral`, `--disable hooks -c project_doc_max_bytes=0`, base-branch `AGENTS.md` in the prompt): run project checks + `agents.qa.commands`, write throwaway tests per AC; if 2a has any `fail` → stop, 2b does not run → **2b** only if `gates.qaUi`, `devServerCommand` set and `playwrightAvailable`: in-place memory wait (X1) → orchestrator starts the dev server (6.2-08) → a second Codex run (15 min; network flag only per Q13 + `allowUiNetwork`) writes Playwright scripts and runs them with the repo's Playwright against the URL → dev server stopped in `finally`; otherwise UI ACs `unclear` with the reason. Each run is admitted by `assertCodexAdmission` (6.1b-05) and recorded on the gate row (W7): 2a uses the claim's run (`preCreatedRunId`), 2b and any crash retry are created by `RoleRunService` and recorded through its `onRunCreated` hook (F5) — both `run()` calls pass 6.2-19's `gateRunHooks()`; the QA agent never inserts `agent_runs` rows itself. **Entry point (round-3 MAJOR-A):** `QaAgent implements GateAgent` (6.2-19) — `runGateJob({ storyId })` locates the highest-attempt `pending` `qa` row at the candidate SHA (none → `{ ok: true, skipped }`, nothing written) and calls `runGate(currentGateJob(), { gate: 'qa', execute })`; its constructor takes the same kind of deps the reviewer does (`db`, `workflow`, `bus`, runtime registry, `worktreeManager`, `interrupts`, `redact`, `memoryCheck`). This task does **not** wire it into `app.ts`/`runner.ts` — 6.2-24 does. The flow is runtime-neutral behind a `QaRunner` interface (`runFunctional`, `runUi`) so 6.2-20 adds the Claude implementation without touching the merge/normalise/evidence code. The orchestrator merges both results into **one** `QaV1` → one `qa_results` row (M17). Post-checks: `assertWorktreeUntouched(storyWorktree, candidateSha)`; candidate ref unchanged. Normalise: any `fail` or missing AC → `changes_requested`; `unclear` never blocks (B5) but is listed; UI `pass` without Playwright → `unclear`; `depsUnavailable` → dependency-dependent ACs `unclear`.

### 6.2-11 — Security gate agent with deterministic severity rule (C+X, R2, R10) [Codex] · Review: Claude-sec

**Spec:** §5.7.3, §6.4; R3, R16 (deterministic security blocking, acknowledgement limited to medium), OQ Q9.

**Files:** Create `apps/orchestrator/src/agents/{security.ts,prompts/security.ts}`, `apps/orchestrator/test/security-gate.test.ts`. Modify `apps/orchestrator/src/git/worktree.ts` (`ensureSecurityWorktree`, `removeSecurityWorktree`), `apps/orchestrator/test/worktree.test.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/src/stories/closure.ts` (`security-worktree` removal step, closure deps only — no `app.ts` change), `apps/orchestrator/test/wiring/closure-steps.ts` (remove `security-worktree` from `PENDING`), `apps/orchestrator/test/story-closure.test.ts`.

Runs through `runGate`. **Entry point (round-3 MAJOR-A):** `SecurityAgent implements GateAgent` (6.2-19) — `runGateJob({ storyId })` locates its highest-attempt `pending` `security` row (none → `{ ok: true, skipped }`), resolves the security role's route **at claim time** and runs the Claude or the Codex path below; every `RoleRunService.run` passes `gateRunHooks()`. Same constructor deps as the QA agent. This task does **not** wire it into `app.ts`/`runner.ts` — 6.2-24 does. Its own detached worktree `<key>.security` at the candidate: `ensureReviewWorktree` is keyed by story key only (`<key>.review`, `worktree.ts:149`, and is deleted/re-created by every code-review run), so this task adds `ensureSecurityWorktree`/`removeSecurityWorktree` (`<key>.security`, read-only, `workspaces` kind `review`, same `strip` option as 6.1b-10) and registers its removal as a **closure step** (6.1b-12 hook). **Claude route:** read-only + `--restricted` + empty scratch cwd + `--add-dir <key>.security` (the Q9 scratch copy when 6.1a-T0 found `CLAUDE.md` loading), non-persistent. **Codex route (§5.5, 6.1b-10):** `-s read-only -C <key>.security --ephemeral`, `--disable hooks -c project_doc_max_bytes=0`, base `AGENTS.md` in the prompt, `SecurityV1` strict schema; inputs: diff base..candidate, plan `securitySensitive` + `dependencyChanges`, candidate verification's secret-scan and vulnerability-scan results (status + redacted tail; `unavailable` when absent; reuse `secrets/scan.ts` and `security/vulnerability.ts` results, no duplicate scanning). The orchestrator always adds one **agent-config tampering** finding at **medium** listing the changed files matching `.claude/**`, `.codex/**`, `.mcp.json`, `AGENTS.md`, `AGENTS.override.md`, `CLAUDE.md`. Verdict is **deterministic**: any `critical`/`high` finding → `changes_requested` whatever the model says; otherwise `approved`; `medium` recorded, non-blocking, must be acknowledged before merge; `low`/`info` informational only. Persist `reviews(gate='security')` + `review_findings` in the fenced result transaction. Post-check `assertWorktreeUntouched`.

### 6.2-16 — Upgrade migration 0005: freeze and clean up stories already in review or merge (R10) [Codex] · Review: Claude-sec (state machine)

**Spec:** §5.7 "Migration", §5.7.0 row "Migration"; R1, S2, T9 (AWAITING_MERGE included), U6, V3, V8, W5, X6.

**Files:** Create `packages/db/migrations/0005_gate_backfill.sql` (hand-written data migration) + `meta/0005_snapshot.json` — **no `_journal.json` entry** (6.2-22 adds it in the engine-on commit; see the sub-phase intro, rule 4) — `packages/db/test/migrate-0005.test.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/test/gate-migration.test.ts`, `packages/db/test/helpers/apply-pending-migration.ts` (test-only: applies an un-journaled migration file to a DB already migrated through 0004, splitting on `--> statement-breakpoint` like drizzle; deleted by 6.2-22), and a **duplicate** `apps/orchestrator/test/helpers/apply-pending-migration.ts` for `gate-migration.test.ts` (round-3 minor 7: an orchestrator test never imports from `packages/db/test/` — test files are not part of `@aik/db`'s `exports`, and adding a test-utils entry to a product package's `exports` for a helper that lives one task would widen its public surface; the ~20-line helper is duplicated instead, both copies carry a `// TEMPORARY — deleted by 6.2-22` comment, and 6.2-22 deletes both). Modify `packages/db/test/journal.test.ts` (6.1a-02) to allow exactly one journal-less file, `0005_gate_backfill.sql`, with a `// TEMPORARY — removed by 6.2-22` comment. (Runs once; changes no story `status`.)

**Why the journal entry waits for 6.2-22 (MAJOR-1, verified):** while `gateEngine` is off, the cancellation below would spend each in-review story's `review_<id>_<sha>` key (`queue.ts:64` `onConflictDoNothing`), so the legacy `discoverReview` (`runner.ts:344-367`) could never re-enqueue it and the story would stall in READY_FOR_REVIEW; the backfilled rows would also go stale while the legacy reviewer keeps moving stories. Journaled by 6.2-22, the migration runs once, at the first start of the build that has the engine on, against the real state of every story.

For stories in READY_FOR_REVIEW or AWAITING_MERGE, and BLOCKED stories whose `blocked_from` is one of them, at their candidate SHA, `freeze_seq = 1`, gate `code_review`, attempt 1:
- READY_FOR_REVIEW → `pending` (the gate simply runs again, V8);
- AWAITING_MERGE and BLOCKED-from-review/merge → status from the latest `reviews` row at the candidate SHA: APPROVED → `approved`, CHANGES_REQUESTED → `changes_requested`, none or other → `error` with `counted = 0` (X6, W5; `reopen_gate` re-creates `pending` on recovery).
Also: every queued or leased `review` job → `cancelled` (U6); `active_run_id = NULL` on every migrated READY_FOR_REVIEW story; any `running` reviewer `agent_runs` row of those stories → `interrupted` (V3).

### 6.2-17 — Gate lifecycle invariants I-G1…I-G4: model-based property tests (R10) [Codex] · Review: Claude-sec (state machine) — **most important tests of the phase**

**Spec:** §5.7.0 "Invariants" (I-G1, I-G2, I-G3, I-G4) and every row of the §5.7.0 table; regression scenarios behind R1, S1, S2, S7, S8, T1, T2, T3, U1, U2, U3, V1, V2, V3, V6, V7, W1, W2, W4, W6, X1, X2, X3, X7, Y1, Y2, Y3, Y4, Y5.

**Files:** Create `apps/orchestrator/test/gate-lifecycle.property.test.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/test/helpers/gate-harness.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/test/gate-lifecycle.regressions.test.ts`. No product code (bugs found here are fixed in the owning task's file by a follow-up commit on that task, with the shrunk counterexample added to the regressions file).

`gate-harness.ts`: a real temp SQLite DB + real `WorkflowService`, `gate-rows`, `runGate`, `discoverGates`, `JobQueue`, startup sequence; fake runtime with scripted per-gate results; injected clock, free-memory reading, lease heartbeat and crash points; a test-only SQLite trigger that logs every `gate_runs` status change with the writer tag (for I-G4).

Model-based test (fast-check `fc.commands`, `numRuns` 500 by default, `AIK_PROPERTY_RUNS` override, seed printed on failure) over commands: `Discover`, `ClaimNext`, `Complete(approved|changes_requested|blocked|error|quota)`, `LateResult` (a stopped handler finishes), `Stop`, `Fail(reason)`, `Cancel`, `Recover(READY|resume|extraAttempts)`, `HumanChangesRequested` (once 6.3-05 lands; skipped before), `NewCandidateSha`, `ReturnToSeenSha`, `ToggleGate(qa|security)`, `ChangeRoute(qa→opencode | allowPaid false)`, `MemoryLow/MemoryOk`, `AdvanceClock(minutes)`, `CasConflict`, `LoseLease`, `HandlerThrows(beforeClaim|afterClaim|afterResult)`, `DeferExit`, `Restart(fast|slow)`.

Assertions after every command, at quiescent points:
- **I-G1:** AWAITING_MERGE is entered only by T7 or T16, each with a non-empty frozen set whose every gate is `approved` at the candidate SHA (checked from the transition log + rows).
- **I-G2:** for a story in READY_FOR_REVIEW, either T7's guard holds, or the **first** frozen gate (code_review → qa → security) whose highest row is not `approved` has that row `pending` or `running` (and a `running` row has a live job or will be closed by the restart operation).
- **I-G3:** each (story, sha, gate) has at most one `pending` or `running` row.
- **I-G4:** every `gate_runs` status change was made by a transaction whose fence matched the story's current status and SHA, by the Close of a story transition, or by the interrupted-gate op on a row that was then the highest attempt and `pending`/`running` (writer-tag log).
- **Liveness (derived):** from any READY_FOR_REVIEW state, running discovery + handlers with all-`approved` results and plenty of memory reaches AWAITING_MERGE within a bounded number of steps (no stall), unless the story left review.
- An `approved` row's status never changes (Close acts only on `pending`/`running` rows; the interrupted-gate op skips non-live rows); a late result never changes a row's status (U1).

### 6.2-21 — Gate test helpers and arrange-step migration of existing review/merge tests (plan review F2b) [Codex] · Review: Claude-sec

Modify (**arrange steps only — never an assertion, expected value or test name**) the existing files that seed READY_FOR_REVIEW/AWAITING_MERGE (grep at `9df7973`): `apps/orchestrator/test/{reviewer,merger,merge-approval,merge-evidence,github-merge,github-poll,github-publish,github-sync,stories,runner,verifier,verification,pr-evidence,cleanroom-verification}.test.ts`. (`packages/workflow/test/{fixtures,guards,properties}.ts` belong to the machine owner: 6.2-22 updates `fixtures.ts`; e2e `appearance-board`/`appearance-column` only render statuses and need nothing.)

Helpers (arrange-only direct inserts, exempt from the "`gate_runs` only via `WorkflowService`" rule exactly as `forceStory` is exempt from the status rule; they fill every column the snapshot reads — `freeze_seq`, `counted`, `reopened_at_attempt` — so the rows match what `gate-rows.ts` would write):
- `seedFrozenGates(db, storyId, sha, { gates = ['code_review'], status = 'pending' | Partial<Record<GateKind, GateStatus>>, freezeSeq = 1 })` — one row per gate at attempt 1.
- `seedApprovedPipeline(db, storyId, sha, { gates? })` — frozen gates all `approved`, with the matching `reviews` row (`gate='code_review'`, APPROVED) and, for QA, a `qa_results` row — what an AWAITING_MERGE story looks like after 6.2-22.
- `seedGateResult(db, storyId, sha, gate, status, { attempt?, counted? })`.
- `forceAwaitingMerge(db, storyId, sha)` = `forceStory` + `seedApprovedPipeline` (the arrange step most merge tests need).

Method: temporarily run the suite with `gateEngine` defaulted on (a local, uncommitted flip), list the failing tests, and change only their **arrange** steps to call the helpers (e.g. merge tests that `forceStory(... 'AWAITING_MERGE')` + insert an approved review now also `seedApprovedPipeline`). With the engine still off in this commit the added rows are ignored by the snapshot, so every file stays green before and after. Record the list of touched tests in the execution record; a test that cannot be fixed by an arrange change is escalated (it means product behaviour changes), never rewritten.

### 6.2-25 — Retention and project purge for run logs and QA evidence (plan review r5 MAJOR-3) [Codex] · Review: Claude-sec

**Spec:** §5.7.2 "Screenshots (C4)" ("deleted with the story's evidence by the existing retention job"), §5.3 + §5.2 (run logs, schema/`-o` files and scratch cwds under `<dataDir>/runs/<runId>/`), parent spec §15.3 (project purge).

**Why (verified at `9df7973`, Reality check #25):** 6.1a-04 writes a redacted run log of up to 8 MiB per CLI run to `<dataDir>/runs/<runId>/`, and 6.2-10 moves QA screenshots to `<dataDir>/evidence/<storyKey>/<runId>/qa/`. Nothing removes either: `RetentionService.prune()` (`maintenance/retention.ts:35-107`) knows only jobs, outbox, idempotency rows, `removed` workspaces and plain files in `logsDir`; `ProjectService.delete` (`projects/service.ts:104-112`) removes worktrees and DB rows only; and the story-closure hook (6.1b-12) removes worktrees and sessions. The spec's promise ("deleted … by the existing retention job") therefore had no implementing task, and the traceability's "Unmapped: none" was wrong. Note: there is no scheduled retention **job** today — `prune()` runs only through the maintenance route (`api/routes/maintenance.ts:11`; the `retention` job kind is never enqueued). This task keeps that trigger and does not add a scheduler (recorded under "Spec errata noted").

**Files:** Create `apps/orchestrator/src/maintenance/artifacts.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/test/artifact-retention.test.ts`. Modify `apps/orchestrator/src/maintenance/retention.ts` (deps gain `runsDir?`, `evidenceDir?`; `PruneResult` gains `prunedRunDirs`, `prunedEvidenceDirs`), `apps/orchestrator/src/config.ts` (`dataPaths()` gains `runs` and `evidence`), `apps/orchestrator/src/projects/service.ts` (optional `artifactPurge` dep, called after a successful `purgeProjectData`), `apps/orchestrator/src/app.ts` (pass `runsDir: paths.runs`, `evidenceDir: paths.evidence` to `RetentionService`; pass `artifactPurge` to `ProjectService`; replace 6.2-24's `path.join(opts.dataDir, 'evidence')` with `paths.evidence`). Existing `retention-diagnostics.test.ts` and `project-delete.test.ts` stay unchanged (new cases live in the new file).

- **Age pruning (`prune()`, same `maxAgeDays`, default 30):** run folders are classified **by name** (round 6 m7): (a) a decimal name `^[1-9][0-9]*$` is an `agent_runs` id — deleted when its row is not `running` and its last update is older than the cutoff, or, when the row no longer exists (purged with its project), when the folder's mtime is older than the cutoff; a folder whose run is `running` is never touched; (b) **`probe-<uuid>`** (`^probe-[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$`, the Settings **Test** probe's run id from 6.1a-11, never an `agent_runs` id) — deleted by folder mtime only, never looked up in the DB; (c) any other name — left in place, counted as `unknownRunDirs` and logged once per prune (never deleted, so a future layout change cannot be wiped by mistake). `<evidenceDir>/<storyKey>/<runId>/` is deleted when older than the cutoff **and** its story is DONE or CANCELLED or no longer exists — evidence of an open story is kept, because the acknowledgement before merge (6.2-13/6.2-23) and the QA evidence tab (6.3-08) still need it; an emptied `<storyKey>/` folder is removed too. Every candidate's `realpath` must be inside `runsDir`/`evidenceDir`, symlinks are never followed, and a failure on one entry is counted and logged (redacted) without stopping the prune.
- **Project purge:** `collectProjectArtifacts(db, projectId)` reads the project's story keys and agent-run ids **before** `purgeProjectData` deletes the rows; after the purge commits, `removeProjectArtifacts(...)` deletes `<evidenceDir>/<storyKey>/` and `<runsDir>/<runId>/` for each (same containment rules; failures logged, never failing the delete — the DB purge has already succeeded). 6.3-13 adds the project's chat-session folders (`<dataDir>/chat/<chatSessionId>/`) to the same function.
- **Story closure is deliberately not a deletion trigger** (round-5 review suggested it as an option): a DONE story's Logs and QA-evidence tabs (6.3-08) and its stored acknowledgement must stay viewable after the merge; age pruning removes them later.
- **Readers degrade, never fail:** a pruned run log → 6.1a-14's `logUnavailable: true`; a pruned evidence file → 6.3-08's 404 "evidence expired".

- [ ] **RED** `artifact-retention.test.ts` (temp `dataDir` via `makeTempDir`, real DB, fake clock / `utimes`): a finished run's folder older than 30 days → removed and counted, a 29-day one kept; a `running` run's folder older than 30 days → kept; a `probe-<uuid>` folder older than 30 days → removed by mtime with **no** DB lookup (spy), a 29-day one kept; a numeric folder whose row was purged → removed by mtime; a folder named `notes` or `probe-x` (not a uuid) → kept and counted in `unknownRunDirs`; evidence of a DONE story older than 30 days → removed with its empty `<storyKey>/` parent, of an AWAITING_MERGE story → kept; a symlink inside `runsDir` pointing outside → the link itself is removed at most, its target untouched; `maxAgeDays: 0` from the maintenance route prunes everything eligible; project delete → the project's evidence and run folders gone, another project's untouched, the DB purge result unchanged; an undeletable file (held open) → counted as a failure, other entries still removed, the delete still returns 200; after pruning, `GET /stories/:id/agent-run` for that run returns `logUnavailable: true`.
- [ ] Run `npx vitest run apps/orchestrator/test/artifact-retention.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/retention-diagnostics.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/project-delete.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/app.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/app-wiring.test.ts` → FAIL → PASS (the two existing files unchanged).

### 6.3-T0 — Verify Q2 and Q16 (R2, R10) [Claude]

**Spec:** OPEN QUESTIONS Q2, Q16; §5.2 (stable chat cwd), §5.8.1, §5.8.2; S3, T7.

Q2 needs Codex credits; Q16 needs only Claude quota.

| Q | Exact command (spec) | Answer → what the plan does |
|---|---|---|
| Q2 | In a temp repo: `& $codex exec - --json @H -s read-only` (stdin "say ok"; persisted on purpose) → note the session id; then `& $codex exec resume <id> - --json @H -c sandbox_mode="read-only"` with stdin "create a file x.txt" → `Test-Path x.txt` must be `False`. Repeat the second command with `-c sandbox_mode="workspace-write"` from the story-worktree-like cwd (file **should** appear) for the coder-resume path. Afterwards `& $codex delete --force <id> @D` and `Get-Process codex -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue` → empty | **Read-only honoured** → Codex chat resumes its chat session as designed (6.3-04). **File created** → the override is not honoured: Codex chat never uses `resume`; each turn starts a fresh `codex exec -s read-only -C <key>.chat` with the context packet plus the redacted, budget-capped prior chat transcript (CLI-side memory lost, read-only kept). **workspace-write override not honoured** → 6.3-11 ("continue previous coder session") is dropped and the reason recorded. Also confirm resume uses the spawn cwd. |
| Q16 | Folder A, B = two empty temp folders. Spec: create a session from folder A with `--session-id <uuid> "say ok"`, then `--resume <uuid> "repeat"` from folder A and from folder B. Concretely: from A `claude -p --session-id <uuid> --tools Read,Grep,Glob --permission-mode dontAsk --permission-prompts none "say ok"`; then `claude -p --resume <uuid> --tools Read,Grep,Glob --permission-mode dontAsk --permission-prompts none "repeat"` from **A** and from **B**; afterwards `claude purge <A> -y` and `claude purge <B> -y` | **Found only from A** (design assumption) → chat keeps the **stable** `<dataDir>/chat/<chatSessionId>/cwd` for its first turn and every `--resume` (6.3-04). **Found from both** → the stable cwd stays (harmless); record. **Not found from A** → stop; Claude chat falls back to the Q2-style fresh-session-per-turn mode, recorded. |

**Depends on:** consent (Q16), Codex credits (Q2). **Parallel with:** 6.3-01…03.

### 6.3-03b — Chat service: redaction before send, persist + enqueue (R2, C, R10) [Codex] · Review: Claude-sec

**Spec:** §5.8.1, §5.8.3, §8 P4; B4, S14 (side-effecting chat jobs).

**Files:** Create `apps/orchestrator/src/chat/service.ts`, **`apps/orchestrator/src/chat/messages.ts`**, `apps/orchestrator/test/{chat-service,chat-messages}.test.ts`.

**One writer for chat rows (round-5 m6).** `chat/messages.ts` exports `appendChatMessage(tx, {storyId, author, role, runtime, runId, chatSessionId, clientMessageId?, body, turnId?})` and `setTurnStatus(tx, turnId, status, { error?, from? }): boolean` (`from` = compare-and-set on the current status — 6.3-13's handler start check uses `from: 'queued'`; returns whether a row changed, and emits only then); each writes its row **and**, in the same transaction, one outbox `chat.message` event `{messageId, storyId, turnId?}` (ids only — for a turn-status change `messageId` is the turn's user message). Every chat write in the phase goes through these two functions — user posts (here), agent replies, the post-turn "worktree reset" system message and every turn transition `queued → running → succeeded|failed|interrupted` (6.3-04), steering-action system messages (6.3-05), "story closed" / "interrupted by restart — send again" messages and turn changes (6.3-13) — so the Chat tab (6.3-09), which refetches only on `chat.message`, sees agent answers and turn states without a reload. Before this round only the user's own post emitted the event. A direct `insert(storyChatMessages)` / `update(chatTurns)` outside this module is a review-blocking defect (a grep check in `chat-messages.test.ts` over `apps/orchestrator/src/**` enforces it).

Resolution through 6.3-03a's `resolveChatRole`, frozen at acceptance. `ChatService.post(storyId, req)`: run the redactor on the body; if it matched and `sendRedacted !== true` → `needs_redaction` with pattern names, nothing stored or sent; otherwise, in one transaction: insert the **redacted** body, a `chat_turns` row `queued`, a `chat` job (slot `light`, idempotency `chat_<messageId>`, `sideEffecting: true`, no retry), outbox `chat.message`. There is no "send unredacted" path. The service has no dependency on `WorkflowService`. **Wiring note (round-4 MAJOR-B c):** this is the first source that enqueues `chat`; `chat` stays in `wiring/job-kinds.ts` `PENDING` (owner 6.3-04) because `app.ts` does not construct `ChatService` until 6.3-06, which lands after 6.3-04 has wired the handler — so no running code path can enqueue a chat job before it has a handler. This task does not touch `app.ts`.

- [ ] **RED** `chat-service.test.ts`: the accepted turn's role equals `resolveChatRole` at acceptance and does not change if the story moves on; registered secret → `needs_redaction` with pattern "GitHub token", DB and outbox untouched; resend with `sendRedacted:true` → stored body contains the redaction marker, not the secret; OpenCode-routed role → `unavailable`; job is `sideEffecting` in slot `light`; structural test: `ChatService` constructor deps exclude `WorkflowService`; a body containing "approve and merge" causes no transition. `chat-messages.test.ts`: `appendChatMessage` → one message row + one `chat.message` outbox row carrying only `{messageId, storyId, turnId?}` (no body key); `setTurnStatus` → turn updated + one event with the turn id; a throwing transaction leaves neither; the source grep finds no `storyChatMessages` insert or `chatTurns` update outside `chat/messages.ts`.

### 6.3-04 — Chat turn executor: `<key>.chat` worktree, `ask()` for Claude and Codex, context packet (R2, R10) [Codex] · Review: Claude-sec

**Spec:** §5.8.1 (chat worktree, per-runtime argv, post-turn check, timeout/budget), §5.8.2 (chat sessions separate, context packet), §5.2 (stable chat cwd, `-n` naming), §5.4; R2, R3, R4, R16 (context packet redaction, Claude budget on chat turns), S3, S4 (no `Agent` for chat), S5/S6 (hardening on resume), OQ Q2, Q12, Q16.

**Files:** Create `apps/orchestrator/src/chat/{turn,context,sessions,chat-worktree}.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/test/{chat-turn,chat-context,chat-worktree}.test.ts`. Modify `apps/orchestrator/test/runtime-role-matrix.test.ts` (chat columns), `apps/orchestrator/src/agents/runtimes/{claude-code,codex}.ts` (`ask`), `apps/orchestrator/src/agents/runner.ts` (`handleChatJob`; `AgentRunnerDeps.chat: ChatTurnExecutor` — **required**), **`apps/orchestrator/src/app.ts`** (construct `new ChatTurnExecutor({ db, registry, roleRuns, worktreeManager, redact, memoryCheck, … })` once and pass it as `chat` to `new AgentRunner`; `wiring.chat` added to the seam), `apps/orchestrator/src/git/worktree.ts` (`ensureChatWorktree`/`resetChatWorktree`/`removeChatWorktree`), `apps/orchestrator/test/wiring/job-kinds.ts` (`chat` leaves `PENDING`), **`apps/orchestrator/test/app-wiring.test.ts`** (round 6 m4: the chat case below — `chat` ∈ `wiredJobKinds()` and `wiring.chat` identity; owner chain 6.2-05 → 6.3-04 → 6.4-06), **`apps/orchestrator/src/workflow/service.ts`** (round 6 m3: the `cancel_jobs` executor never cancels kind `chat`), `apps/orchestrator/test/workflow-service.test.ts` (new case only), create `apps/orchestrator/test/chat-early-lifecycle.test.ts`, create `apps/orchestrator/test/helpers/chat.ts` (`noChatExecutor`: a stub whose `run()` throws "chat executor not expected in this test"), and the arrange-only constructor line `chat: noChatExecutor` in every existing `new AgentRunner({…})` call in `apps/orchestrator/test` (grep at this commit; at least `{runner,run-interruption,plan-feedback,lane-cap,gate-discovery,gate-startup,runner-dispatch}.test.ts`, `helpers/gate-harness.ts`, `review-window.integration.test.ts` and `gate-pipeline.integration.test.ts` — for the last two this is their one sanctioned edit, ownership map; zero `expect(` lines changed anywhere).

**Chat-safe startup and stops before any chat job can exist (round 6 m3, verified).** 6.3-06 (W27) is the first commit in which the running app can enqueue a `chat` job; until round 6 the startup `fail(interrupted)` skip for `chat` and the `cancel_jobs` exclusion for `chat` landed only in 6.3-13 (W28), so a chat job swept at restart would have **BLOCKED its story** (`app.ts:122-131` fails the story of every interrupted side-effecting job — chat jobs are `sideEffecting: true`, 6.3-03b) and a story Stop/`fail`/T8 would have cancelled queued chat turns' jobs, leaving the turns `queued` forever. Both rules move here, before 6.3-06: (1) **`app.ts` startup loop:** the existing `for (const job of swept.interrupted)` loop `continue`s for `job.kind === 'chat'` (story untouched; the swept job stays `failed` and is not re-run); turning the orphaned `chat_turns` row into `interrupted` with the "send again" system message stays with 6.3-13 — until then such a turn only shows a stale `queued`/`running` state, never a story change; (2) **`workflow/service.ts` `cancel_jobs`:** the executor's job query excludes `kind = 'chat'` for every caller (`stop_run`, `fail`, cancel, T8, `human_changes_requested`); 6.3-13 then adds the CANCELLED → queued turns `failed` "story closed" part on top. 6.2-05's gate skip in the same loop is unchanged.

**Wiring (round-4 MAJOR-B c, verified):** before this round this task added `handleChatJob` to the runner but nothing constructed the executor or passed it in, and `runner.ts:420-421`'s `default:` would have completed every chat job silently — turns would stay `queued` forever. Now the dep is **required** (a construction without it is a type error), `app.ts` builds the executor in the same commit, the dispatch entry exists only through that dep, and `app-wiring.test.ts` (with `chat` out of `PENDING`) fails if `chat` is enqueued anywhere but missing from `wiring.runner.wiredJobKinds()`.

- **Chat worktree (R2):** one read-only detached worktree `<key>.chat` per story (`workspaces` kind `chat`), checked out before each turn at the chat session's SHA — the candidate SHA once one exists, otherwise the base branch for planner chat; `.codex/` deleted after checkout when Q12 requires it. Chat never runs in the story, review or QA worktree.
- **Context packet** (`buildContextPacket(storyId): {text, hash}`): story key, title, description, status + blocked reason, approved (or latest proposed) plan, ACs, latest gate verdicts and findings, diff stat + diff truncated to 40 KB; **redacted before sending**; labelled as untrusted project content. Injected at the start of every new chat session and refreshed when the story's status or SHA changed since the last turn (hash).
- **Sessions:** per `(story, role, SHA | plan version)`; the first turn starts a new **persistent chat** session with the packet; later turns resume it. Chat **never** resumes or forks a role-run session (role runs are non-persistent anyway).
- **Claude `ask`:** cwd = the **stable** `<dataDir>/chat/<chatSessionId>/cwd` (created once, reused by every `--resume` — S3/Q16), `--add-dir <key>.chat`, read-only tool set **without `Agent`**, `--permission-mode dontAsk --permission-prompts none`, `--restricted` when the SHA is a candidate, `--session-id <uuid>` + `-n "aik:<KEY>:<role>"` on the first turn and `--resume <chatSession>` after, `--max-budget-usd` from the role's setting, `--strict-mcp-config` (clean setup).
- **Codex `ask`:** first turn `codex exec - --json <HARDENING> --disable hooks --disable multi_agent -c project_doc_max_bytes=0 -s read-only -C <key>.chat`; later turns `codex exec resume <chatSession> - --json <HARDENING> --disable hooks --disable multi_agent -c project_doc_max_bytes=0 -c sandbox_mode="read-only"` with cwd = `<key>.chat` set by the spawner (or the Q2 fallback); base-branch `AGENTS.md` in the prompt; admitted by `assertCodexAdmission`.
- **Each turn:** memory guard (outside a gate → `queue.defer`; 30 min → turn `failed`); 5-min timeout; after the turn, HEAD unchanged and `git status --porcelain` empty **in `<key>.chat` only** — otherwise delete and re-create `<key>.chat`, mark the turn `failed` with a `system` message, story untouched; agent reply redacted, capped 32 KB, stored as `author:'agent'`; every message and turn transition is written through 6.3-03b's `appendChatMessage`/`setTurnStatus`, so each emits `chat.message` (round-5 m6); `agent_runs` row (`purpose='chat'`, real role name) + `model_usage` row; `chat_sessions.cwd`/`session_ref` stored for purge (6.3-13).

- [ ] **RED** `chat-turn.test.ts` (fake shim: a chat turn is a read-only run without a schema, so it is keyed `ask` on both CLIs — first turn and resume turns alike; arrange `{ prompts: { ask: 'ask-ok' } }`, a two-turn conversation `{ prompts: { ask: ['ask-ok', 'ask-ok'] } }`, and `ask: 'writes-file'` / `ask: 'hang'` for the failure cases — round 6 MAJOR-1): argv for both runtimes match the spec lines exactly (golden), including the full `<HARDENING>` on the Codex resume turn, no `--json-schema`, no `Agent`, Claude `--restricted` at a candidate and not for planner chat on the base branch; the Claude resume turn uses the same cwd as the first turn; `-n "aik:STORY-1:qa"` present; role-run session refs are never passed to `--resume`; fake `writes-file` → `<key>.chat` re-created, turn `failed`, system message, story status/version unchanged, story worktree untouched; low memory → deferred (attempts unchanged).

### 6.3-11 — "Continue previous coder session" per-story option (R2, R10) [Codex] · Review: Claude-sec

**Spec:** §5.8.2 (the only role-run resume), §5.2 (resume argv); T7, OQ Q2.

**Files:** Modify `apps/orchestrator/src/agents/implementer.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/src/agents/runtimes/codex.ts` (use `buildCodexResumeArgs`), `apps/orchestrator/src/agents/runtimes/claude-code.ts` (implementer resume path), `apps/ui/src/components/story/overview-tab.tsx` (toggle), `apps/orchestrator/test/{implementer,claude-implementer}.test.ts`.

When `continue_coder_session` is set and the story has a previous coder session that is **resumable** (not interrupted, not swept) **on the same runtime** as the current implementer route, rework resumes it. Codex: `codex exec resume <id> - --json <HARDENING> -c sandbox_mode="workspace-write"` (+ `-c sandbox_workspace_write.network_access=true` when §6.2 allows it), cwd = the story worktree, no `-s`/`-C`. ### 6.3-13 — Chat lifecycle: light slot cap, `cancel_jobs` exclusion, closed stories, restart, chat stop registry, purge (R10) [Codex] · Review: Claude-sec

**Spec:** §3.1 rule 5, §5.8.1 (`cancel_jobs` exclusion, DONE/CANCELLED handling, re-check before start), §5.13 ("Startup sweep" chat turns, "Job sweep interaction" chat, "Chat and the stop registry"), §5.4 (purge of chat cwds/sessions, chat worktree removal); S12, S14, T2, T8 (chat excluded from `cancel_jobs`), U4, U5.

**Files:** Create `apps/orchestrator/src/chat/{lifecycle,stop-registry}.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/test/chat-lifecycle.test.ts`. Modify `apps/orchestrator/src/agents/runner.ts` (light-slot cap 1, outside the lane cap), `apps/orchestrator/src/workflow/service.ts` (on CANCELLED, queued chat turns → `failed` "story closed"; the `cancel_jobs` exclusion of `chat` itself landed in 6.3-04 — round 6 m3), `apps/orchestrator/src/app.ts` (startup: orphaned `chat_turns` → `interrupted` + system message; the `fail(interrupted)` skip for `chat` landed in 6.3-04), `apps/orchestrator/src/agents/session-purge.ts` (chat cwds + Codex chat sessions), `apps/orchestrator/src/stories/closure.ts` (register the chat closure steps `chat-fail-turns` → `chat-worktree` → `chat-sessions`, using only the deps `StoryClosure` holds since 6.1b-12), `apps/orchestrator/test/wiring/closure-steps.ts` (remove the three chat steps from `PENDING` — the list is then empty), `apps/orchestrator/test/story-closure.test.ts`, `apps/orchestrator/src/resources/memory-guard.ts` (chat 30-min outcome hook), `apps/orchestrator/src/maintenance/artifacts.ts` (6.2-25's project artifact purge also deletes the project's `<dataDir>/chat/<chatSessionId>/` folders, collected from `chat_sessions` before the DB purge). Every chat message and turn change here (story closed, interrupted by restart, memory timeout, handler start CAS) goes through 6.3-03b's `appendChatMessage`/`setTurnStatus`, so each emits `chat.message` (round-5 m6).

- **Slot:** at most **1** running chat turn app-wide (slot `light`), not counted in `maxConcurrentLanes`.
- **`cancel_jobs`** (used by `stop_run`, `fail`, cancel, T8, `human_changes_requested`) **excludes `chat`** (since 6.3-04 — round 6 m3; this task keeps the 6.3-04 test green and builds on it) — queued turns still run on a stopped/failed story. On CANCELLED, `cancel_jobs` marks queued turns `failed` ("story closed"); the DONE cleanup — the 6.1b-12 closure hook, which runs for local merge, GitHub merge and cancel alike (T10 emits no `cancel_jobs`) — marks them `failed` **before** removing `<key>.chat`. The chat handler re-checks that the story is not DONE/CANCELLED before it starts.
- **Handler start check (review minor 7):** the chat job handler claims its turn with a compare-and-set `setTurnStatus(tx, turnId, 'running', { from: 'queued' })` (= `UPDATE chat_turns SET status='running' WHERE id=? AND status='queued'`); if no row changed (the turn was already `failed` by cancel/closure, `interrupted` by restart, or is a duplicate delivery), the job completes immediately — no spawn, no `agent_runs` row, no message.
- **Stop registry:** chat turns never register in `RunInterruptRegistry`; they use `ChatStopRegistry` keyed by `chat_turns.id`; a story Stop or `fail` never interrupts chat; a user "Stop answer" acts only on that turn.
- **Restart:** the existing `fail(interrupted)` loop **skips kind `chat`** (T2; since 6.3-04 — round 6 m3); `chat_turns` in `queued`/`running` → `interrupted` with a system message "interrupted by restart — send again"; the story is untouched; swept chat jobs are not re-run.
- **Memory:** a chat turn deferred for 30 min → turn `failed`, story untouched.
- **Purge (DONE/CANCELLED):** `claude purge <cwd> -y` for each chat session's stable cwd then delete it; `codex delete --force <uuid>` per Codex chat session; remove `<key>.chat`.

- [ ] **RED**: two chat jobs for different stories never run concurrently; a chat job runs while both heavy lanes are busy; `stop_run` with a queued chat turn → the turn still runs; cancel → queued turn `failed` "story closed"; DONE → queued turn `failed` before the worktree is removed, and a handler started after DONE refuses; story Stop during a running chat turn → turn continues; restart with a `running` turn → `interrupted` + system message, story status/version unchanged, no `fail(interrupted)`; 30-min memory deferral → turn `failed`; DONE purge calls `claude purge` on the chat cwd and `codex delete --force` on the chat session, and removes `<key>.chat`; **all three closure paths** (local merge, GitHub merge, cancel — 6.1b-12) fail queued turns, remove `<key>.chat` and purge chat sessions, in that order; **handler start check:** a chat job whose turn is `failed` (closed story), `interrupted` (restart) or already `running` (duplicate delivery) completes without spawning — the fake shim record stays empty and no `agent_runs` row is written (and no `chat.message` event, since nothing changed); "story closed" and "interrupted by restart" each emit one `chat.message` per message and per turn change; project delete removes the project's chat-session folders; `app-wiring.test.ts` → the app's `StoryClosure` has all eight steps in order and `closure-steps.ts` `PENDING` is empty.
- [ ] Run `npx vitest run apps/orchestrator/test/chat-lifecycle.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/chat-early-lifecycle.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/runner.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/session-purge.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/story-closure.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/artifact-retention.test.ts apps/orchestrator/test/app-wiring.test.ts` → FAIL → PASS.
- [ ] Pipeline guard (6.2-24, round-3 MAJOR-A): `npx vitest run apps/orchestrator/test/gate-pipeline.integration.test.ts` → PASS, unchanged (this task edits a file on the gate pipeline path); record it in the pipeline guard log.

## Settled MINOR decisions (builder's calls, taken in this plan)

Withdrawn or replaced by revision 10 are marked; the rest still stand.

- **M1** Minimum versions = the verified 2.1.319 / 0.167.0; a newer version shows a non-blocking "unverified version" note.
- **M2** *(rewritten — R8)* `model_usage` is rebuilt per §5.12 (nullable profile/HTTP/latency, new CLI columns); no synthetic `cli` provider/profile rows; `cost_usd` NULL = "not reported"; `opencode_version` renamed `runtime_version`.
- **M3** Applying a preset writes resolved roles into the project's settings; later preset definition changes never alter a project silently.
- **M4** `timeout` keeps each role's existing failure handling; only the spec's listed classes map to new block reasons.
- **M5** *(withdrawn — §5.4)* Prompt first-line title tag; replaced by `-n "aik:<KEY>:<role>"` for persisted Claude sessions; Codex sessions are identified by stored ids.
- **M6** *(withdrawn — §5.4, S18)* Planner plan worktree; the Claude planner runs read-only in `project.repoPath` with `--setting-sources project`.
- **M7** Open-in-CLI spawns a detached console exe without a shell; only OpenCode runs are offered.
- **M8** Switching back to legacy leaves `allowPaid` as is (it is a consent record, not routing).
- **M9** Settings saves send the full object; last write wins (as today).
- **M10** A per-role model/effort override is used as-is, no escalation.
- **M11** *(rewritten, pending N2)* Until the user answers N2, "Use my personal setup" changes nothing in Codex argv: every `codex exec`/`exec resume` keeps the full `<HARDENING>` set (§5.2 is the more specific security rule); the toggle only relaxes Claude (§5.4) and never removes `--restricted` from Claude gate roles.
- **M12** Codex `-o` file lives in the app run dir, is redacted on read and deleted.
- **M13** Codex crash retry only on a clean worktree; dirty → BLOCKED `provider_down` with a recovery hint.
- **M14** *(narrowed — spec now defines the reasons)* Gate `blocked` → T13 `reviewer_blocked`; errors over the limit → `environment`; the builder's part is only the detail prefix "Code review:" / "QA gate:" / "Security gate:".
- **M15** *(withdrawn — §3.1 rule 1)* Per-preset lane sharing; the spec's global `min(lanes, 2)` cap applies.
- **M16** *(withdrawn — §5.7.1 step 3)* `shell:true` for clean-room installs; installs use `cmd.exe /d /s /c` with the allowlisted env.
- **M17** QA = two Codex invocations (2a, 2b) under one gate row, merged into one `qa_results` row (now also spec §5.7.2).
- **M18** Agent-config edits produce one deterministic **medium** finding listing the files (now spec §5.7.3) — hard-blocking awaits P2.
- **M19** *(now spec R2)* One detached `<key>.chat` worktree per story.
- **M20** Claude subagent cap is advisory (helper prompt + recorded warning) with the parent deadline as the hard bound.
- **M21** Claude effort is not passed unless set per role (CLI default; recorded as `cli-default`) — no silent cost escalation.
- **M22** *(now spec §5.13)* An interrupted chat turn is not re-run; system message "interrupted by restart — send again".
- **M23** Codex probe subcommands (`login status`, `debug models`, `features list`, `delete`) get only `--disable daemon_auto_start` (spec §5.2).
- **M24** The Settings **Test** button writes one `model_usage` row with `agent_run_id NULL` (no `agent_runs` row; `purpose` is `role_run|chat` only).
- **M25** The P6 notice acknowledgement is stored once, app-wide (`app_settings` key `agents.credentialRiskAcceptedAt`, served by new `GET`/`PUT /agent-settings/credential-risk` in `routes/agent-runtimes.ts` — no app-settings route existed) and shown read-only in Settings.
- **M26** UI-QA network consent is a per-project boolean `agents.qa.allowUiNetwork` (default false), set only on the project settings screen.
- **M27** A `gate_completed` that neither finishes nor fails the pipeline is accepted as transition id `GATE` that changes nothing but `statusVersion`.
- **M28** Migration numbering: 0003 runtimes, 0004 gates, 0005 gate backfill (data; file written by 6.2-16, journaled — i.e. made active — by 6.2-22), 0006 chat, 0007 subagents.
- **M29** Until 6.4 lands, every adapter runs with effective `maxSubagents = 0` (no `Agent`, `--disable multi_agent`), whatever the project setting says.
- **M30** `sweepExpired({allLeases:true})` is introduced in 6.1a for all job kinds (safe: only this process holds leases) before gate/chat kinds exist.
- **M31** If the Codex Windows sandbox needs a one-time elevated setup (Q14), the app shows the instruction and blocks Codex roles with `environment`; it never elevates itself.
- **M32** Codex chat turns count toward `cliTokensPerStory` (they spend the same quota).
- **M33** A gate agent that modifies its own worktree (QA: the story/candidate worktree; security/code review: the review worktree) has its result discarded and the gate set to `blocked` → T13 with a "<gate>: modified the … worktree" detail. This is an orchestrator safety outcome, not a model verdict, so it does not conflict with the deterministic security rule.
- **M34** *(new — review F4)* Claude as implementer gets `Read,Grep,Glob,Edit,Write` (+ `Agent` per `maxSubagents`) under `--permission-mode acceptEdits`, **no `Bash`**: the orchestrator's verification runs the checks, and an unsandboxed shell would bypass the §6.2 coder network rule that the Codex sandbox enforces. Exact tool names confirmed by the 6.1a-T0 "Claude write roles" probe.
- **M35** *(new — review F1)* The closure hook removes the story worktree on CANCELLED as well as DONE (spec §5.7.1 "the story's other worktrees on DONE/CANCELLED"); removal is never forced — a dirty worktree is kept with a warning — and the story branch is never deleted. A `cleanup` job enqueued in the T10/T17 transaction is the durable trigger; the local merger also runs the hook inline to keep its existing `warning` result.
- **M36** *(new — review minor 2)* The Settings **Test** probe runs in a fresh OS-temp dir that is `git init`-ed (one empty commit) and deleted afterwards, instead of allowing `--skip-git-repo-check` for one probe: the forbidden-flag list stays absolute (one golden-argv rule, no exception path to review), and `git init` is local, free and fast.
- **M37** *(new — review F2)* The gate engine lands behind a temporary `gateEngine` switch (default off) plus a machine compatibility rule for `gates === null`; both are removed by 6.2-22. Chosen over one big integration commit for 6.2-03…6.2-09 so each Claude-sec review stays one task wide. *(Extended — review round 2, MAJOR-1)* While the switch is off the reviewer keeps its legacy path (6.2-09) and migration 0005 stays un-journaled (6.2-16), so the running app never stalls in READY_FOR_REVIEW; 6.2-22 deletes the legacy path and journals 0005 in the same commit that turns the engine on.
- **M38** *(new — review F4)* Claude as QA uses `Read,Grep,Glob,Edit,Write,Bash` with an explicit `--allowedTools Bash` allow rule under `acceptEdits` (never a bypass mode), scratch cwd + `--add-dir <qa worktree>`, non-persistent; `--restricted` only if the 6.1a-T0 probe shows Bash survives it, otherwise `--setting-sources project` on the empty scratch cwd. Its commands run unsandboxed (same exposure as project checks, covered by the P6 notice); Settings says so when QA is routed to Claude.
- **M39** *(new — review F4)* Codex as planner/reviewer/security with `model: auto` uses the router's **QA** table row (cheapest adequate tier for a read-only role); the reason string says so. An explicit slug overrides as usual.
- **M40** *(new — review r3 MAJOR-A)* Gate-agent wiring is its own task (6.2-24) rather than an extension of 6.2-22: 6.2-22 is already the one-wave serial point with the machine, snapshot, reviewer, migration journal and test passes; the wiring is a separate Claude-sec review of `app.ts`/`runner.ts` and gets its own commit and guard test. Agents are built by one factory (`agents/team.ts`) that the app and the integration test share; routes are resolved per claim, not at construction.
- **M41** *(new — review r3 minors 3, 7)* Tests inject the runner's memory check (`plentyOfMemory`) instead of setting `AIK_MIN_FREE_MEM_MB=0` (an env override leaks across files in one Vitest worker and still reads the real `os.freemem()`); the temporary `apply-pending-migration.ts` helper is duplicated in the orchestrator test helpers instead of exporting a test-utils entry from `@aik/db` (no public surface added for a helper that lives for one task).
- **M42** *(new — review r4 MAJOR-B)* The composition-root guard inspects the **real** `startOrchestrator()` result through a read-only `wiring` seam instead of extracting a separate composition function or relying on source-text guards: it tests exactly what runs, and the seam adds no behaviour. Enqueued job kinds are found by a source scan that refuses non-literal `kind:` values, because a runtime trace would miss kinds the test never triggers. Expectation lists live in one small file per concern (`test/wiring/{job-kinds,runtimes,agents}.ts`) so parallel tasks (6.1b-04 and 6.1b-12 in W8) extend the guard without sharing a file. the `closure` runner dep (for `cleanup`) and the `qa`/`security` slots stay optional (absent → `unwired_job_kind` or the 6.2-05 no-agent path, both visible, and `app-wiring.test.ts` requires them in the app) while `chat` is required, as the review asked; the `merge` no-merger branch is kept, now logged, so existing runner tests keep their behaviour.

- **M43** *(new — review r5 MAJOR-3)* Run logs and QA evidence are deleted by age (`RetentionService.prune`, default 30 days; evidence only once its story is DONE/CANCELLED or gone) and immediately on project purge — **not** by the story-closure hook: a merged story's Logs and QA-evidence tabs and its stored acknowledgement must stay viewable after the merge. No scheduler is added; the existing maintenance-route prune is the trigger.
- **M44** *(new — review r5 self-check)* The run-log path is stored on `agent_runs.log_path` (plan addition to migration 0003) instead of giving `StoryService` a data-directory dep: the transcript reader (6.1a-14) then needs no `app.ts` change in W5, where `app.ts` belongs to 6.1a-12.
- **M45** *(new — review r5 m3)* `binaryPath`/`binaryPrefixArgs` are added to `RuntimeStatusDTO` by 6.1a-09, which creates the DTO and resolves the binary, rather than by 6.1b-07/6.1a-15 (the review's suggestion): 6.1a-15 consumes them and 6.1a-20's e2e asserts them, both before 6.1b-07 lands. The DTO exposes a local path to the renderer; it carries no secret, and the main process re-validates it before spawning.
- **M46** *(new — review r5 MAJOR-2)* The Settings **Test** probe is user-initiated and app-wide, not a project role run: it writes no `agent_runs` row and is not gated by a project's `allowPaid` (the button itself states that it uses quota).

## Spec errata noted (no user decision needed)

- §6.1 timeout table ("Other bound" = "token watchdog" for coder and QA) and §7 item 2 ("token watchdog") predate R6. The plan follows R6/§6.1 text: role timeout + `commandsPerRun` + between-run `cliTokensPerStory` (6.1b-05). Fix the two leftovers when the spec is next edited.
- §5.4 "the toggle removes the restrictions" vs §5.2 "`<HARDENING>` on every `codex exec`/`exec resume`": the plan applies §5.2 until N2 is answered (M11).
- §5.1 types `RoleRunInput.outputSchema?: JsonSchema`. The plan (round 7 m7) uses `{ name: 'PlanV1'|'ReviewV1'|'QaV1'|'SecurityV1'; schema: JsonSchema }` so each adapter can set the CLI schema's `title` from the contract name (6.1a-08, 6.1a-10, 6.1b-04). It narrows the type and changes no behaviour. Update §5.1 the next time the spec is edited.
- §5.7.2 says screenshots "are deleted with the story's evidence by the existing retention job". At `9df7973` there is no retention **job**: `RetentionService.prune()` runs only through the maintenance route (`api/routes/maintenance.ts:11`), the `retention` job kind is never enqueued, and the prune knew nothing about evidence. 6.2-25 implements the deletion inside that prune (and on project purge); reword to "by the retention prune" when the spec is next edited.

## Designer questions still open

**Dropped as answered by the spec (rev 3–10):** old U5 UI-QA network → §5.7.2 (explicit per-project consent; default `unclear`) and M26; old U8 API-key login → §6.2 / Q8 (not supported, never passed); old plan-mode fallback (Q5) → R14 (dropped); `-p aik` profile → §5.2 (`--ignore-user-config`); live chat injection / Codex queue → A2 (§5.8.1); token watchdog → R6 (§6.1); "Send to coder" from READY_FOR_REVIEW → R15 (`human_changes_requested`, §5.8.4); gate storage/freeze → §5.7, §5.7.0, §5.12; QA `unclear` → B5 (§5.7.4); chat lanes → §3.1 rule 5; chat worktree → R2; session persistence → R4; credential-read refusal → T4 (now P6). The earlier draft-level questions listed in the rev-2 plan (C Q-1…Q-21, X Q1…Q12) were all either answered by spec rev 2 or settled as MINOR decisions above.

**Remaining USER questions** (spec §8 P1–P6, then two new ones and the spec §9 confirmation). Each has a default the plan implements, so none blocks execution:

1. **P1 — In-product Codex review of Claude's PlanV1 before the user approves it?** Default: not built (§4.1 is a build-process decision only). A "yes" adds a 6.3-level task.
2. **P2 — Hard-block coder edits to `.claude/`, `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`/`AGENTS.override.md`, `.mcp.json`, `.codex/` (enforce protected paths in the workflow), or only flag them via the security gate?** Default: flag only (M18, 6.2-11). Related: `protectedPaths` (incl. `.github/workflows/**`) is computed but never enforced today (`machine.ts:125`) — enforcing it would change behaviour for every project.
3. **P3 — Is losing mid-run chat steering (A2) acceptable, or wanted in a later phase?** Default: acceptable; chat is read-only Q&A, steering via stage-gated buttons and Stop.
4. **P4 — Chat for roles that run on OpenCode.** Default: unavailable, header says "Chat needs Claude or Codex for this role" (6.1a-08, 6.3-03a, 6.3-09). Alternative: build OpenCode read-only Q&A after verifying its permission/resume API.
5. **P5 — Planning failures in NEW.** Default: stay NEW with `planning_failed` + reason; auth/quota/budget not auto-retried; new `retry_planning` event and "Retry planning" button (6.1a-12, 6.1a-18, 6.3-08). Alternative: extend the machine so NEW can go to BLOCKED.
6. **P6 — Credential exposure to agent-written code.** Default **(a)**: accepted residual risk, shown once when the agent-team preset is enabled and recorded in Settings (6.1a-16); network-enabled Codex runs proceed. **(b)**: refuse network-enabled Codex runs if Q15 shows unconfined reads **and** run checks, installs and the dev server under `codex sandbox`, with a per-project "I accept the risk" override — this would add three tasks (sandboxed check runner, sandboxed install/dev server, override setting). Q15's result (6.1b-T0) is evidence for this decision.
7. **N1 (new) — Confirm the initial Codex tier mapping.** §5.6 leaves the `fast`/`default`/`strongest` slugs to 6.1b. The builder proposes slugs from the free `codex debug models` output; the user confirms before 6.1b-02 lands, because it decides quality and quota use.
8. **N2 (new) — What exactly does "Use my personal Claude/Codex setup" remove for Codex?** §5.4 says the toggle "removes the restrictions" except `--restricted` on Claude gate roles, but does not say whether Codex QA/candidate chat keep `--disable hooks`, `project_doc_max_bytes=0` and web search off, or whether `--disable daemon_auto_start` (needed for tree-kill, §5.13) may be dropped. Plan default M11: until answered, the toggle changes **nothing** in Codex argv (full `<HARDENING>` everywhere, per §5.2); it affects Claude only. Options: keep that; or let the coder (never QA or candidate chat) drop user-config/rules/feature flags while keeping `--disable daemon_auto_start`.
9. **Confirm spec §9 "Calls I made".** The spec lists 18 builder calls for the user to check (e.g. clean setup by default, 2-lane cap for agent-team projects, critical/high-only security blocking, JS-only QA installs, no "send unredacted", purge on DONE/CANCELLED). The plan implements all 18 as written (traceability above); a single "confirmed" or a list of exceptions is enough.

- `login status` (+ `--disable daemon_auto_start`): exit 0; **status line goes to stderr, not stdout** — one line `Logged in using ChatGPT`. Empty with `HTTPS_PROXY=http://127.0.0.1:9` (local/credential-cache check passes). Detectors (6.1b-01) must parse stderr.

Entry fields: `slug, display_name, description, default_reasoning_level, supported_reasoning_levels[{effort,description}], visibility, supported_in_api, priority, additional_speed_tiers, service_tiers, available_access_programs, upgrade, context_window, max_context_window, multi_agent_reasoning_effort, input_modalities, base_instructions, …`. All models: text+image input, empty `additional_speed_tiers`/`service_tiers`, `upgrade: null`. Hidden / not in API: `gpt-6-chat-thinking` (prio 3), `gpt-reserve` (prio 6), `codex-auto-review` (prio 11). `multi_agent_reasoning_effort: xhigh` only on sol-6.1, terra-6.1, astra-6. Proposed tiers for N1 confirmation: fast `gpt-6-luna`, default `gpt-6.1-sol`, strongest `gpt-6-astra`; placement of `gpt-6.1-terra` left to the user.

Every `<HARDENING>` `--disable` name exists: `daemon_auto_start` (stable, default **false**), `apps`, `plugins`, `remote_plugin`, `browser_use`, `browser_use_external`, `browser_use_full_cdp_access`, `computer_use`, `in_app_browser`, `image_generation`, `goals`, `tool_suggest`, `hooks` (all stable, default true), **`multi_agent`** (stable, default true). ### Delegation policy (updated 2026-11-07, user decision)

- Worker/implementation sub-agents: **GLM models only** (starting with batch 2 of W0). The five batch-1 tasks already running on Claude finish as started.
- Codex stays reserved for implementation until its credits return; `[Codex]` tags are kept so the intended split is restored afterwards.
- Claude is used **only** in the project-manager / BA role: planning, questions, clarifications, and BA/security review of implemented tasks (the plan's "Review: Claude-sec" and independent reviews stay on Claude as the BA function).
- Reason for the batch-1 exception: verified 2026-11-07 that GLM sub-agents run in the same shell-less harness as the coordinator (`spawn sh ENOENT` — file read/edit only, no command execution), and the Codex provider has no credits. Adopted worker split until a shell is available to GLM or Codex credits return: **GLM authors all code and tests (file work only); Claude runs the checks (targeted vitest, typecheck, lint), lands commits, and performs BA/security review.** Batch 2 (6.1a-04, 6.1a-05, 6.1a-13) starts under this split.
- **GLM directory-creation gap:** `write_file` cannot create directories, so a GLM author cannot start a file in a new folder. Workarounds used so far: 6.1a-04 created `src/agents/runtimes/` via a temporary T3 project registration (deleted immediately afterwards, verified clean); 6.1a-05 part A had the three fixture dirs created by a single trivial `cmd /c mkdir` delegation. Standing rule from now on: at each wave start, a Claude child pre-creates every new directory the wave's tasks will own, so GLM authors never need workarounds.
- **2026-11-07 observation, needs user confirmation:** the trivial Codex delegation for the `mkdir` **succeeded**, although the working assumption was that the Codex workspace has no credits. If the user's Codex credits have returned, Codex can resume its intended executor role per the plan's executor note (the `[Codex]` tags were kept for exactly this); until the user confirms, Claude continues to stand in.

### PM/BA decisions (2026-11-07, round 1)

- **Worker split ratified** with required controls: the check-runner proves RED itself (sources moved aside → new tests must fail for the expected reason; 2–3 planted bugs must each turn a named test red — a test that stays green under its planted bug rejects the task); the check-runner never edits product/test code (recorded `eslint --fix` only) and returns failures to the author word for word; Review: Claude-sec tasks get a separate reviewer agent that sees the staged diff + plan/spec sections but NOT the author's summary; staging by explicit path only, `git diff --cached --name-only` must equal the task's Files list + approved amendments; PID-set diff of `claude`/`codex`/`opencode` around every run (diff by PID — two Codex app-server daemons pre-exist); reviewer checklist for author mistakes (exact `toEqual` assertions, no `as any`/`@ts-ignore`/`eslint-disable`, no invented imports/deps, nothing labelled `recorded` without a capture, no real home paths/emails, no `spikes/` imports).
- **Amendment 6.1a-05a:** add `apps/orchestrator/test/fixtures/agent-cli/** -text` to `.gitattributes` (repo has `core.autocrlf=true`; without it scenario `.jsonl` endings are rewritten on checkout, breaking manifest sha256s and the CRLF-replay test).
- **Amendment 6.1a-05b:** `fake-runtime.ts` moves from 6.1a-05 to **6.1a-08** (the `AgentRuntime` interface does not exist until then).
- **N1 Codex tiers (standing default, user-overridable):** fast `gpt-6-luna` (efforts low, medium) · default `gpt-6.1-sol` (medium, high) · strongest `gpt-6-astra` (high, xhigh). Condition on strongest: a fresh free `codex debug models` must position astra above 6.1-sol in its descriptions, else strongest maps to `gpt-6.1-sol` too (escalation then raises effort only). `gpt-6.1-terra` is not routed (explicit per-role override only). Escalation chain: fast+low → fast+medium → default+medium → default+high → strongest+high → strongest+xhigh (cap). Missing slug falls one tier down; unsupported effort clamps down, never up; fast missing → block `provider_down`. Tier file header: `schemaVersion: 1`, `codexCliVersion: "0.167.0"`. `max`/`ultra` never chosen automatically.
- **N1 condition resolved (2026-11-07, fresh free `codex debug models`):** astra = "Frontier intelligence for the most demanding work" (priority 4, default effort low, supports xhigh/ultra) vs 6.1-sol = "Latest workhorse model for coding and everyday work" (priority 1) → astra is positioned as the frontier/strongest model; **strongest stays `gpt-6-astra`** (high, xhigh). Terra stays unrouted.
- **Check-runner round 1 (6.1a-13):** 35/35 green, all four planted bugs caught, scope/hash/PID/clean-content PASS; one no-skips violation (`it.skipIf` at open-cli.test.ts:131) returned to the GLM author for fix round 1. Protocol refinements adopted: RED-by-reversal moves the task's SOURCE files only (moving the tests too yields "no test files found", not a meaningful RED); for routes whose auth lives in the global hook, planted-bug (auth) targets `apps/orchestrator/src/api/server.ts` (sanctioned temporary edit, hash-restored).
- **Security review round 1 (6.1a-13): CHANGES REQUIRED, 0 blocking, 3 MAJOR.** MAJOR-1: spawn errors arriving as an async 'error' event were uncaught (uncaught exception in Electron main after the handler returned) → fix: widen `OpenCliSpawn` with `once('error')`, attach before `unref()`, async-error test. MAJOR-2: the IPC handler's real trust check was dead code (`trusted: true` hardcoded) and allowed-roots were fetched before payload validation → fix: pass the real trust result, fetch roots lazily, handler-level test (untrusted → zero fetches/spawns). MAJOR-3 **deferred to 6.1a-17 by plan decision:** `detached:true` + `stdio:'ignore'` may give OpenCode no visible usable console on Windows (DETACHED_PROCESS + NUL stdio); 6.1a-17 adds a live manual console check, and if it fails the fix is a no-shell console opener (e.g. `wt.exe` fixed argv or CREATE_NEW_CONSOLE via a helper) with spec sign-off — `cmd /c start` stays forbidden. Minors (trailing-separator root, win32-only case folding, `\\?\` cwd strip, `..foo` rejection, statSync isFile + `.exe` for the exe resolver, exact-assertion upgrades, `\\?\`-inside-root case, flow-level missing-cwd test, wrong-token 401 code assertion) returned with the same fix round. Accepted residual risks: TOCTOU window between realpath and spawn (requires local write access to the data folder); `AIK_OPENCODE_EXE`/managed path trusted as main-process config.
- **Security review round 1 (6.1a-04): CHANGES REQUIRED — 1 BLOCKING, 1 MAJOR + minors, all returned as fix round 2.** B1 (probe-confirmed): an EMPTY escaped value (`\"\"`) let the non-greedy R12 match spill into the next key, persisting a real refresh token in clear; fix = backreference-tied escape depth (`/((\\*)"(?:access_token|refresh_token|id_token)\2"\s*:\s*\2")((?:(?!\2")[^"])+)(\2")/g`) so a pair either matches coherently or not at all. M1: `redactValue` was key-blind, so credentials in real JSON fields (`tokens.access_token`, `Authorization` headers) persisted in clear → key-aware whole-string redaction with a credential-key list (now incl. camelCase `accessToken|refreshToken|idToken`, which also makes the `.credentials.json` comment true). Minors: overflow diagnostic must reserve its own bytes (exact ≤ maxBytes), short-write loop + truncate-mark on write error, JSON-value typing for `appendFrame` (Buffer/BigInt), fd leak on `fstatSync` throw, `runLogPath` device-name/trailing-dot rejection + `createRunLog` path assertion, incremental newline scan (O(n²) fix), and a documented contract that `onMalformed` content is unredacted until it passes `appendDiagnostic` (6.1a-06 depends on it). Accepted residuals: a secret split across two separate FRAMES is persisted as two halves (6.1a-06 must reassemble or not use partial deltas); credentials under key names with no rule stay unredacted.
- **Security review round 2 (6.1a-04): CHANGES REQUIRED — converging.** All round-1 findings confirmed resolved, nothing leaks. NEW MAJOR (M2, regression from the round-1 path fix): `RUN_ID_PATTERN` demanded ≥2 chars, so runs 1–9 of every fresh install would throw — exact replacement regex supplied by the reviewer. Minors m1 (credential-key list missed Proxy-Authorization/prefixed forms — widened regex supplied), m2 (non-string values under credential keys — whole-value replacement before recursion), m3 (partial-write newline integrity + `n<=0` loop guard; the short-write test uses the `vi.spyOn(fs,'writeSync')` seam the round-1 author wrongly said didn't exist). Edge case (escaped quote inside a token value truncates redaction at that quote, every depth) ruled ACCEPTABLE RESIDUAL — documented at the rule, hardened regex intentionally NOT shipped this round. ESCALATION NOTE: this is the third failed verification round on the task; one final converging GLM fix round was granted because the reviewer confirmed approval-readiness and supplied every fix verbatim — a further failure escalates to the user.
- **Task 6.1a-13 COMPLETE — commit `ac8a513`** (`fix(desktop): open-in-CLI launches validated argv without a shell (§6.3)`, 9 files, +1056/−45, not pushed). Pipeline: GLM author → check-runner (35/35, 4 planted bugs bite; 1 no-skips finding) → fix r1 → re-check PASS → security review r1 CHANGES REQUIRED (uncaught async spawn errors; dead trust flag + eager roots; MAJOR-3 console visibility DEFERRED to 6.1a-17) → fix r2 → re-check#2 FAIL on 2 test-hygiene items (spy fixture did not record the 'error' event; forbidden `import()` type annotation) → coordinator applied both verbatim, disclosed → re-check#3 READY (44/44, 5 plants bite incl. a bonus second-layer lazy-roots plant) → security review r2 **APPROVED**. Round-2 OPTIONAL minors assigned to **6.1a-15** (next owner of open-cli.ts): (1) resolve the exe lazily so an untrusted sender causes zero `statSync` calls; (2) `on('error', …)` instead of `once` (a second error event would be uncaught); (3) add a `process.nextTick`-timing error test case (real Node delivery) alongside the synchronous-fire case. Accepted residuals: orchestrator fully trusted for cwd + repo-path roots (approved design); a launch can report ok:true without a visible console until the 6.1a-17 live check.
- **Task 6.1a-04 COMPLETE — commit `07da770`** (`feat(orchestrator): UTF-8 safe JSONL decoder, redacted run log, credential-token redaction (TEAM-2/3, §5.3, §6.2)`, 6 files, +863, not pushed). Pipeline: GLM author → check-runner (greedy R12 leak + impossible test + lint) → fix r1 → re-check (direct IN/OUT probes, 4 planted bugs incl. the historical leak shape) → security r1 CHANGES REQUIRED (BLOCKING empty-escaped-value leak → backreference-tied depth regex; key-blind redaction → key-aware whole-value; 8 minors) → fix r2 → re-check#2 (direct probes + 5 plants) → security r2 CHANGES REQUIRED (M2 run-id regression breaking runs 1–9 + m1/m2/m3, exact fixes supplied; third failed round — final GLM round granted, escalation documented) → fix r3 → re-check#3 FAIL (short-write mock defect only; coordinator fixed, disclosed) → re-check#4 READY (119/119, broken-loop plant now detected) → security r3 **APPROVED**. Follow-up minors (fold into the next transcript.ts owner, 6.1a-11): comment overstating prefixed-camelCase key coverage (transcript.ts:59-61); boolean values under flag-ish keys redacted failsafe (transcript.ts:65). Accepted residuals: escaped-quote truncation inside token values documented at redactor.ts:29; prefixed camelCase keys and unknown key names rely on the pattern redactor; run-log readers must skip unparseable lines.
- **Check-runner protocol adopted** (10 steps, evidence pasted raw): scope (`git status --porcelain`, staged set == Files list); RED-by-reversal with `Get-FileHash` restore proof; GREEN via `--reporter=json --outputFile.json` (success:true, failed/pending/todo all 0, every task test file in testResults; minimum passing counts: 6.1a-04 → cli-jsonl 4, cli-transcript 4, redactor +2 · 6.1a-05 → fake-cli 21 · 6.1a-13 → open-cli 10, open-cli-target 4); planted-bug rounds with revert hashes; grep proving no skip/only/todo and counting `as any`/`@ts-ignore`/`eslint-disable`; existing-test diff must have no removed lines; PID sets before/after; typecheck+lint exit codes (zero diagnostics in task files, nothing new vs baseline); clean-content grep (emails, `C:\Users\USER`, `spikes/`) + `package.json`/`package-lock.json` untouched; commit only after review PASS, then `git show --stat` recorded.
- **Escalation rule:** a GLM task failing evidence/review goes back to GLM with the raw failures; Claude never authors fixes (recorded formatting only); after three failed rounds on one task, escalate to the user.
